## 1. VERDICT — Condition A MET on the letter by one study only (Roman et al. 2021, re-confirmed after two more adjudication panels); no segmentation-series twin exists in 1,359 logged queries; Condition B: a design now PASSES the adversarial checklist (PACC, 17/17/16, item 7 unanimous, the first in five rounds) but is UNCERTIFIED on the data and its own pilot shows the map series cannot carry the trend, so B is met on the checklist's letter and not on the contract's purpose and is not claimed; the negative is documented at Condition-T depth.

Plain-language answer. Five rounds plus a dedicated shadow/variance sub-hunt (nine contract angles; sixteen, twelve and twelve modality sweeps; Google Scholar phrase queries; Internet Archive Scholar full-text queries; Japanese, Dutch, Flemish, German and French grey literature; preprint servers; citation graphs) found no study that does what ours does: map canopy wall-to-wall from a multi-vendor, uncalibrated, sub-meter aerial archive over five or more epochs, measure its own inter-date inconsistency, and validate change against a design that can see change. Four studies have the exact shape and all stop at 3/5 for the same reason: Charlotte 2008–2022, Montréal 2011–2023, Blackman & Yuan's 1938–2017 St. Peter series (now including the 131-page thesis behind it, read in full and adjudicated 3/5 unanimously) and Cai et al.'s 2013–2021 Orlando series produce multi-epoch wall-to-wall canopy maps from heterogeneous aerial archives, and none quantifies flicker or spurious change and none validates the change it reports. Three more sit next to that shape: Richardson & Moskal's Seattle synthesis, Zhou et al.'s Gwynns Falls series, and Nix et al.'s Philadelphia parks study (1959–2018, four epochs, interpreter-derived detectable-change thresholds of 6.1/2.1/4.7 pp), which a three-adjudicator panel scored 3/5 unanimously because it has four mapped epochs and its second-interpreter plots are spent on the consistency criterion and cannot also count as change validation. Roman et al. 2021 (Philadelphia 1970–2010) remains the one study adjudicated 4/5: five epochs from two uncalibrated aerial programs, a second-interpreter detectable-change threshold of 3.2 pp, paired-point McNemar change; it misses only segmentation, so it is the published precedent for our arbiter (F8), not for our map series. Pawlak et al. 2026 (California NAIP 2016–2022, U-Net, Olofsson-adjusted) stays the closest published analogue of the automated half, adjudicated 3/5.

Condition B moved and the movement is the most consequential result of the night. Ten designs were written across rounds 2 and 5 and the sub-hunt, each judged by three independent panels against the nine-item checklist. One passes: PACC, Panel-Anchored Cross-Date Certification, 17/17/16 with item 7 (the kill test can fail on cross-date bias) unanimous. Its designer ran a read-only pilot on the real data and the pilot produced the number that matters for the paper: the map series, used as a model-assisted auxiliary to the paired human panel, shrinks the panel's confidence interval by about 4 % (β̂ 0.15–0.26; ±1.89 → ±1.81 pp), and the β = 1 difference estimator is worse than the panel alone (±3.68 pp). That is round 2's kill test K0 failing. The same pilot found that the anchor-based bias correction swings 2.2 pp across the contamination term's own confidence interval against a panel half-width of 1.10 pp (KT-2 clause c fires), and that the arbiter's cross-date bias on 1,045 human-certified stable points is −2.32 ± 3.62 pp, a confidence interval spanning zero and the whole effect (KT-3 undetermined). So the verdict on B is one compound sentence and every appearance of it below keeps both halves: PACC passes the adversarial checklist; it is uncertified on the data; its decisive measurement is that the map series adds about 4 % precision to the panel and cannot carry the trend. The round-5 critic's word for the pass is "nominal", and it belongs next to the judges' PASS. A parallel session on this repository reached the same conclusion independently at 10:31 today (commit dbf630a, `Reports/CHANGE_DETECTOR_DESIGN_2026-09-06.md`: "Panel A stays the pace authority; this detector owns WHERE only").

The shadow question the user added has a literature answer and a repository answer. Literature: shadow is a real, large, separately detectable source of spurious change in multi-date VHR aerial imagery (Storey et al. 2017 classify transient shadow at 93.3 % overall accuracy without a DSM; Gorte et al. 2014 model it from sun position), but no published study attributes a measured flicker rate to shadow against edge, operating-point and delivery confounds, and no published detector is both variance-aware and shadow-aware. Repository: no shadow model exists, flight times are not in the catalog ("only dates", IMAGERY_FACTS.md:810), sun geometry has been measured for about 5 of 37 deliveries, and the raw shadow-dating evidence is archived out of the tree. Three shadow/variance designs were judged and all are CONDITIONAL; the settling experiment that costs nothing new is a per-crown disagreement-anisotropy test between the afternoon 2021 delivery and the morning 2016/2020 deliveries, with the same-flight 2019 pair as the isotropic control. That is a recommendation, not evidence.

## 2. CONDITION A — the study that clears, the closest segmentation analogue, and the two adjudicated splits

Scoring rules given to every adjudicator, so the scores are reproducible: (i) C4 is satisfied by any across-date consistency number produced by the study's own design, including an interpreter-derived detectable-change threshold, but not by per-date accuracy alone; (ii) C5 is satisfied by a validation design that re-observes the same reference units at each date and tallies change per unit, even if the published estimator ignores the pairing; (iii) one state-agreement design cannot be counted for both C4 and C5; (iv) C2 requires at least five mapped epochs spanning at least ten years; (v) C1 requires printed evidence of more than one acquisition program or vendor and the absence of any radiometric cross-calibration step (per-date classifier training is fine). Rule (i) decided Roman's C4; rule (ii) decided Pawlak's C5; rule (iii) decided Nix's C5 in the final panel. Dispute on record: one round-2 skeptic refuted Roman's C1 on the ground that PASDA and DVRPC are repositories rather than vendors; all three adjudicators upheld C1 on Table 1's printed heterogeneity (61/61/61/46/9.7 cm; grayscale and colour; three of five epochs unrectified; no radiometric step).

### 2a. Roman L.A., Fristensky J.P., Lundgren R.E., Cerwinka C.E., Lubar J.E. (2021). "Linking Urban Tree Cover Change and Local History in a Post-Industrial City." Land 10(4):403. doi:10.3390/land10040403. Read in full (agent, 31 pp), pp.5–8 re-read by the orchestrator; adjudicated 4/5 by three independent panels (C1 Y, C2 Y, C3 N, C4 Y, C5 Y).
- Archive: "Aerial photos used to assess land cover. Images were obtained from the Pennsylvania Spatial Data Clearinghouse (PASDA) and the Delaware Valley Regional Planning Commission (DVRPC)" (Table 1, p.6): 1970 colour "partially on", 1980/1990/2000 grayscale leaf-off, 2010 colour leaf-off; pixel sizes 61, 61, 61, 46, 9.7 cm; "2000 and 2010 — the only years that were orthorectified" (p.6). No radiometric step anywhere.
- Method: 10,000 random points, single trained interpreter following Nowak & Greenfield, 8,691 usable after excluding permanent water and points uninterpretable in any year (p.5–6). Change: McNemar on the paired points (p.7). Uncertainty: "a random 5% subsample of points to a second, independent interpreter … 91.5% agreement … The two interpreters' UTC estimates were close: the average difference across all years was 1.3 percentage points" → detectable-change threshold "any 40-year UTC changes larger than 3.2 percentage points are considered detectable change" (Eq. 1, p.7–8); 2010 interpretation vs 2008 lidar 93.4 % agreement (p.7).
- Result: citywide UTC +4.3 pp 1970–2010, "both statistically significant and beyond our detectable change threshold"; 65.0 % of 1970 tree points stable through all five images (p.8).
- What it is for us: the peer-reviewed warrant for F8 (fixed-point multi-epoch paired interpretation of a heterogeneous archive with an interpreter-derived floor).
- F1–F8 scorecard for Roman 2021 (no map series, so most rows are "not testable" and are stated as such):
  - F1 (map sawtooth, sign opposite to the human panel): not testable, no map series. Its point series is the kind of series F1 says a map cannot reproduce.
  - F2 (fraction-grid harmonization null): not testable.
  - F3 (operating point dominates the signal): not testable for maps; the human analogue is measured: two interpreters' UTC estimates differed by 1.3 pp on average across years (p.7–8).
  - F4 (radiometric drift within a sensor family): not testable; the archive spans grayscale film to 9.7 cm colour and the only per-epoch quality signal printed is the share of uninterpretable points, 2.7 % (1970), 6.0 % (1980), 2.7 % (1990), lower later (p.6).
  - F5 (flicker census; smoothing pilot): not testable; the nearest figure is point persistence, 65.0 % of 1970 tree points stable through all five images (p.8), a persistence share, not a flicker census.
  - F6 (edge-concentrated loss, 2.0 m median): partially testable. Parallax and misregistration are handled per Nowak & Greenfield and points uninterpretable in any year are excluded (8,691 of 10,000 usable, Table 2 p.6–7); no edge analysis is reported.
  - F7 (same-flight floor): analogue only. Second-interpreter mean difference 1.3 pp per year, detectable-change threshold 3.2 pp per 40 years (Eq. 1, p.7–8), against our 1.3 pp delivered-cut and 0.15 pp matched-cut same-window floors.
  - F8 (paired human panel as arbiter): direct precedent. 8,691 paired points at five epochs with McNemar; interpreter error from a 5 % second-interpreter subsample (about 500 points, 91.5 % agreement) against our 2/28 duplicate disagreements on 1,250 points; their threshold 3.2 pp/40 yr against our ±1.10 pp/8 yr; their external check is 2010 interpretation vs 2008 lidar (93.4 %), ours is the leaf-on third-epoch verify pass and four QC controls.

### 2b. Pawlak C.C., Yost J., Ventura J., Guizan G., Arnold S., Okin G.S., Cavanaugh K., Fricker G.A., Ritter M.K., Gillespie T.W. (2026). "Mapping California's Urban Forest at Scale: An Error-Adjusted Canopy Time Series for Monitoring Change." bioRxiv preprint doi:10.64898/2026.05.04.722774 (7 May 2026; no journal version found). Read in full twice; adjudicated 3/5 unanimously (C1 Y, C2 N, C3 Y, C4 ?, C5 Y).
Method and numbers (page-cited; preprint line numbers in parentheses): NAIP 60 cm RGB+NIR, 2016/2018/2020/2022, 39,032 km² (p.4 ll.76–78); "collected and processed by multiple contractors and then assembled into a single statewide product" (p.26 ll.528–531); bands min-max scaled with fixed 2020 bounds (p.8–9 ll.180–183); U-Net/ResNet-34 with a single visually chosen threshold 0.65 for all years (p.9 ll.197–200); 1,700 points re-interpreted once per year (p.11–12 ll.239–246); Olofsson-adjusted areas 24.89/24.16/18.32/21.58 %, canopy PA .640/.662/.823/.690 (Table 3 p.17); Sen's slope −0.60 %/yr, CI [−2.76, +1.38] (Table 4 p.19); the 2018→2020 drop "may reflect a combination of real canopy loss and view angle acquisition variability" (p.22 ll.444–445); NAIP canopy products "should not be used for pixel-to-pixel change comparisons" (p.27 ll.545–546).
F1–F8 scorecard: F1 reproduced in kind (a 6.6-pp drop and 3.3-pp rebound between biennial NAIP years that survives error adjustment); F2 not tested; F3 consistent and unaddressed (one threshold for all years; UA .94–.98 with PA .64–.82 is a conservative cut whose recall floats by acquisition); F4 consistent, unmeasured (only a global affine normalization); F5 consistent in conclusion, no census; F6 consistent (crown footprints shift with view angle); F7 not tested; F8 same authority, weaker design (unpaired per-year estimates, single labeller, no controls; level CIs ±1.5 pp imply ≈±2.1 pp on any two-year difference, against our paired ±1.10 pp on eight years).

### 2c. The two round-5 splits, settled by a formal three-adjudicator panel (workflow wf_dcdf2d63-f11, six Opus agents, each reading the complete text; adjudication_nix_blackman.json)
- Nix S., Roman L.A., Healy M., Rogan J., Pearsall H. (2021/2023). "Linking tree cover change to historical management practices in urban parks." Research Square preprint posted 30 Dec 2021 (Clark Digital Commons copy, 2022; Philadelphia; 26 pp read by all three). 3/5 unanimous (3/3/3): C1 Y (PASDA + DVRPC; City of Philadelphia 2018 colour 0.076 m; DVRPC 1959/1980/2000 grayscale 0.61/0.61/0.46 m; preprocessing purely geometric, p.6); C2 N ("aerial photography captured in 1959, 1980, 2000, and 2018", p.6: four mapped epochs); C3 Y ("One interpreter delineated all UTC patches to maintain consistency", p.8; Table 3 complete per park per year); C4 Y ("The detectable change thresholds for the entire 59-year period were 6.1, 2.1 and 4.7 percentage points", p.8); C5 N (the second-interpreter 3-ha plots, 87.4–96.2 % agreement, are the input to the C4 threshold and cannot be counted twice; the text never states the same plots were re-delineated at every date). The first reader's 4/5 rested on C5, which two of the three round-5 skeptics had carried forward untested. It is the third precedent from the same group, after Roman 2021 and Healy 2022, for interpreter-derived detectable-change thresholds on a heterogeneous aerial archive, and belongs in the F8 citation list.
- Blackman R. (2019). "Long-term urban forest cover change detection with object based image analysis and random point based assessment." MSc thesis, Minnesota State University Mankato (131 pp, read in full by all three). 3/5 unanimous (3/3/3): C1 Y ("different histogram stretches, learning options, and input representations" per image type, p.58: per-date, no cross-date step); C2 Y (1938/1951/1964/1995/2008/2017, p.47); C3 Y (city-boundary OBIA maps every epoch, p.53/58); C4 N (only "likely … within error margins", p.74; no number); C5 N (i-Tree 1,000 points re-interpreted at two dates only, 2008 Google Earth and 2019 Google Maps, aggregate proportions with binomial SE, no per-point change tally, p.59 and Table 22). The thesis therefore closes the question the 2020 paper left open: there is no consistency analysis the paper omitted.

## 3. CONDITION B — the design that passes the checklist, why it is not certified, what its pilot measured, and the shadow/variance grafts

What was tried. Ten designs, thirty judge verdicts, all out of 18 on the nine-item checklist (items 1–6 from the contract; 7 "the kill test can fail on cross-date bias", 8 "preserves the dispersed 2 m edge loss", 9 "cited precedents" added by the panels):
- Round 2: TDI 18/16/17; G-COPT 16/17/15; PAT 15/16/14; CE-BSI 16/15/13. All failed item 7: within-date replicate pairs cannot certify removal of cross-date bias.
- Round 5: PACC 17/17/16, item 7 unanimous, PASS; Change Census 17/17/15, item 7 unanimous, PASS on the letter with a circular kill test; E2-MRF 13/13/13, FAIL (item 7 split; calibration domain excludes every date's edge ring, which is 35.7 % of the city and 65.8 % of ever-canopy).
- Variance-aware sub-hunt: SHADE 16/16/15 (item 8 2/3); RC-LoD 16/17/16 (item 8 0/3); GPA-WEDGE 16/15/17 (item 8 2/3). All CONDITIONAL.

### 3a. PACC — Panel-Anchored Cross-Date Certification (designer's one-line thesis: "the paired human panel is the estimator, not the validator")
1. Invariant reference frame. Anchor lattice on the 2 m analysis grid: A_can = lidar canopy in both 2005 and 2016 (24.90 % of the city; ≥4 m interior core 5.04 %, 310,294 cells), A_bare = lidar non-canopy in both epochs and impervious (eroded, 5.52 %, 340,001 cells). Nothing is resampled; nearest-neighbour warps onto the grid.
2. Distance stratification, the load-bearing move: bands 0–2, 2–4, 4–8, 8–16, >16 m from the lidar canopy boundary inside each anchor. Nuth & Kääb's stable-terrain bias as a function of covariates, not a scalar.
3. Per-delivery response profile R_d = P(map = canopy | band) at the deployed cut, for each of the 37 deliveries on its own footprint. No DN and no radiometric quantity crosses a delivery boundary, which is the escape F4 requires.
4. In-bracket invariance fit on the five epochs whose stability is lidar-certified at both ends (2009–2016), then extrapolation, with a contamination term ℓ_t (share of anchor cells that really changed after 2016) estimated from a disjoint simple random sample of anchor cells (Card 1982 form R_obs = (1 − ℓ)R_true + ℓ·R_bare).
5. Estimation: the fixed paired human panel at every epoch is the estimator; the map enters only as a locator for stratification and as a GREG auxiliary with an estimated coefficient allowed to collapse to zero (McRoberts 2014; Sannier et al. 2014; Stehman 2013; Olofsson 2014).
6. Certification, pre-registered with a commit hash before labelling: (a) |Δ_map,corrected − Δ_panel| > 1.96·SE_panel on ≥2 of 3 intervals, evaluated at the measured interpreter error; (b) the domain statistic B = Σ_h W_h [P(map_t2 = canopy) − P(map_t1 = canopy)]_h on human-certified stable points exceeds 1.0 pp with a CI excluding zero, or McNemar χ² > 3.84 on the discordant pairs. If either fires the map is demoted to locator-only and the panel carries the estimate alone. A fourth test, a flagged-loss review lane with a commission threshold (Pierce/WDFW HRCD precedent), is specified and was not run.
Scaling (measured rates, not assumed): 1.94 s per paired label (1,393 Panel A labels, phase4/qc/panel_a_labels.csv), elapsed/worked 2.16; 77.9 GPU-min per delivery (Scripts/run_registry.csv). Certification to ±1.0 pp needs 4,657 stable points ≈ 2.5 h of labelling; ±1.5 pp needs 2,070 ≈ 1.1 h. No training, no new inference.

Pilot numbers (single run by one agent on 2026-09-06 against HEAD 9f59073 and `D:\edmonds-pipeline\trend8_stack_2m.npz`, which reproduces trend8_harmonized_fractions.csv exactly; scripts listed in the header; re-run before any number is cited):
- Edge is where the bias lives: P(map = canopy) in the 0–2 m lidar-stable band swings 13.2 pp across epochs (0.6485 in 2015 to 0.7802 in 2011s; 2016 .7543, 2024 .6955) on a population lidar certifies unchanged, while the >16 m core reads 1.0000 in seven of eight epochs. Permanent non-canopy within 2 m of canopy is called canopy 16–26 % of the time (.1620–.2569).
- KT-1 in-bracket invariance: citywide fraction raw sd 1.704 pp → anchor-regression residual 0.454 pp, leave-one-out RMSE 0.841 pp, coefficient +0.324 stable. PASSES as a screen; it cannot fail on post-2016 bias.
- KT-2 contamination: ℓ_2024 = 8.18 % ± 2.17 (from 159 incidental Panel A anchor points); the corrected 2016→2024 estimate runs −1.57 pp (no contamination) / −2.96 (ℓ = 8.18 %) / −2.21 to −3.79 across ℓ's CI, against raw matched-cut −3.48 and Panel A −2.21 [−3.31, −1.11]. Clause (c) FIRES: a 2.2 pp swing against a 1.10 pp panel half-width. The anchor correction as a standalone number is killed at the present sample size.
- KT-3 retrospective, on Panel A's 1,045 human-certified no-change points: cand_change stratum +0.78 pp, stable_canopy −7.02 pp, stable_other −2.56 pp; area-weighted B = −2.32 pp, 95 % CI ±3.62, McNemar b = 50 / c = 37, χ² 1.66. UNDETERMINED: the CI spans zero and the whole effect. Bias-removed map net would be −1.16 pp.
- Model-assisted: Panel-only −2.93 ± 1.89 pp (three-stratum partition, n = 1,098 matched); GREG with β̂ (pixel .149, 10 m .231, 22 m .258, 50 m .102; correlations .20–.49) −2.80 to −2.90 ± 1.81; β = 1 difference estimator −2.09 ± 3.68. The map is worth about 4 % CI shrinkage as an auxiliary; forcing β = 1 is worse than ignoring the map.
- Loss geometry at matched cuts 2016→2024: 31,455 loss components, gross loss 9.10 pp, gross gain 5.62 pp, net −3.48 pp; MMU 20/52/100/200/500 m² retains 8.62/7.74/6.62/4.99/2.67 pp.

Judges' must-fixes (conditions, not suggestions): (1) the ℓ_t term is estimated from the same human instrument that serves as arbiter (ℓ_2024 from 159 Panel A points; R_bare = 21.4 % rests on 3 of 14 verified loss points); the SRS must be disjoint from and labelled before the certification panel, and the instrument response should first be identified from the same-flight pairs where subject change is identically zero (traveling-subject precedent: Dewey 2019, Zuo 2021, Zhan 2023). (2) The arbiter's own error is measured and never propagated: Panel A's blind-duplicate gate failed at differential error 0.036 against a 0.005 gate (phase4/qc/panel_a_estimate.txt, 2/28) and the blur control is 0/30 (rule-of-three upper bound 10 %); points wrongly certified stable carry real change the map often detects, pushing B negative by roughly 0.5–2 pp, comparable to the effect; closed form B_true = (B_obs − ε·d̄)/(1 − ε) with ε from a properly sized duplicate sample (~2,550 per panel) and a second reader. (3) Blind and randomize the interpretation: strip date labels and resolution cues, randomize left/right epoch order, resample both chips of a pair to the coarser delivery's GSD for the change call, native resolution only in a confirmatory pass.

### 3b. Change Census (the review lane; HRCD-style exhaustive polygon verification with a routed, never deleted, sub-MMU stratum), 17/17/15
Measured on the stack: the persistence-before gate retains only 25/42 panel-verified losses exact-cell (30/42 at 3×3) and is dead as a filter; MMU-as-delete drops polygons whose median distance to the 2016 canopy boundary is 2.00 m while kept polygons sit at 2.83–4.00 m (MMU 52–200 m²), so it preferentially deletes F6 and is admitted only as a stratum boundary; raw mask differencing captures 88.4 % of lidar-certified 2005→2016 loss area at MMU 4 m² and 81.6/65.7/50.2 % at 28/100/200 m², failing the 90 % gate, so a trained change classifier is required; raw differencing flags 7,574 of 7,860 tiles (96 %). Its strongest cross-date test is operating-point invariance: review the union of delivered-cut and matched-cut candidates once, blind, and kill if the two generators' verified estimates differ by more than about 1.5 pp (they embody a 9.8 pp raw difference). Its defects: kill test 3 is circular as written (the classifier is trained on the lidar-certified cells it is then scored against; fix by training on Panel A labels only or spatial block cross-validation), the census stratum is declared error-free while its control gates inherit a value the repository records as failing, and one judge's realistic cost is 11 worked / 25 elapsed human-days for two legs (6 s per tile times 7,860 tiles is an irreducible 13 h per leg), which is outside "a few human-days" as parametrized. Independent corroboration arrived today: the parallel session measured the shipped persist rule `persist = loss & ~c21` as a pure recall tax (weighted precision −0.010, P = .426) that deletes all 13 terminal 2021→2024 verified losses.

### 3c. The shadow / variance grafts and the settling experiment
- SHADE (shadow-covariate latent-state HMM on the existing probability rasters; discriminability d′ per shadow × edge × GSD cell; k-of-N confirmation from Rui 2021): 16/16/15. Fatal per judges: calibrated only on extreme surfaces (≥5 m persistent crowns and inert built ground), blind to sub-5 m canopy where the 2017 quartet's 3.55 pp residual sits; run-length confirmation censors the terminal step; the GSD bin collapses to one value per date (96 populated cells, not 288). The parallel session's finding that HMM per-year corrections correlate 0.858 with median-3's is a further warning: an HMM core inherits the smoothing trap unless it only weights evidence.
- RC-LoD (replicate-calibrated per-pixel level of detection with shadow as a variance covariate; Wheaton 2010 / Hugonnet 2021 form): 16/17/16 but item 8 false 0/3: σ is monotone in an endogenous edge distance, so the level of detection sits above the amplitude of the 2 m edge loss this archive contains; also standardizes before differencing (fix: difference the recentered margins first, then divide by √(σ₁² + σ₂²)).
- GPA-WEDGE (object-level sequential test with delivery-learned heteroscedastic noise; glaucoma Guided Progression Analysis template, Rui 2021, Artes 2014): 16/15/17. Fatal per judges: its shadow kill test K0 nominated of_2017/of_2017k as a different-sun pair, but imagery_pixelsize_and_date.csv records them as the same orthomosaic (identity measured 2026-08-23, r median 0.9923), so the shadow arm was unfalsifiable; and its per-date bias b_t is a scalar on core objects where shadow bias does not live. Repairs: use the archive's only measured sun-hemisphere flip, 2016 (Aug 12 morning, SUNEL 34–37, shadow azimuth 283–295°) and 2020 (azimuth 120.8–192.5°) against 2021 (afternoon, sun at 247–256°), and fit b_t per depth band and bearing (Nuth & Kääb's full aspect-dependent form).
- Critic's synthesis: no published method is both variance-aware and shadow-aware. Shadow-aware multi-date VHR change detection exists and is variance-blind (Storey 2017: I_ratio ≤ 0.90 and B/I_ratio ≥ 1.10, OA 93.3 % vs Otsu 84.7 %; Gorte 2014 sun-position Z-buffer before differencing). Variance-aware detection exists and is shadow-blind (Jiang 2023 unchanged-forest difference envelope; Chugg 2021 Δ-logL, AUC 0.86; Nguyen 2018 Neyman–Pearson statistical detection of change; Rui 2021 and Artes 2014 heteroscedastic test-retest quantiles with k-of-N confirmation). Unswept as primaries: IR-MAD (Nielsen 2007; Canty & Nielsen 2008), CCDC/COLD (Zhu & Woodcock 2014; Zhu 2020), and the archived `Scripts/qc/imagery_date_evidence/` (47 files, the only raw per-frame azimuth/elevation measurements).
- Settling experiment (recommendation; existing files; no shadow raster): per-crown disagreement anisotropy. For each 2020-crown, compare the sun-ward and anti-sun-ward half disagreement rates between the afternoon 2021 mask and the morning 2016/2020 masks. Only shadow predicts that the asymmetry axis rotates with azimuth; GSD and season move magnitude, not direction. Control: the same-flight 2019s/2019n pair (disagreement 6.54 %, IoU 0.7376) shares sun geometry and must be isotropic; a radial-from-frame-centre residual is lean/relief (the ~5 m east-side ortho-vs-CHM offset), not sun. Both isotropic means shadow is not driving the 47.83 % ever-canopy flicker and every shadow arm is decoration. Before it, the cheapest shadow-geometry step is to measure azimuth per delivery with the validated shadow-azimuth instrument (2012 control 8/10 in-window, P = 6.9e-5) and invert azimuth + date window to time of day, never shadow length.

### 3d. Why B is not claimed, and what would make it so
B passes the checklist's letter (PACC 17/17/16, item 7 unanimous, precedents on disk for every load-bearing component) and fails the contract's purpose: the map series does not carry the trend in the one measurement designed to let it (GREG gain ≈ 4 %), the standalone anchor correction is killed at the present sample size, and the certification statistic is undetermined with an unpropagated arbiter error of the same order as the effect. The design is nevertheless implementable in a day of compute and about three hours of blinded labelling, and its kill test can fail on the failure mode that matters. The single experiment that closes it, recommended by the round-5 critic and by two PACC judges independently: a pre-registered, date-blinded, order-randomized re-interpretation of a Neyman-sized stable stratum (N ≈ 2,070 for ±1.5 pp, 4,657 for ±1.0 pp), with same-window replicate deliveries seeded as true nulls and the fail threshold fixed in advance. It supplies PACC's missing term, de-circularizes the Census's kill test 3, and tests E2-MRF's edge claim if the anchors include 0–2 m cells. The parallel session's fit-free step-shape certifier (Olofsson-weighted precision 0.249 → 0.431, empirical null from the same-flight twin and hard-negative parcels, tiered output with REVIEW-TERMINAL for the 31 % of loss that eight epochs cannot corroborate) is the natural locator for PACC's review lane and is, by its authors' own note, unreproducible from repo plus lake until it is ported as an instrument.

## 5. FIVE CLOSEST MISSES (all read in full; one sentence each on the failing criterion)
1. Pawlak et al. 2026 (bioRxiv, California NAIP 2016–2022, U-Net, Olofsson-adjusted) — 3/5: four epochs over six years, and its only inconsistency evidence is an 11-crown parallax experiment the authors say is not a quantification of bias.
2. Charlotte–Mecklenburg canopy series 2008/2012/2016/2018/2022 (UVM SAL 2014 report; PlanIT Geo 2023 report) — 3/5: five epochs from three vendors at 1 m and 60 cm with no calibration, but no inconsistency metric and only a single-date 2022 accuracy; the 2012 report itself says cross-study differences "likely result from the quality of the mapping as much as changes in the landscape" (p.3).
3. Communauté métropolitaine de Montréal, Indice de canopée 2011/2015/2017/2019/2021/2023 (méthodologie 2017/2024) — 3/5: wall-to-wall 1 m NDVI-plus-height classification with the NDVI threshold re-tuned by eye each year ("ajusté à 0,4 pour l'année 2023 pour tenir compte de l'ombrage", p.2), no accuracy or consistency number anywhere.
4. Blackman & Yuan 2020, Remote Sensing 12:1820, with Blackman's 2019 thesis (St. Peter, Minnesota, 1938/1951/1964/1995/2008/2017 from city, state-archive and NAIP imagery) — 3/5 unanimous in two panels: per-epoch OBIA with independent histogram stretches and raw post-classification differencing; only per-date accuracies (89–96 %), "likely … within error margins" instead of a consistency number, and a two-date i-Tree point re-interpretation that does not tally change.
5. Cai, Hu & Parajuli 2025, Scientific Data 12:713 (Orlando biennial NAIP 2013–2021) — 3/5: five epochs but eight years, and validation on ~3,600 polygons of land "unchanged from 2013–2021".
Also read and close: Nix et al. 2021/2023 (Philadelphia parks, 3/5 unanimous, detectable-change thresholds 6.1/2.1/4.7 pp; fails C2 and C5); Healy 2022 dissertation (same 4-epoch Chelsea/Holyoke series, thresholds 1.07/4.76 pp; fails C2 only); Carmel et al. 2001 (location plus classification error propagation in multi-temporal databases, PCC 0.58–0.74 combined; a method paper, 3/5 → 2); Atlanta 2018–2023 (GT CURA 2023; "approximately 500 acres or 1.25 % of the city's canopy" is false or temporary regrowth misclassified as gain; 3/5 → 2); Atlanta 2008–2018 (2/5); Zhu, Shi & Peng 2020 (geo-eco zoning removes 3,786 of 6,390 changed patches as spurious; Landsat scale); Pontius & Lippitt 2006 (error can explain observed map differences at 85 % user's accuracy; conditional, no data); Gong et al. 2017 HMM (18.91 % of pixels keep their label 2007–2015 before correction); Walton, Nowak & Greenfield 2008; Moskal, Styers & Halabisky 2011; Berlin Vegetationshöhen 2020 (~11 % omitted vegetation area in the 2009/2010 product); Bodembedekkingskaart Vlaanderen 2015 (CI-based detectable-change threshold between epochs; blames a 6.0 → 4.4 % shift on a classifier rule change); De Wit 2022 (Detroit 2014–2018 GEOBIA change validated against demolitions: 42 % detection, 62.7 % commission; two epochs); Ellerman 2025; Bonney 2022; Richardson & Moskal 2014 (3/5); Zhou et al. 2011; Landry et al. Tampa 1973–2021; Liu, Kelly & Gong 2006 (1/5; spatio-temporal MRF that "screen[s] out isolated change pixels"); Storey et al. 2017; Gorte et al. 2014; Rui et al. 2021; Artes et al. 2014; Nguyen et al. 2018; Brown 2004; Montesano et al. 2017; Marty et al. 2025; Nguyen et al. 2024 (temporal prior would erase edge loss). Unreadable near-misses (metadata only), candidates for the user's library route: Kropp 2024; Bonney & He 2019 LUP; Tong & Zhang 2026; Duncan & Boruff 2023; Stehman 2013; Stehman 2022; Xing & Stehman 2024; Sales 2022; Béland 2025/26; Fensham & Fairfax 2007; Foody 2010; Congalton 1991; van Oort 2007; Olofsson et al. 2013; Khorram 1999; Burnicki et al. temporal dependence simulation; Shen et al. 2024 (aleatoric uncertainty for change detection); Lopatin 2019; Hoberg et al. 2015; Moser & Serpico 2011; Melgani & Serpico 2003; Solberg et al. 1996; the two Japanese vegetation-survey method papers; the Davey Watsonville 2022 report.

## 6. REASONS TO DOUBT §1 (evidence only, from the repository's own files, read-only)
1. F1's sign-opposition is superseded by the repository's own later verdict, and four numbers in the on-disk truth document are contradicted by HEAD artifacts. The truth document's file time is 2026-09-05 22:02 at HEAD c0c1d83. Commits a9dd52c ("trend8 matched-cut series (sweep-derived): sign FIXED, jitter remains", 2026-09-06 00:44), e0cad3c ("EagleView triple complete: endpoints agree with Panel A (−0.94pp/4yr)", 02:13), 447dc28 ("CAMPAIGN COMPLETE: trend8 + overlap_floor verdicts; recut artifacts", 06:40) and dbf630a ("Reports: change-detector design + adversarial review", 10:31) post-date it. `Scripts/experiments/trend8_uniform_rgb.yaml` verdict: "DELIVERED-CUT SERIES RETRACTED as a trend read … At policy-C matched cuts … SIGN TEST vs Panel A: PASSES — 2016->2024 = −5.59pp sweep-footprint / −3.48pp city-clipped masks (delivered cuts had said +4.2, wrong sign) … 41/42 of Panel A verified losses corroborated … ±2–4pp inter-year jitter persists … pixel flicker did NOT collapse". The four numbers: (a) F1 sign, as above; (b) F3 "collapse to 1.07 pp" (truth doc l.29) against `phase4/qc/overlap_factorial_read.csv` "quartet spread pp 3.55 vs 10.1 delivered-cut spread"; (c) F5 "50.7 % of ever-canopy" (l.41) against the yaml's "47.8 % vs 50.7 %" at matched cuts; (d) F6 "largest persistent cluster 0.41 ha" (l.45) against `phase4/qc/trend8_hotspots.csv` cluster 2512 at 1.53 ha, the persist-rule output, which the parallel session found 0 % certified because its trajectory (0.75 → 0.07 → 1.00 → 0.11) contains a physically impossible resurrection; the two definitions may differ and the pair needs reconciling, not overwriting. The paper's F1 framing has to move from "sign-opposite" to "matched-cut maps overshoot 1.6–2.5× and single-year steps are unreadable".
2. The repository's own data corroborate F4 and F6 directly. PACC's pilot measured the 0–2 m lidar-stable edge band at .6485–.7802 across epochs (13.2 pp swing) while the >16 m core holds 1.0000: the inter-date inconsistency is an edge phenomenon on a population lidar certifies unchanged, which is what F4 (drift within a sensor family) and F6 (edge-concentrated loss at 2.0 m median) jointly predict. Supporting: `phase4/qc/eagleview_sign_test.csv`, 2020→2024 −0.94 pp vs Panel A's linear expectation −1.1 pp, "SIGN+PACE AGREE at endpoints; interior +1.9/−2.8 wobble NOT monotone".
3. F7's "noise floor no algorithm downstream of delivery can remove" is overstated. `overlap_factorial_read.csv`: "floor |CoE−King| same-window pp 0.15 PASS (≤1.3)"; "quartet spread pp 3.55 vs 10.1 delivered-cut spread"; season effect −1.73 pp; the 1 m arm carries the residual. The 1.3 pp figure is a delivered-cut number.
4. F2 ("inconsistency is baked in at detection time") is broader than its evidence and in tension with F3: only fraction-grid harmonization was shown null; reference-matched cuts are post-hoc and they work; the 2 m probability-raster stack adds +0.006 AUC with a CI spanning zero (parallel session), so the matched cut already extracted the information. Narrow F2 to "grid harmonization is null".
5. F5's smoothing claim had no record when the hunt began and acquired a measured reproduction, but still no instrument, at 10:31 today. The only "smoothing" in the repository tree is checkpoint-selection smoothing (`--select-smooth` in `Scripts/pipeline/phase4seg/cli.py`, `config.py`, `core.py`, `select.py`; the 2026-08-29 queue_smooth_2009.yaml launches are that training run); `phase4_qc_flicker.py` is a stable-parcel test (Cemetery 7.07 pp std, Stadium 4.52 pp, Parking/Water ≈ 0), and neither experiment yaml mentions a temporal pilot. The parallel session's report then states "the smoothing trap has no fact home in the repo" and reproduces it fresh from the cached stack: median-3 turns −3.49 raw into −0.59 against −2.21 ± 1.10 human truth, per-year correction correlating −0.999 with deviation from the neighbour mean, endpoints never cancel, and the shipped persistence rule deletes all 13 terminal losses. So F5's conclusion is now supported and its provenance is not: per the repository's own measurement contract it needs an instrument and a gated CSV before the paper cites it. The exclusion of the whole temporal-prior family (Nguyen 2024, SHADE, E2-MRF) should cite that reproduction, not a pilot that was never recorded.
6. Citation gaps (not doubts): F3's 10.1 pp / near-binary / DN≈5–9 pp, F6's 2.8 m baseline, the F4 AUROC 0.86–0.93 range and F5's crown-level 12.4:1 ratio are written only in the truth document (`qc_indep_2017.txt`, `qc_indep_report.csv`, `panel_a_paired_change.py:457`, CHATLOG:525 corroborate the components). "41/42 corroborated" is a 10 m-halo convention (pixel-exact 36/42, parallel session) and should never be printed without it; the gold counts are 1,169 no-change / 42 loss / 2 gain, not 1,155 / 7.
7. F8's interpreter-error figure is thin and now quantified as comparable to the effect: `panel_a_estimate.txt` records duplicate disagreement 2/28 (differential error ≈ 0.036 against a stated ≤ 0.005 gate) and a 0/30 blur control (rule-of-three upper bound 10 %); PACC's judges bound the resulting spurious negative bias in any stable-point statistic at roughly 0.5–2 pp. Roman 2021 used a 5 % second-interpreter subsample of 10,000 points; Nix 2023 re-delineated ~10 % of each park; Xing et al. 2021 report interpreter CVs up to 97 % for rare change classes. The pairing (F8) is what makes −2.21 ± 1.10 reachable; Pawlak's unpaired design cannot resolve a trend of this size, and the paper should say so and should size a second-reader sample (~2,550 duplicates per panel) before the next label campaign.
8. Scope: the parallel session's report sets "Atlas scope 2016→2024, hard", because no validated null exists before 2016 and a backward HMM fabricates a 15-year trend (−2.13 raw → −4.86). PACC's in-bracket fit (2009–2016) is lidar-certified at both ends and is the only pre-2016 statement this hunt found defensible; anything else pre-2016 in §1 should be labelled as unvalidated.

Footnote (outside §6): the Literature_Tracker ledger lists Healy 2022 under the wrong journal (Environmental Management) and attributes Kropp et al. 2024 to Berland.

## 5. Full-read notes (one block per paper read in full; page-cited)

### R1. Pawlak C.C., Yost J., Ventura J., Guizan G., Arnold S., Okin G., Cavanaugh K., Fricker G.A., Ritter M., Gillespie T.W. (2026). "Mapping California's Urban Forest at Scale: An Error-Adjusted Canopy Time Series for Monitoring Change." bioRxiv preprint, doi:10.64898/2026.05.04.722774, posted 2026-05-07, NOT peer-reviewed, CC-BY. 53 pp (text pp.1–45; 46–53 blank/figures). READ-FULL by agent (4.1/4.5) AND by orchestrator (all pages via Read). Copy: bioRxiv PDF → `txt/pawlak2026_california_utf.txt`.
- Imagery: NAIP 60 cm, 4-band RGB+NIR, years 2016/2018/2020/2022, all California census-designated places (39,032 km²) (p.4 ll.76–78; p.6 ll.125–126). "NAIP mosaics vary in orthorectification quality and off-nadir viewing angle both spatially across California and temporally across acquisition years because imagery is collected and processed by multiple contractors and then assembled into a single statewide product" (p.26 ll.528–531). Radiometry: no cross-calibration; bands min-max scaled with FIXED bounds precomputed from 2020 NAIP and applied to all years (p.8–9 ll.180–183).
- Method: U-Net ResNet-34; labels = 3DEP lidar CHM ≥1.8 m ∧ NDVI>0.05 (30,458 tiles) then fine-tuned on 645 manual 2020 tiles; single fixed threshold 0.65 for all years "selected based on visual inspection" (p.9 ll.197–200). Model trained on 2020 only, applied to 2016/2018/2022 (p.5 ll.110–112).
- Reference/validation: 1,200 urban + 500 non-urban points (50/50 mapped canopy/non-canopy), each point human-labelled ONCE PER YEAR against that year's NAIP (4,800 validations), NA if uncertain (p.11–12 ll.239–246); Olofsson 2014 stratified error-adjusted area per year, 95% CI; change = Wald Z on difference of adjusted areas; Sen's slope over 4 error-adjusted points with CI from pairwise-slope percentiles (p.13–14 ll.287–296). No duplicate/blind interpreter controls; no paired change response design; single labeller per point-year (p.11–12).
- Numbers (Table 3, p.17): error-adjusted canopy % 24.89 (2016) / 24.16 (2018) / 18.32 (2020) / 21.58 (2022), CIs ±~1.5 pp; UA(canopy) .956/.978/.940/.980; PA(canopy) .640/.662/.823/.690; OA .903/.915/.958/.930. Sen's slope statewide −0.60 %/yr, CI [−2.76, 1.38] (Table 4, p.19) — "not statistically distinguishable from no change" (p.18 ll.353–355). 2018→2020 significant declines in three zones (−6.4 to −8.6 pp), partial rebound 2020→2022 (+7.8 Inland Empire) (p.18 ll.363–369). Authors: decline "may reflect a combination of real canopy loss and view angle acquisition variability" (p.22 ll.444–445). CAL FIRE product re-adjusted with their sample: mapped 18.85%/18.78% → adjusted 28.15%/23.98% (p.25 ll.495–499); Chesapeake change accuracy 75% (2013/14) and 56% (2021/22) cited from Bouffard 2025 (p.25 ll.503–506).
- Inconsistency quantification: (i) per-year PA swing .64–.82 with model fixed (Table 3); (ii) 11 hand-selected conical crowns digitized in all 4 years: "between year percent area differences that are larger than botanically possible from true tree growth over two-year intervals. While this experiment is not intended to quantify statewide bias…" (p.27 ll.548–556; Fig. 8 p.28). No pixel flicker census. Conclusion: "canopy products produced from NAIP imagery should not be used for pixel-to-pixel change comparisons" (p.27 ll.545–546); "NAIP-derived canopy time series contain an inherent error that is unaccounted for in estimates" (p.27 ll.559–560).
- A-SCORE (orchestrator): C1 Y (aerial 60 cm; multi-contractor; uncalibrated — but ONE program, 4-band), C2 N (4 epochs / 6 yr), C3 Y, C4 Y-weak (quantified at tree level + per-year PA, not a map-consistency metric), C5 Y (per-year re-interpreted points → adjusted-area differences with CIs; not stable-only; but not paired and no interpreter-error measurement) → 4/5 (3/5 under a strict C4).
- B-relevance: none new — it IS the Olofsson error-adjustment already used in our Panel A; shows that with 4 epochs and ~1,700 unpaired points/yr the method yields ±1.5 pp CIs and cannot resolve a ≤1 pp/yr trend.

### R2. Cai D., Hu L., Parajuli J. (2025). "Biennial sub-meter tree coverage dataset of Orlando (2013–2021)." Scientific Data 12:713, doi:10.1038/s41597-025-05059-9, CC-BY. 14 pp. READ-FULL by agent AND orchestrator. `txt/cai2025_orlando_biennial.txt`.
- Imagery: NAIP 1 m (2013–2017) and 0.6 m (2019–2021), 4-band; sensors ADS40-SH51/SH91 (2013), ADS-80SH81 (2015), ADS-100 (2015–2021) with different band passes; "Such variations cause inconsistencies in spectral and radiometric responses" (p.2); Fig. 5 caption: "there is a clear calibration inconsistency present among five surveyed years" (p.8).
- Method: rule-based hierarchical thresholds (NDVI/NDWI → brightness/texture), thresholds set INDEPENDENTLY PER YEAR by "hybrid" auto+manual tuning, using "invariant reference features (e.g., permanent water bodies, well-established urban areas) as anchors to ensure consistency" (p.3; Table 2 p.5). 2017 NIR saturation fixed by a multi-year mask: M_corrected = M_NDVI AND (M2013 OR M2015 OR M2017) (p.5) — i.e., stability inheritance from other years for non-vegetation.
- Inconsistency metric: 2017 Stage-I non-veg mask overlapped only 34.97/43.57/39.01/41.6 % with 2013/2015/2019/2021 masks vs >65% for any other pair (p.4–5).
- Trend (Table 3 p.8): metro 41.1 (2013) / 34.6 / 35.9 / 36.4 / 38.2 % (2021); city 39.5/35.2/35.9/35.9/37.8. No CIs, no change validation.
- Validation: "~3,600 polygons across static land area (unchanged from 2013–2021)" interpreted per year (p.5); tree-canopy OA 78.3/82.3/83.7/87.8/88.8 % (Table 5 p.9). Product "designed for tract or neighborhood-level applications, rather than individual tree canopy analysis" (p.9).
- A-SCORE: C1 Y (multi-sensor NAIP, 1 m is borderline sub-meter), C2 N (5 epochs but 8 yr), C3 Y, C4 Y, C5 N (stable-area-only validation; no CI) → 3/5.
- F-relevance: per-year manual threshold re-tuning against stable anchors = a hand version of F3's reference-matched cuts; the 2013→2015 −6.5 pp then rebound is an F1-type sawtooth across a sensor change.

### R3. Nguyen T.-A., Rußwurm M., Lenczner G., Tuia D. (2024). "Multi-temporal forest monitoring in the Swiss Alps with knowledge-guided deep learning." Remote Sensing of Environment 305:114109. READ-FULL by agent 4.2 (16/16 pp, WUR repository copy) — orchestrator has NOT yet re-read; see `angle_4_2.md` §3 for page cites. Agent extraction: swisstopo aerial 1946–2020, single agency, multiple camera generations, no radiometric calibration (p.2–3); 12–20 epochs per tile, span 74 yr (p.3); U-Net + irregular ConvGRU with temporal contour-alignment (tCA) loss, beats naive temporal MSE smoothing (Table 5 p.12); inconsistency shown only qualitatively (Fig. 13 p.12) and authors say quantitative evaluation of multi-temporal segmentation "for measuring subtle changes" is open (p.12); validation = per-random-date spatial accuracy on 200 tiles, no change validation. A-SCORE (agent): C1 N (single agency), C2 Y, C3 Y, C4 N, C5 N → 2/5. B-candidate: needs no calibration, no lidar, light (~19M params, 1 GPU); gaps: no operating-point handling; temporal prior may suppress edge/dispersed change (F6 risk) — kill test = apply to our 8 maps vs Panel A −2.21.

## 6. Closest misses (running top-5 with the one-sentence failure reason)
- [R2, exact shape] Charlotte–Mecklenburg tree-canopy series — American Forests 2008 layer; UVM SAL 2012 (1 m aerial + lidar OBIA); PlanIT Geo 2016; PlanIT Geo/EarthDefine 2018 (reprocessed) and 2022 (NAIP 60 cm ML) — read in full (PlanIT Geo Nov-2023 report 42 pp; UVM 2014 report 14 pp; `r2_read_1.md`): C1 Y, C2 Y (5 epochs, 14 yr), C3 Y, C4 N, C5 N → 3/5. Series 51 → 47.1 → 46.8 → 47.8 → 47.3 %; the 2012 report itself says differences from earlier studies "likely result from the quality of the mapping as much as changes in the landscape" (p.3); the 2023 report claims methodological consistency only for 2018 vs 2022 by reprocessing 2018 with the 2022 pipeline (p.3); only a single-date 2022 accuracy (97.3 %, p.36) — no inconsistency metric, no change validation.
- [R2, exact shape] Communauté métropolitaine de Montréal, Indice de canopée métropolitain 2011/2015/2017/2019/2021/2023 — methodology note (Apr 2017, upd. Sept 2024; 6 pp, read via Wayback; `r2_read_12.md`, `txt/r2_12_cmm_methode.txt`): 1 m NDVI ≥ 0.3 ∧ photogrammetric height ≥ 3 m, per 1 km² tile, wall-to-wall; no lidar; NDVI threshold "vérifié chaque année … ajusté à 0,4 pour l'année 2023 pour tenir compte de l'ombrage" (p.2) = F3 by hand; 2015 processed from raw 16-bit RGBI rather than delivered 8-bit RGB (p.4–5); no accuracy or consistency number anywhere; indicator "oscille autour de 25,5 % … ne montre pas de tendance nette", dips attributed to emerald ash borer without an artifact-vs-signal test → C1 Y, C2 Y, C3 Y, C4 N, C5 N = 3/5.
- [4.5] Cai, Hu & Parajuli 2025 (Sci Data, Orlando biennial NAIP 2013–21) — 3/5: five epochs but only 8 years, and validation used ~3,600 polygons on land "unchanged from 2013–2021", so its trend (41.1→38.2 %) is never checked against anything that can see change.
- [4.6] Landry et al. 2021 Tampa 1973–2021 (6 epochs, multi-vendor aerial) — 3/5: the multi-epoch change estimate deliberately abandons the canopy map for dot-grid photo-interpretation (p.29), so no segmentation series and no inconsistency metric exist.
- [4.2] Nguyen et al. 2024 (RSE, Swiss Alps 1946–2020) — 2/5 as twin (single agency, inconsistency only shown qualitatively, no change validation); as a B-candidate it is the best single method found but ignores operating-point matching and its temporal prior may suppress the edge-concentrated loss that is our signal.
- [4.7] Chesapeake Bay 1 m LULC change (UVM SAL / Chesapeake Conservancy, 2013/14→2017/18→2021/22) — 2–3/5: only 2–3 epochs and the change product's own accuracy assessment was "TBD" at publication (methods p.38); later-reported change accuracies 75 % / 56 % (via Pawlak p.25).
- [4.8] Kulha et al. 2018 (Forests, boreal aerial-photo Bayesian calibration) — has a real per-vintage bias/error model + credibility-threshold kill criterion, but calibration needs destructive per-landscape dendrochronology fieldwork for every vintage → fails scalability, not just needing "two epochs of lidar."
- [4.8] Munyati & Sinthumule 2016 (SpringerPlus, Kruger woody cover) — texture-not-DN trick sidesteps radiometry, but authors explicitly disclaim their own change magnitudes as merely directional because classification error is never propagated into the change estimate → no kill criterion, fails outright.

## 7. Reasons to doubt §1 (evidence only)
Seven items, written in FINAL_DELIVERABLE_v2.md §6: (1) F1 delivered-cut sign test retracted by commits a9dd52c/e0cad3c/447dc28 (matched cuts −5.59/−3.48 pp, sign agrees, overshoots); (2) F7's 1.3 pp floor is a delivered-cut number (matched 0.15 pp; quartet 3.55 pp); (3) F2 overstated relative to F3; (4) F5 smoothing pilot has no record (CHATLOG, flicker items, phase4_qc_flicker.py, both experiment yamls checked); (5) citation gaps for F3/F4/F5/F6 figures; (6) F8 interpreter-error evidence thin (2/28); (7) EagleView −0.94 pp/4 yr corroborates Panel A pace.

Item 7 (kill test can fail on cross-date bias): passed 3/3 for the first time by PACC and Change Census, but nominally — PACC's B statistic is presently UNDETERMINED (−2.32 ± 3.62 pp) and Census KT3 is circular as written; the single settling experiment = pre-registered, date-blinded, order-randomized re-interpretation of a Neyman-sized stable stratum (N≈2,070 for ±1.5 pp ≈ 1.1 h; 4,657 for ±1.0 pp ≈ 2.5 h) with same-window replicate deliveries seeded as true nulls.
- R5b VARIANCE-AWARE DESIGNS (wf_90caa0c0-6d5; 3 opus designers; judges + critic pending at 10:35): (1) SHADE — shadow-aware discriminability-emission HMM on the existing probability stack: per-(date × shadow-proneness bin × edge bin × GSD bin) discriminability d′ = (m1−m0)/σ on a rank-probit scale (36 cells × 8 dates), Hugonnet-style heteroscedastic error from covariates; shadow-proneness from 54 DSM ray-casts (azimuth 100–260°, elevation 15–55°) computable from DATE alone since flight times are absent from the catalog; loss = pairwise posterior ≥ τ_loss AND 2-of-N run-length confirmation; no MMU, no morphology, no spatial MRF, no raised edge threshold; τ set by Neyman–Pearson on stable anchors (false-loss ≤ 0.30 pp of domain area per epoch); K0 = partial R² of shadow-proneness on per-pixel transition count controlling density and edge (PASS ≥ 0.10, KILL < 0.03); < 1 min per EM sweep, 0 GPU. (2) RC-LoD — replicate-calibrated shadow-aware level-of-detection gate on margin units z = (logit p − logit θ)/σ; σ²(x) fitted by GBM/GAM on halved squared replicate differences (scan–rescan estimator) with covariates predicted shadow exposure s_t, observed shadow index/dark_px, edge distance, GSD, bands, texture, height, caster staleness; shadow enters as variance covariate, as a per-date mean offset b_t stratified by shadow class (the term that lets a whole-delivery shadow bias fail K3), and as a class never a deletion; object rule Z(O) = |mean Δz|/σ_mean(O) > k2 with k1 = 1.0 fixed seeds and failing objects routed to a SUB-LoD stratum, not to 'no loss'; K0 = on 2019 only, excess variance σ²_acq − σ²_deliv regressed on |Δ shadow| in bins, kills the shadow arm if the slope CI includes zero or partial R² < 0.10; ~1.2 GPU-days inference, ~4 human-days. (3) GPA-WEDGE — object-level sequential test on edge wedges: y_it = μ_i + signal + b_t + ε_it with σ²_it = σ²_reg + σ²_model + σ²_shadow + σ²_grid learned from the archive's replicates as a stratified empirical residual table (same-flight 2019 pair = shadow-free floor; same-window cross-flight 2017 pair = shadow+acquisition excess); event if r_it < −q_0.05(stratum) with GPA-style confirmation; b_t = trimmed-median residual on CORE stable objects (Nuth & Kääb per-epoch bias); shadow via within-date darkness covariate and wedge bearing (morning flights shadow the west side) — no cross-date radiometric comparison; K0 = cross-flight excess variance < 10 % of per-object variance kills the shadow arm; K2a de-biased total must fall inside Panel A's [−3.31, −1.11] pp; ~0.15 GPU-days (needs 2015n/2015s inference).
- R5b RESULT part 1 (wf_90caa0c0-6d5, 41 agents, 87 min, cut by the session usage limit at 11:10 → resumed as wu9v3yl54 for the 6 missing judges + critic): 5 sweeps → 175 queries, 59 raw / 57 unique candidates (uncertainty-aware CD 42 q / 13 c; shadow 45 q / 21 c; temporal variance/LoD 40 q / 10 c; cross-domain test-retest 36 q / 15 c; S2 12 q / 0 c — 429 throughout); repo fact-finder OK; 22 reads (21 READ-FULL, 1 UNREADABLE: Shen 2024 TGRS aleatoric-uncertainty semi-supervised CD). No published method IS a variance-aware, shadow-aware change detector for multi-date VHR canopy maps; closest pieces: Brown 2004 GeoDynamics chapter (per-pixel geometric + thematic uncertainty combined into a normalized change-vector gate on airborne CASI; synthetic no-change false-change rates); Storey et al. 2017 (transient-shadow detection in multi-temporal aerial imagery via two-date intensity ratios, OA 93.3 % vs Otsu 84.7 %; penumbra 'beyond scope'); Rui et al. 2021 (glaucoma event-based progression: per-location baseline-stratified 5 % test-retest quantile + k-of-N confirmation, FPR ≈5 %); Artes et al. 2014 (population test-retest limits + persistence, cumulative FPR 2.6 %); Nguyen et al. 2018 (Neyman–Pearson statistical detection of change on subtraction images, sensitivity .964 vs .614 reader); Montesano 2017 (sun elevation >35° vs <25° flips which surface a DSM sees; RMSE <0.68 m vs >1.0 m); Clements et al. (directional Benjamini–Hochberg mdFDR change testing across pixels); Zhou et al. 2014 (object-level geometric spurious-change flag for urban vegetation at individual-tree scale); Gaber 2025 uncertainty-aware tree-height change regression (label-side gates only). SHADE judged 16/16/15, CONDITIONAL×3, item 7 true×3, item 8 (edge preservation) F/T/F; flaws: (a) the 2-of-N confirmation is undefined at the terminal 2024 epoch (no later imagery exists), (b) within-date rank transform + anchors makes K2 pass toward zero for the wrong reason, (c) the DSM shadow-proneness covariate is TIME-INVARIANT and nearly coextensive with the edge band, so 'shadow-aware' degenerates to 'edge-aware' and down-weights exactly the population carrying the verified signal — a per-date shadow field needs per-flight sun geometry, which the catalog lacks (dates only).
- R5b RESULT part 2 (resume wu9v3yl54, 41/41 agents; rv_result_inner.json, rv_designs.json, rv_critic.md, QUERY_LOGS_RV.md, rv_reads_table.md): judges /18 — RC-LoD 16/17/16 CONDITIONAL×3, item 7 true×3, item 8 (preserves 2 m edge loss) FALSE×3: σ is monotone in an ENDOGENOUS edge distance so the level of detection is set above the amplitude of the loss this archive contains (Δz = m̃(1/σ₂−1/σ₁) is non-zero at stable pixels whenever σ changes; standardize-then-difference is the wrong order — Wheaton/Hugonnet difference first then divide by √(σ₁²+σ₂²)); SHADE 16/16/15 CONDITIONAL×3, item 7 true×3, item 8 T/F/T: calibration blind to sub-5 m canopy (D1 anchors are ≥5 m in both CHMs), terminal-epoch confirmation hole (~25 % of 2016–24 loss censored), covariate set collapses to shadow×edge (GSD indexed by date only; 96 not 288 cells; no >45 cm epoch in the trend8 set); GPA-WEDGE 16/15/17 CONDITIONAL×3, item 7 true×3, item 8 T/F/T: the two replicates nominated to estimate σ_shadow both hold sun geometry CONSTANT (of_2017/of_2017k are the SAME orthomosaic, r median 0.9923 — imagery_pixelsize_and_date.csv:12,19), and b_t is a scalar on CORE objects while shadow bias is edge-concentrated (fix: Nuth & Kääb bias SURFACE b_t(bearing, depth band) using the 1,211 Panel-A no-change points with measured boundary distances). CRITIC (rv_critic.md): (1) NO published method is both variance-aware and shadow-aware for multi-date VHR vegetation maps — Storey 2017 shadow rule is variance-blind; Jiang 2023 / Chugg 2021 are variance-aware without shadow; the variance half is cross-domain (glaucoma GPA, Rui 2021); (2) repo can estimate per-delivery sun geometry only partially — no structured sun column, ~5 of 37 measured, raw shadow-dating evidence dir archived; cheapest path = the validated shadow-azimuth instrument per delivery → time-of-day → elevation, then a CHM ray-cast (minutes) — with the killer caveat that a single-epoch CHM cannot cast the shadow of a tree that is gone; (3) GPA-WEDGE conditionally best (only object-level k-of-N test with published evidence of preserving hit rate at high-noise locations); SETTLING EXPERIMENT on existing files, no shadow raster: per-crown disagreement ANISOTROPY — sun-ward vs anti-sun-ward half disagreement, afternoon mask (2021, sun az 247–256°) vs morning masks (2016 SUNEL 34–37, az 283–295; 2020 az 121–193); only shadow predicts the asymmetry axis ROTATES with azimuth; control = same-flight 2019s/2019n must be isotropic; both isotropic ⇒ shadow is not driving the 47.83 % flicker; (4) unswept: Nielsen 2007 IR-MAD / Canty & Nielsen 2008 (per-pixel χ² no-change probability invariant to affine radiometric change), Zhu & Woodcock 2014 CCDC / Zhu 2020 COLD (RMSE-scaled residual + 3-consecutive confirmation; Fmask cloud-shadow projection as DSM-free caster template), the archived Scripts/qc/imagery_date_evidence/ (47 files); (5) for the paper: the literature supports shadows as a real, large, separately detectable source of spurious change in multi-date VHR aerial imagery (Storey 93.3 % OA; Gorte 2014 physical correction) but NOT as the dominant cause of per-pixel canopy flicker — no study attributes a measured flicker rate to shadow against edge, operating-point and delivery confounds.

**Total engine calls:** OA ≈ 19 (incl. abstract lookups), arXiv 5, WebSearch 2 (of 4 allowed). No Semantic Scholar / Crossref used (not needed; OpenAlex + arXiv + WebSearch sufficed).

**Note on OpenAlex relevance search:** the `search=` full-text relevance ranking on OpenAlex is very noisy for compound technical phrases in this niche — most 15-result pages returned generic RS/DL survey papers unrelated to the query terms. `filter=title.search:` (Q6/Q7) was much more precise but only catches papers with the phrase in the title. Recommend future angles lean on title-search + direct arXiv phrase search over free-text OpenAlex relevance search.
