# APPENDIX E — Round-2 B-design panel (4 designs × 3 judges, verbatim)
# Round-2 Condition-B designs and judge panels (verbatim structured returns)

## DESIGN: {
 "name": "Traveling-Delivery Invariance (TDI)",
 "angle": "paired-delivery-invariance",
 "one_paragraph": "The archive already contains its own traveling subjects. F7 is one flight delivered two ways and F3 is three deliveries of the same year; in both, true canopy is identical by construction, so every pixel of disagreement is pure processing-chain noise with zero temporal confound — the aerial analogue of scanning the same subject twice back-to-back, where \"the brain volume difference should be 0%\" (DeepBVC p.5). TDI mines those two natural experiments into a co-registered pair bank and trains one segmentation network on the 2020 hand labels plus a symmetric consistency term computed only between members of a same-date pair, at a 2 m tolerance scale so ortho-DEM misregistration is not mistaken for chain difference. Nothing is ever paired across dates, so the model is never told change is rare, and F6's dispersed, edge-concentrated loss is never smoothed away. The frozen model is then BCTS-calibrated once on held-out 2020 labels and given a per-date operating point by Saerens EM plus inference adjustment, which needs no per-date reference — and the intellectual glue is that the consistency training is precisely what makes p(x|y) delivery-invariant, which is exactly the precondition label-shift correction requires (\"a presumption for rectifying the classifier bias using Eq. (4) is the data conditional distribution is well aligned\", Liu 2022 p.3). Finally, the same-date disagreement that survives all of this is not hidden: it is measured and published as the minimum-detectable-change floor the trend must clear.",
 "inputs": [
  "F7 pair: the single flight delivered two ways (deliveries D_a, D_b), full city coverage of both. Split by spatial block into three disjoint sets: blocks A (goes into the pair bank), blocks B (lambda/hyperparameter tuning only), blocks C (never seen by anything - reserved for the secondary kill test).",
  "F3 2017 triple: three same-year deliveries T1, T2, T3. T1 and T2 supply pair-bank tiles. T3 is held out ENTIRELY - no training, no calibration, no hyperparameter tuning - and is chosen as the delivery whose program is most unlike the two training chains (e.g. if T1/T2 are city + commercial, hold out the NAIP/state-consortium delivery), so the transfer test is not easier than the archive.",
  "2020 hand labels plus the 2020 acquisition raster, split by spatial block: block A trains the supervised term; block B is the calibration set only and is never trained on.",
  "All 37 acquisitions, resampled to a common 0.5 m working grid - inference only.",
  "The 1,250 F8 paired interpretation points: LOCKED. Not used for training, calibration, threshold selection, loss weighting, or the kill criterion. Final validation only, after the kill decision is made.",
  "Lidar 2005 and 2016: external sanity anchor only. Deleting both leaves training, calibration, the trend estimate and the kill criterion unchanged.",
  "A ~200-tile human registration-QA sample drawn from the pair bank (the only new human labelling in the design)."
 ],
 "steps": [
  "1. Common working grid, BEFORE detection. Resample every acquisition to one 0.5 m grid, one fixed resampler, one projection. This is explicitly not the F2 post-hoc fix (which acted after detection and moved <0.1 pp); its only job is to remove GSD as a free variable inside the pair bank so the consistency term learns compression/sharpening/colour-balance/seam/ortho-DEM artifacts rather than resolution. 1 m NAIP is upsampled; 5 cm city flights are downsampled.",
  "2. Pairwise co-registration of the natural experiments. Per-tile dense tie-point matching plus affine between D_a/D_b and between T1/T2. Log residual shift per tile; discard tiles with >1.5 px residual. Human QA on ~200 tiles. This is the step that decides whether the consistency loss is measuring chain difference or measuring misregistration.",
  "3. Build the pair bank: ~10,000-20,000 co-registered 512x512 tile pairs drawn from (D_a,D_b) blocks A and (T1,T2). Every pair is a zero-temporal-confound sample - canopy is identical in both members by construction, exactly as DeepBVC treats two same-day scans as 'a longitudinal pair with no atrophy' (p.5).",
  "4. Train ONE segmentation network with L = L_sup(2020 block A) + lambda * L_pdi. L_pdi is symmetric (neither delivery is the reference - unlike DeepHarmony, which harmonizes toward Protocol #2) and compares the two members' probability maps AFTER 2 m pooling to fractional canopy. The 2 m tolerance scale is deliberate: pixel-exact consistency would penalize residual ortho misregistration at crown edges and train the model to blur exactly where F6 says the real signal lives. No temporal term anywhere. No cross-date pairing.",
  "4a. Deliberate omission, stated as part of the design: no Aslani-style marginal domain-confusion head over program/year. Program correlates with year and canopy prevalence differs by year, so making features program-uninformative can suppress the real ~2 pp signal - marginal alignment 'may lead to the phenomenon of misalignment' (Liu 2022 p.2) and label shift biases the classifier even after alignment (Liu 2022 p.3, Eq. 4). Same-date pairing sidesteps this because the label distribution is identical within a pair by construction.",
  "5. Freeze all weights. Fit bias-corrected temperature scaling (class-specific bias parameters, not plain TS) once on 2020 block B. This is the direct F3 remedy for the two of three probability rasters that were near-binary/miscalibrated: 'Best results are obtained with variants of TS containing class-specific bias parameters that can correct for systematic bias' (Alexandari p.2).",
  "6. Per-date operating point, 37 times, with no per-date labels. Run Saerens EM on the calibrated probabilities to estimate that date's canopy prior q_t, then apply inference adjustment - rescale the posterior by q_t/p_s and argmax (Liu 2022 p.5, Eq. 14). The estimated prior IS the per-date threshold. Log q_t, EM iterations to convergence, and posterior entropy per date as diagnostics.",
  "7. Measure the invariance floor. After step 6 recompute (a) the F7 pair's citywide fraction difference and IoU on held-out blocks C, and (b) the spread among T1/T2/T3. Define sigma_chain as the SD of same-date matched fraction differences. Use simple averaging of T1/T2 as the reference fraction rather than latent-class modelling, because SA 'would be less sensitive to the interpreters selected' (Xing p.11) and here we have only three deliveries.",
  "8. Convert sigma_chain into a minimum detectable trend using a paired-difference form - the same move Bautin makes from scan-rescan variability to a minimum detectable atrophy (p.1) and Roman makes from interpreter disagreement to a detectable-change threshold (p.7-8). Publish the 2016-2024 map difference WITH this floor attached, not as a bare number.",
  "9. Validation (deliberately not the kill): compare the TDI 2016-2024 estimate against the F8 human paired-point estimate of -2.21 pp (CI +/-1.10). Sign agreement first, magnitude second. This happens after the kill decision so the criterion stays non-circular.",
  "10. External anchor: compare the 2005 and 2016 TDI fractions to the two lidar epochs. Expect a level offset (lidar applies a height cut-off, image interpretation does not - Roman p.7 reports 93.4% agreement with 4.3% of disagreement from that definitional difference). Check the 2005-2016 CHANGE, not the levels."
 ],
 "outputs": "37 per-date canopy maps on one 0.5 m grid, each carrying its own explicitly estimated operating point (q_t) rather than a delivered threshold; a per-chain invariance report card giving same-date citywide fraction difference and IoU before and after L_pdi, for the F7 pair (held-out blocks C) and the 2017 triple; sigma_chain in percentage points; a minimum-detectable-trend floor derived from sigma_chain; the 2016-2024 and full-series trend estimates reported against that floor; and an ablation table (L_pdi on vs off) that says how much of any improvement the paired-delivery angle actually bought.",
 "checklist": {
  "no_radiometric_calibration": "Nothing in TDI estimates or applies a cross-date radiometric transform - no PIF, no histogram matching, no image synthesis, no target-chain rendering. All invariance supervision comes from two deliveries of the SAME flight, so whatever radiometry each chain produced is simply what the pair contains. The only per-date adjustment is a scalar class prior estimated from the model's own calibrated outputs by EM, which never touches pixel values. This is a deliberate departure from the harmonization family: DeepHarmony synthesizes images into a target contrast, HACA3+ recombines anatomy and contrast latents; TDI enforces invariance at the decision level only, which also removes any hallucination risk on the degraded end of the archive.",
  "no_extra_lidar": "The 2005 and 2016 lidar epochs appear once, in step 10, as an external sanity anchor on the 2005-2016 change. They are not training data, not calibration data, not inputs to EM, and not part of the kill criterion. Deleting both files leaves every number in steps 1-9 bit-identical. No third lidar epoch is requested or implied.",
  "operating_point": "Directly and centrally addressed, not orthogonal. F3 is the design's premise: three same-year deliveries spread 10.1 pp at delivered thresholds and collapse to 1.07 pp under reference-matched cuts, and two of three rasters were near-binary/miscalibrated. TDI attacks both halves - BCTS repairs the miscalibration on the 2020 held-out block ('first, calibrate predictions using the held-out training set, then perform domain adaptation on the calibrated predictions using EM', Alexandari p.2), then Saerens EM plus inference adjustment gives each of the 37 dates its own decision threshold with no per-date reference labels. The whole claim is that L_pdi buys the reference-matched collapse without a reference.",
  "not_smoothing_not_inheritance": "Consistency is enforced ONLY between two deliveries of the same flight, where true change is exactly zero by construction - never between dates, never with a 'change is rare' prior. Contrast Nguyen 2024, whose temporal consistency rests on the assumption that 'the forest cover remains mainly unchanged between consecutive acquisitions' (p.7) and who then has to engineer a gradient-magnitude scaling term to stop the loss suppressing real loss (p.8). TDI needs no such escape hatch because it has no temporal term to escape from. At inference the 37 maps are produced independently: nothing is propagated, nothing is inherited, no state carries across dates. F6's dispersed losses (median 2.0 m from a crown edge, largest cluster 0.41 ha) are therefore still detectable, and F5's failed temporal-smoothing pilot is not repeated.",
  "scales": "<=4 GPU-days and ~3 human-days on commodity hardware, no bespoke sensors. Training on ~15k co-registered tile pairs at 512x512 is 1-2 GPU-days on one card; inference over 37 acquisitions x 25 km2 at 0.5 m (~10^8 px each) is ~1.5 GPU-days; BCTS/EM sweeps are ~0.5 GPU-day (EM is O(mN) per iteration and needs no retraining or hyperparameter tuning - Alexandari p.2). Human cost is 1 day of registration QA on ~200 pair tiles, 0.5 day reviewing calibration and EM diagnostics, 1-1.5 days readout. The F8 points already exist and no new interpretation is commissioned.",
  "kill_criterion": "PRIMARY (transfer): the fully held-out 2017 delivery T3, after BCTS+EM+IA, must land within 1.07 pp of the simple average of the matched T1 and T2 citywide fractions. A gap >=1.07 pp means TDI has bought nothing over the reference-matched cuts of F3 - which cannot be produced for the 35 unlabeled years - and the design is dead. PRIMARY (attribution, the one that tests THIS angle): run the identical frozen architecture, identical 2020 labels, identical BCTS+EM+IA, with L_pdi switched OFF. TDI survives only if the L_pdi-on T3 gap beats the L_pdi-off T3 gap by >=0.3 pp absolute or >=25% of the L_pdi-off gap, whichever is larger. If L_pdi-off already lands under 1.07 pp without that margin, the operating-point step did all the work, paired-delivery invariance contributed nothing, and the angle is dead even though the pipeline 'passes'. SECONDARY (floor): on F7 blocks C - never in the pair bank, never used for lambda tuning - the two deliveries' citywide fractions must differ by <1.3 pp and IoU must exceed 0.738. Failing to beat the frozen-model noise floor on genuinely unseen ground means the invariance did not generalize past the tiles it was fit on."
 },
 "expected_failure_modes": [
  "Misregistration masquerading as chain difference. Different ortho DEMs between deliveries shift crown edges by a pixel or more; a pixel-exact consistency loss would train the model to blur edges - destroying exactly the F6 signal (verified losses median 2.0 m from a crown edge). Mitigated by the 2 m tolerance scale and the >1.5 px tile rejection. Diagnostic symptom: contour-band F1 on 2020 block B falls while overall F1 holds.",
  "Coverage extrapolation. Paired supervision spans at most four chains from at most two dates. 2005 county imagery and 1 m NAIP are plausibly off the learned invariance manifold, and the pair bank contains no 1 m member at all, so nothing supervises the upsampled-NAIP regime. Upsampling 1 m to 0.5 m invents no detail but does change texture statistics relative to 5 cm city flights.",
  "F4's within-sensor radiometric greenness gradient has zero paired supervision, because both members of every pair share the same radiometry. EM will silently absorb any residual p(x|y) drift into what looks like an ordinary prior shift and return a plausible, confident, wrong q_t - a smooth wrong trend rather than a sawtooth one. This is the single most dangerous mode because it is quiet.",
  "Season is likewise unsupervised. Flights run Feb-Oct and every pair shares a season, so leaf-off/leaf-on p(x|y) shift has no paired anchor and rides the same EM absorption path as F4. F4 says the measured gradient is a whole-distribution radiometric shift and not phenology, so this should not be overweighted - but it is structurally identical and equally invisible.",
  "EM degeneracy. On a near-binary or very low-entropy date (e.g. a heavily compressed early acquisition), the maximum-likelihood prior can converge to something that tracks scene brightness rather than canopy prevalence. Diagnostic: log posterior entropy and EM iteration count per date; flag dates whose q_t sits at the edge of the plausible range.",
  "Consistency collapse. The trivial minimizer of a symmetric consistency term is a constant prediction. If lambda is too large, citywide fraction drifts toward 0 or 1 on unlabeled dates while the pair difference looks excellent. Cap lambda on F7 blocks B and monitor the fraction trajectory, not just the loss.",
  "The F7 pair is ONE flight. Its two chains may differ less than a typical cross-program pair, which would make sigma_chain optimistic and the published minimum-detectable-change floor too small - i.e. the design would understate its own uncertainty in exactly the direction that produces false trend confidence."
 ],
 "sources": [
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_15_deepbvc.txt",
   "page": "5",
   "claim": "A same-occasion image pair can be treated as a labelled zero-change sample - the precedent for using the F7 pair and the 2017 triple as zero-temporal-confound supervision.",
   "quote": "We grouped the data into baseline follow-up pairs as follows: the two scans from the same day and subject were used as a longitudinal pair with no atrophy. We used 60 pairs in total (20 pairs per subject). For this experiment, we assumed that there would be no atrophy because the pairs were scanned back-to-back (i.e. the brain volume difference should be 0%)."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_15_deepbvc.txt",
   "page": "4",
   "claim": "A consistency regularization term added to a supervised loss makes a segmentation/regression model insensitive to acquisition-chain differences - the form of L_pdi.",
   "quote": "Furthermore, to render the model insensitive to differences in imaging quality during training, we adopted a consistency regularisation loss. Specifically, the loss minimised the difference between the predictions derived from the original images and the augmented images, such that brain volume differences were maintained in the context of an isolated change in imaging acquisition conditions."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_15_deepbvc.txt",
   "page": "11",
   "claim": "Consistency regularization is the mechanism that delivers protocol invariance, not merely an accuracy trick.",
   "quote": "Consistency regularisation renders the predictions invariant to noise applied to the input, and is widely used in semi-supervised learning and learning from noisy labels"
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_38_deepharmony.txt",
   "page": "n/a - file is unpaginated HTML full text; cited by section heading '2.1. Data'",
   "claim": "A small overlap cohort scanned under both chains is sufficient training data for chain harmonization - and in DeepHarmony it had to be acquired prospectively, whereas TDI finds its overlap cohort already in the archive.",
   "quote": "To create a training dataset for all harmonization methods, 12 subjects (10 subjects with multiple sclerosis (MS) and 2 healthy subjects) were scanned twice within 30 days on two separate Philips Achieva 3T scanners (Philips Healthcare, Best, The Netherlands) with differing hardware and a different scanning protocol"
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_38_deepharmony.txt",
   "page": "n/a - unpaginated HTML; cited by section heading '3.4. Longitudinal Stability' and Table 3",
   "claim": "Overlap-cohort training removes a delivery-chain step-change from a longitudinal trend - the outcome TDI is aiming at for the 2016-2024 sign flip.",
   "quote": "only the cGM volume from harmonized data retained a significant effect of protocol on the model, whereas all volumes from the acquired data show a significant effect."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_36_haca3.txt",
   "page": "8",
   "claim": "Same-subject repeat acquisitions are the standard way to quantify inter-chain variability, and reduction of that variability is itself the harmonization metric - the model for TDI's invariance report card.",
   "quote": "To quantify inter-scanner variability, the coefficient of variation (CV) was computed for both DSC and volume for each brain region. Lower CV values indicate reduced variability and, thus, better harmonization consistency across scanners."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_36_haca3.txt",
   "page": "16",
   "claim": "Traveling-subject validation demonstrably reduces measured inter-chain variability in the downstream quantity of interest (regional volume), i.e. the paired residual is the right thing to optimize and report.",
   "quote": "On the FTHP dataset involving a single subject who traveled to 116 different sites, harmonization with either model led to significant reductions in inter-scanner variability, measured as the CV for both DSC and regional brain volume using SLANT labels."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\alexandari_bcts.txt",
   "page": "2",
   "claim": "Calibrate-then-EM is the correct order of operations for per-date prior estimation, and it requires neither retraining nor hyperparameter tuning - which is why step 6 costs almost nothing on 37 dates.",
   "quote": "This suggests a simple yet novel hybrid algorithm for adapting to label shift: first, calibrate predictions using the held-out training set, then perform domain adaptation on the calibrated predictions using EM."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\alexandari_bcts.txt",
   "page": "2",
   "claim": "Plain temperature scaling is not sufficient; class-specific bias parameters (BCTS) are needed - directly relevant to F3's near-binary, miscalibrated delivered rasters.",
   "quote": "We observed that the popular calibration approach of Temperature Scaling (TS) (Guo et al., 2017) does not tend to achieve the best results for adaptation to label shift, possibly owing to large systematic biases in the calibrated probabilities (Fig. 1). Best results are obtained with variants of TS containing class-specific bias parameters that can correct for systematic bias."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\undoing_label_shift_seg.txt",
   "page": "3",
   "claim": "The precondition that makes per-date prior correction valid is conditional-distribution alignment - which is exactly what the paired-delivery consistency term supplies. This is the intellectual join between the two halves of TDI.",
   "quote": "As shown above, a presumption for rectifying the classifier bias using Eq. (4) is the data conditional distribution is well aligned."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\undoing_label_shift_seg.txt",
   "page": "5",
   "claim": "Inference adjustment - rescaling the posterior by the target/source prior ratio at inference time - is the concrete per-date operating-point mechanism used in step 6.",
   "quote": "An alternative idea is that we directly adjust the output of the existing network Gcda in the inference stage on the target domain. Since the conditional probability distribution is now aligned, the hypothesis of label shift is satisfied."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\undoing_label_shift_seg.txt",
   "page": "2",
   "claim": "Marginal-distribution adversarial alignment can actively misalign - the justification for step 4a's refusal to add a program/year domain-confusion head where program correlates with year and prevalence differs by year.",
   "quote": "This methodology usually focuses on aligning the marginal distribution between the source domain and target domain under the assumption of covariate shift. Recently, researchers have found that this alignment strategy may lead to the phenomenon of misalignment [7, 24]."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_35_scanner_invariant_mri.txt",
   "page": "2",
   "claim": "The domain-adversarial route TDI deliberately declines: an auxiliary head that makes features domain-uninformative. Cited to name what is being rejected and why (it aligns marginals over a domain variable that here is confounded with year).",
   "quote": "However, to handle the domain shift problem, we propose a regularization network including an auxiliary loss function that is designed to encourage the model to ignore domain-specific information."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_28_bautin_mdc.txt",
   "page": "1",
   "claim": "Simulated scan-rescan variability is the correct basis for a minimum-detectable-change statement - step 8's move from sigma_chain to a trend floor.",
   "quote": "In this study, we evaluated the precision and accuracy of automated atrophy measurements by simulating scan-rescan experiments."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_28_bautin_mdc.txt",
   "page": "5",
   "claim": "Repeat-measure coefficient of variation is the quantity that propagates into detectability - the template for reporting sigma_chain rather than burying it.",
   "quote": "At the intra-subject level, the estimated CSA showed good precision, with COVs (across Monte Carlo transformations) of 0.8% for T1w images and 0.57% for T2w images."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\roman2021_land_phila.txt",
   "page": "7",
   "claim": "In-domain precedent for converting measured repeat-observer disagreement into a published detectable-change threshold - TDI does the same for processing-chain disagreement.",
   "quote": "Considering inherent user variation, and to be cautious in our conclusions, we propose a detectable change threshold as follows:"
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\roman2021_land_phila.txt",
   "page": "8",
   "claim": "The threshold is stated as a number that a trend must clear, exactly the form step 8 requires.",
   "quote": "any 40-year UTC changes larger than 3.2 percentage points are considered detectable change."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\nguyen2024_swissalps.txt",
   "page": "7",
   "claim": "The assumption TDI refuses to make. Temporal consistency losses are built on 'change is rare' - the precise assumption that is blind to F6's dispersed loss and that produced F5's failed smoothing pilot.",
   "quote": "The assumption of semantic similarity between consecutive images in a times series or video holds true for the vast majority of pixels in our multi-temporal forest segmentation task, since the forest cover remains mainly unchanged between consecutive acquisitions."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\nguyen2024_swissalps.txt",
   "page": "8",
   "claim": "Even a well-engineered temporal consistency loss has to be explicitly relaxed so it does not suppress real loss - evidence that temporal coupling is the wrong place to buy consistency when loss is the signal. TDI avoids the problem by having no temporal term.",
   "quote": "Scaling the cosine loss term with ||∇p_t,i||2 relaxes the alignment constraint in the case of forest loss since ∇p_t,i has a norm close to 0."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_25_xing.txt",
   "page": "11",
   "claim": "Justifies using simple averaging of T1/T2 as the reference fraction in step 7 rather than latent-class modelling over the three deliveries - SA is less sensitive to which labellers/deliveries happen to be in the set, which matters with only three.",
   "quote": "SA had smaller variability than LCM over different interpreter subsets within each interpreter group size, indicating that SA would be less sensitive to the interpreters selected to determine the reference land cover data."
  }
 ],
 "novelty_vs_known_families": "Nearest family is method-holding / harmonization (DeepHarmony's overlap cohort, HACA3+'s traveling subjects, Mboga's DANN/CORAL). Four differences. (a) The overlap cohort is FOUND, not commissioned: DeepHarmony had to prospectively scan 12 subjects twice; TDI's F7 pair and 2017 triple already sit in the archive as accidental natural experiments, which is why this is cheap and why it works retrospectively on 20 years that can never be re-flown. (b) Invariance is enforced at the decision level with no image synthesis - nothing is rendered into a target chain's appearance, so there is no hallucination risk on the degraded end of the archive and no reference delivery is privileged (L_pdi is symmetric; DeepHarmony harmonizes toward Protocol #2). (c) The same paired residual that trains the invariance is then published as the minimum-detectable-change floor. Roman 2021 (p.7-8) already closes that loop for interpreter disagreement in urban canopy; TDI closes it for processing-chain disagreement, and uniquely derives the floor from the very pairs that trained the invariance, so the training set and the uncertainty budget are the same object. (d) Paired-delivery invariance is fused with explicit per-date label-shift correction, and the first is what makes the second's stated precondition true (Liu p.3) - neither literature does both. Against the other families: not temporal filtering / HMM / LandTrendr-style (there is no temporal model at all, and the 37 maps are produced independently); not calibrated time-series algorithms such as CCDC (those presuppose a radiometrically calibrated stack, which this archive cannot supply); not trajectories or stability inheritance (nothing is inherited across dates, so dispersed edge loss stays visible); not sample-based change estimation in the Olofsson/McRoberts/Stehman sense (the F8 sample is the validator, deliberately locked, not the estimator). And it is explicitly NOT the domain-adversarial route (Aslani p.2, Mboga), which here is a label-shift trap because program is confounded with year.",
 "cost_estimate": "<=4 GPU-days and ~3 human-days. Training: ~15k co-registered 512x512 tile pairs, supervised + consistency, 1-2 GPU-days on a single commodity card. Inference: 37 acquisitions x 25 km2 at 0.5 m is ~10^8 px each, roughly 1 hour per acquisition, ~1.5 GPU-days. BCTS fit plus 37 EM runs plus the L_pdi-off ablation retrain shares the same budget, ~0.5 GPU-day beyond training (EM itself is O(mN) per iteration and needs no retraining or hyperparameter tuning). Human: 1 day registration QA on ~200 pair tiles, 0.5 day reviewing calibration curves and per-date EM diagnostics, 1-1.5 days readout and write-up. No new imagery, no new interpretation (the 1,250 F8 points already exist), no bespoke sensors, no third lidar epoch.",
 "self_assessed_weakest_point": "F4. Every member of every pair shares its radiometry, so the natural experiment has exactly zero purchase on the within-sensor, across-year radiometric greenness gradient (recall -.109 across same-sensor years, a whole-distribution shift). Worse, that residual p(x|y) drift does not announce itself: the EM step will absorb it into what looks like an ordinary prior shift and hand back a plausible per-date threshold, so the pipeline's characteristic failure is a smooth, confident, wrong trend rather than a visibly sawtoothing one - arguably a worse failure than F1's, because it survives inspection. And the only empirical test in the design, the held-out T3 delivery, probes cross-chain shift within a single year; it says nothing about cross-year shift. Seasonality (Feb-Oct flights) rides the same blind path. The lidar anchors at 2005 and 2016 are the only cross-decade check and they are weak - two points, with a definitional level offset between lidar height cut-off and image interpretation. If F4's gradient survives step 4, TDI passes its own kill criterion and still gets the trend wrong."
}

### JUDGMENT: {
 "design_name": "Traveling-Delivery Invariance (TDI)",

 },
 "total": 18,
 "source_check": "Read all cited files in ...\\scratchpad\\txt\\. Every quote is VERBATIM. Spot-verified: r2_15_deepbvc.txt L266-267 (\"the two scans from the same day and subject were used as a longitudinal pair with no atrophy\"; \"scanned back-to-back (i.e. the brain volume difference should be 0%)\") = p.5 as cited; L219 consistency-regularisation quote = p.4 as cited; L467 \"renders the predictions invariant to noise\" = p.11 as cited. alexandari_bcts.txt L28 and L31 both on p.2 as cited, exact. undoing_label_shift_seg.txt L90 \"a presumption for rectifying the classifier bias...\" = p.3 as cited, exact; L45 misalignment quote = p.2 as cited, exact. roman2021_land_phila.txt L242 \"we propose a detectable change threshold as follows:\" = p.7 as cited; \"any 40-year UTC changes larger / than 3.2 percentage points are considered detectable change\" straddles the p.7/p.8 break, cited p.8 — correct. nguyen2024_swissalps.txt L297 (p.7) and L390 (p.8) exact. r2_25_xing.txt L767 = p.11, exact. r2_36_haca3.txt L899 = p.8, L1043 = p.16, both exact. r2_38_deepharmony.txt L303 (12 subjects, two Achieva scanners) and L596 (cGM/protocol effect) exact; the \"unpaginated HTML, cited by section heading\" disclosure is honest. r2_35_scanner_invariant_mri.txt: Aslani quote spans L36-38 across a page break, exact. TWO OFF-BY-ONE PAGE SLIPS, both benign: Liu \"Inference Adjustment (IA)\" is cited p.5 but sits on p.4 immediately before the p.5 marker; Bautin \"COVs (across Monte Carlo transformations) of 0.8% for T1w images and 0.57% for T2w images\" is cited p.5 but is on p.6 (the abstract on p.1 gives the rounded 0.6% variant — the design quoted the body, correctly). One interpretive stretch, not a misquote: DeepBVC's consistency loss (p.4) is computed between original and SYNTHETICALLY AUGMENTED images, while its same-day pairs (p.5) are an EVALUATION set, never training data. TDI fuses the two — real same-occasion pairs used as training supervision — which is genuinely new rather than \"as DeepBVC did.\" The design's own wording (\"precedent\", \"treats ... as\") stays on the right side of this, so fidelity stands at 2.",
 "fatal_flaw": "TDI IS A VARIANCE REDUCER AIMED AT A BIAS PROBLEM, AND ITS HEADLINE ESTIMATOR IS THE ONE QUANTITY ITS BLIND SPOT CONTAMINATES. Three linked steps. (1) What the estimator identifies: after step 6 the map is argmax of a posterior rescaled by q_t/p_s, so each date's citywide fraction is driven by the Saerens-EM prior q̂_t. The trend is therefore, to first order, a sequence of EM prior estimates. (2) Prior shift and conditional shift are NOT separately identified from unlabeled target data — EM cannot distinguish \"there is more canopy\" from \"canopy looks different this year.\" Liu p.3 states the precondition plainly and the design quotes it correctly: rectification is only valid if p(x|y) is aligned. So the whole design rests on L_pdi supplying that alignment. (3) L_pdi provably spans only the axis its pairs span. Both natural experiments are SAME-DATE: the F7 pair is one flight post-processed two ways (same sensor, same sun angle, same season, same GSD — variation only in compression/sharpening/colour-balance/ortho-DEM), and the 2017 triple is one year. Every member of every pair shares its radiometry and its season BY CONSTRUCTION. F4's measured drift — a whole-distribution radiometric greenness gradient, recall −.109 ACROSS SAME-SENSOR YEARS — lies exactly orthogonal to that span. On ~30% canopy a recall shift of .109 is on the order of 3 pp of fraction, larger than the ~2 pp/8 yr signal. EM absorbs that residual p(x|y) drift silently into q̂_t and returns a confident, plausible, wrong prior. So the design buys identification on the axis it can see and ASSUMES it on the axis that carries the signal. Note the diagnosis this implies for F1: the 2016→2024 failure was not merely sawtooth (variance) — it came out SIGN-OPPOSITE, which is bias. Collapsing same-date chain spread does not fix a sign flip produced by cross-year conditional shift, and the failure mode is worse than F1's because a smooth wrong trend survives visual inspection where a sawtooth does not. The kill criterion cannot catch this: T3 is a cross-CHAIN test inside a single year, and the design concedes it \"says nothing about cross-year shift.\" The design self-assesses this as its weakest point, which is admirable honesty — but disclosure is not a fix. TWO SECONDARY DEFECTS the design does NOT flag, both bias not variance. (a) PAIR-BANK SELECTION BIAS: step 2 discards tiles with >1.5 px (0.75 m) residual after per-tile affine. Two deliveries with different ortho-DEMs produce RELIEF DISPLACEMENT, not an affine offset — tall crowns lean differently and by metres. The tiles that fail registration are precisely the tall, dense, high-relief canopy tiles; the surviving pair bank is biased toward open, flat, low-canopy ground. Invariance is thus learned where invariance is easy and deployed where it is hard, and per F6 the real signal lives at crown edges (verified losses median 2.0 m from an edge) — the very geometry being filtered out. (b) CONTAMINATED KILL REFERENCE: the primary kill asks whether T3 lands within 1.07 pp of the average of MATCHED T1 and T2 — but T1 and T2 are in the pair bank, so the model was trained to agree on them. The test asks whether T3 arrives where L_pdi pulled T1/T2, not whether any of the three is right; a partial consistency collapse toward a common wrong fraction passes. Also 1.07 pp came from REFERENCE-matched cuts in F3, so importing it as the tolerance for a self-consistency test is apples-to-oranges. Additionally there is no fallback: the entropy/iteration diagnostics FLAG a degenerate-EM date but nothing says what to do with a flagged 2005 county scan, and no 1 m member exists anywhere in the pair bank, leaving the whole NAIP regime — a large share of 37 acquisitions — with zero invariance supervision.",
 "best_idea_to_graft": "TWO, both transplantable into any winner and neither dependent on TDI's training scheme surviving. (1) THE FOUND NATURAL EXPERIMENT AS A MEASUREMENT INSTRUMENT RATHER THAN A TRAINING SIGNAL. F7 and the 2017 triple are zero-temporal-confound replicates already sitting in the archive — true canopy identical by construction — so their post-matching disagreement is a direct, assumption-free estimate of processing-chain sigma, which converts via the Roman 2021 p.7-8 move (\"we propose a detectable change threshold as follows\"; \"larger than 3.2 percentage points are considered detectable change\") into a published minimum-detectable-change floor that every reported trend must clear. This is the single most valuable element here: it costs nothing, requires no model, cannot be wrong in the way the estimator can, and turns F7's 1.3 pp / IoU 0.738 from an embarrassment into a stated uncertainty budget. Any winner should carry it. One correction on grafting: report sigma_chain as a LOWER BOUND and say so, since one flight processed two ways is the narrowest possible chain contrast and will understate cross-program spread — TDI notices this in its failure modes but still publishes the floor as if it were the floor. (2) THE ATTRIBUTION ABLATION AS A KILL CRITERION. Requiring the L_pdi-ON result to beat the L_pdi-OFF result by ≥0.3 pp absolute or ≥25% relative — so that the design dies even when the pipeline PASSES, if the operating-point machinery did all the work — is the most rigorous kill construction in this design and is the correct general template: pre-register a margin against the ablated baseline, not just a threshold against truth, so a design cannot claim credit for a component that contributed nothing. Graft that pattern regardless of which angle wins.",
 "verdict": "CLEARS THE CHECKLIST OUTRIGHT (18/18) AND IS A TOP-TIER CANDIDATE ON CONSTRAINT SATISFACTION, BUT DO NOT LET IT PRODUCE THE HEADLINE TREND IN ITS CURRENT FORM. Every Condition-B constraint is met cleanly and non-evasively: no cross-date radiometric transform is estimated or applied anywhere (invariance is enforced at the decision level, no image synthesis, so no hallucination risk on the degraded end of the archive); lidar appears once as a droppable external anchor at step 10 and deleting both epochs changes nothing in steps 1-9; the operating point is not dodged but is the design's premise, with BCTS-then-EM in the correct Alexandari order and per-date thresholds that need no per-date reference; there is no temporal term of any kind, the 37 maps are produced independently, nothing is inherited, and the contrast drawn against Nguyen's gradient-scaled escape hatch is exactly right — this is the cleanest non-smoothing, non-inheritance answer among the structural options, and F6's dispersed edge loss stays visible; cost is bounded and, importantly, the expensive human step (registration QA) scales with the NUMBER OF NATURAL EXPERIMENTS (two dates), not with the 37-acquisition archive, so 3 human-days is defensible and the GPU estimate is conservative by roughly an order of magnitude on inference; the kill criterion is sharply pre-numbered, uses a genuinely held-out third program, and uniquely includes an angle-attribution ablation; and the sourcing is the most disciplined I have checked — ~20 verbatim quotes, two trivial off-by-one page slips, honest disclosure of the unpaginated DeepHarmony source, and a deliberate, well-argued REFUSAL (step 4a) to add a domain-adversarial head because program is confounded with year, correctly grounded in Liu p.2. The novelty is real: the overlap cohort is FOUND rather than commissioned, which is what makes it work retrospectively on 20 years that can never be re-flown, and the join — consistency training is what makes the label-shift precondition true — appears in neither the harmonization nor the label-shift literature. The problem is not constraint compliance, it is identification. Same-date pairs contain literally zero information about cross-year conditional shift, F4 has already MEASURED that shift and it is larger than the signal, and the estimator reads the trend off EM priors that absorb exactly that shift. RECOMMENDED DISPOSITION: promote the measurement half (sigma_chain, the MDC floor, the attribution ablation) into the winner immediately; keep L_pdi only as a variance-reduction component, not as the license for EM; and refuse to accept any TDI trend until a CROSS-YEAR kill test exists. Concretely, three cheap repairs would move this from near-miss to winner — (i) build the cross-year test the design lacks: hold out the 2020 hand labels for a second block and, separately, use the 2005/2016 lidar CHANGE as a pre-registered pass/fail on the 2005-2016 EM trend rather than as a post-hoc \"sanity anchor,\" accepting the definitional level offset Roman p.7 documents; (ii) stop reading the trend off q̂_t alone — report the map-derived fraction and q̂_t separately and treat their divergence as a diagnostic, since agreement is assumed rather than checked; (iii) fix the pair bank's registration-failure selection bias by stratifying retained tiles on canopy density and reporting retention rate by density decile, because a bank that silently drops tall dense canopy trains invariance on the wrong half of the city. Without (i) especially, TDI can pass its own kill criterion and still deliver a confident wrong sign — which is precisely the failure the whole exercise exists to prevent."
}
