 },
 "total": 15,
 "fatal_flaw": "The anchor's measurement error is confounded with time, and the estimator has no term for it. The GREG cleans the MAP's per-date bias (F4's ~1 pp chain bias, the greenness offset) because the map is the auxiliary. Nothing cleans the PANEL's per-date bias, because the panel IS the estimand's definition. But the panel's instrument is \"one human reading that year's delivery,\" and the deliveries run 5 cm→1 m GSD and Feb→Oct season in a way that correlates with year. Pengra (the design's own source, p.1 abstract) shows interpreter consistency tracks imagery quality across an archive; Roman p.7 shows the interpreter's tree/shrub call is exactly where a resolution-dependent boundary sits (4.3% over-call vs. lidar, \"large shrubs or small trees\"). A drift of even 0.5–1 pp in where the interpreter cuts shrub-vs-tree between the 1 m early epochs and the 5 cm late epochs is the same order as the entire 2 pp/8 yr signal and is perfectly collinear with the trend. So what μ̂_t identifies is \"the fraction one interpreter called canopy on epoch t's imagery,\" and the GLS slope identifies the sum of the true trend and the drift in that instrument. The design names this (weakest_point item 3, expected_failure_modes item 4) and then declares it unquantified, following Hapke's seasonal-variability precedent. But Hapke could not measure his term; PAT CAN measure its own and does not: the three 2017 deliveries and the F7 same-flight pair are same-date, different-imagery instruments sitting right there, and Steps 13a/13b push them only through the MODEL, never through the INTERPRETER. Declaring an unquantified term that is collinear with the estimand is not the same as identifying the estimand, and this is precisely the class of error (a whole-distribution, epoch-correlated shift, not phenology) that already produced F1's sign-wrong answer on the map route. Compounding, near-fatal in its own right: the vertex protocol is a temporal smoother relocated from the algorithm into the human, and it is the ONLY thing that makes the human budget close, so \"not smoothing\" and \"scales\" cannot both be guaranteed — and the adjudicating test (Step 6b) is self-contaminated, since the blind shuffled pass is run by the SAME primary interpreter who already read those 125 trajectories, so memory biases Δ_blind toward Δ_vertex and the test under-detects the very smoothing it exists to catch. Two secondary statistical slips worth noting but not fatal: (i) Step 7's panel-matched cut forces (1/n)Σŷ_j(t) = (1/n)Σy_j(t), which makes the Step 8 GREG bias term identically ZERO — so the \"two layers, empirical then structural\" story is one layer counted twice, and μ̂_t collapses to the whole-city map fraction at τ_t (equivalently, panel truth plus a panel-to-city map extrapolation). The estimator is still fine and still operating-point-insensitive, but for a different reason than the design states, and only the free-intercept working-model variant does the work claimed for the binary form; (ii) R in Step 12 is computed with τ fitted in-sample on the same 1,250 points at both dates, so it is optimistically biased toward the maps — a failing R kills cleanly, but a passing R needs cross-validation before it can be believed.",
 "best_idea_to_graft": "The fixed-panel T×T residual covariance matrix plus a GLS trend fit — McRoberts' two-date Eq. 13, Cov(μ̂_s,μ̂_t) = [1/(n(n−1))]Σ_j(ε_j(s)−ε̄(s))(ε_j(t)−ε̄(t)), generalized to all 8 epochs and used to fit the slope, instead of treating epoch estimates as independent. This is the single highest value-per-cost element in the design and it is fully separable from everything else in it: it needs no new labels, no map auxiliary, no GREG, no vertex protocol — only that the same points be read at every date, which F8 already guarantees for 2016/2024. It is priced by the source (McRoberts p.155: setting the covariances to zero inflates the model-assisted SE by ~1.5× and the model-based SE by ~2.0×), it is mechanistically justified by canopy persistence at a point (Roman p.8: 65.0% of 1970 tree points stable through 2010), and it is the exact, specific correction to the nearest twin — Pawlak 2026 HAD a fixed panel (4,800 validations, one per point per year) and then took Sen's slope over years treated as independent and got CIs covering zero everywhere. Any winning design that ends up with repeated human observation at fixed locations should carry this; it is nearly free precision that the whole field is currently discarding. Runners-up worth stealing separately: (a) the R = Σ(d_i−d̄)²/Σ(c_i−c̄)² admissibility test as a general, pre-registered, zero-new-label gate on whether ANY map product is allowed to enter a change estimate — it is the cheapest honest go/no-go instrument in the whole tournament and is portable to any candidate; (b) per-date-pair detectable-change thresholds DCT(s,t) = δ_s + δ_t computed from a design-valid 10% random duplicate subsample, rather than Roman's single archive-wide number, which is straightforwardly wrong for a 5 cm–1 m, Feb–Oct archive; (c) the blind shuffled per-date re-interpretation subsample as an anti-smoothing probe — a genuinely good instrument that this design happens to wire up wrong, and that becomes valid the moment it is assigned to the second interpreter rather than the primary.",
 "verdict": "REVISE — keep it as the floor, do not believe its trend yet. This is the most statistically literate and most honest submission I have scored: the estimator is design-unbiased by construction, the variance and covariance estimators are correct and closed-form, the kill criterion is real, cheap, computable today with zero new labels, and pre-registered, and the author volunteers against his own interest that (i) Pawlak already had the panel so the panel is not the novelty, (ii) the Cai-style panel-set threshold means the accuracy figures cannot be quoted as independent validation, and (iii) R ≥ 1 is the most likely outcome, which would demote the whole \"model-assisted\" framing to decoration on the part that matters. That last concession is decisive for scoring: by the author's own reasoning, what survives is Roman 2021 with eight epochs and a covariance matrix — defensible, probably correct for this city, and squarely inside a family the search has already found twice (Roman 4/5, Pawlak 4/5). Hence novelty 1. It clears the archive-facing constraints outright (no radiometry, no lidar, operating point genuinely neutralized by panel anchoring), but it half-clears the two behavioral constraints: the vertex protocol is smoothing relocated into a human, and the budget only closes because of it, so a pass on \"not smoothing\" costs roughly 4× the human budget and a pass on \"scales\" is bought with the mechanism Condition B forbids. The 20–25 h first-pass arithmetic is optimistic by ~2–3× (≈7–9 s per 8-epoch trajectory across heterogeneous orthos with parallax rules, and an asserted 10–15% vertex rate sitting next to F5's 50.7% flicker and F6's edge-concentrated signal), and the synchronized 8-epoch multi-date viewer — co-registering 5 cm county frames to 1 m NAIP well enough that a panel point means the same ground location at a crown edge 2.0 m wide — is real engineering that appears nowhere in the cost line. Two fixes make this a serious contender rather than a well-instrumented fallback, and both are cheap and run on data already in hand: (1) re-interpret the duplicate subsample on all THREE 2017 deliveries and on both renderings of the F7 flight, which converts the fatal anchor-drift term from \"declared unquantified\" into a measured, GSD-indexed panel-instrument variance that can be carried in U_t and, if it is monotone in GSD, partially differenced out of the slope; (2) hand the blind shuffled anti-smoothing pass to the SECOND interpreter so the test is not contaminated by the primary's memory of those same trajectories. Do those, cross-validate R out-of-sample, and report the GLS slope with the panel-instrument term inside the CI rather than in a footnote. As submitted: an excellent, honest measurement floor and a near-certain source of grafts, but its headline number is not yet identified.",
 "source_check": "Read and verified all cited passages verbatim in the scratchpad text files; fidelity is high and I found no fabricated quote. Confirmed: r2_29_mcroberts2014.txt — \"difference between the initial estimator and the expectation of its bias estimate\" (line 235); \"systematic classification errors induce bias into this estimator which, for equal probability samples, can be estimated as\" (line 217); \"Under the assumption that classification errors for 2002 and 2007 are independent for different population units, Cov(μ̂2007, μ̂2002) is calculated using the sample data as\" (line 297); the 1.5×/2.0× SE-inflation passage (line 814); the trajectory/direct/post-classification data-requirements passage (line 29). roman2021_land_phila.txt — 91.5%/92.9%/94.9% and the 1.3 pp two-interpreter difference (line 242); \"3.2 percentage points are considered detectable change\" (line 258); \"minimized by having a single interpreter [24], which was our approach\" (line 79); 93.4% lidar agreement with 4.3% over-call as tree/shrub (line 241); 65.0% stable 1970→2010 (line 267); parallax/misregistration + uninterpretable-point rule (line 127). r2_24_pengra.txt — the 10% simple-random duplicate probability-sample passage (lines 75/88, split across a page break); vertex→annual-label extrapolation (line 108); McRoberts 2018 SE underestimate \"by a factor of 2.3\" (line 41); \"increased interpreter consistency for the post-2000 data record\" (line 19, abstract); 88% overall / 89–94% for the four prevalent classes (line 205). r2_25_xing.txt — forest gain/loss interpreter disagreement, majority assigned forest loss to only 40% of pixels any interpreter flagged (line 153); LCM needs ≥3 interpreters (line 125); the SA compensating-random-error assumption (line 127). pawlak2026_california_utf.txt — Sen's slope −0.60%/yr with CIs including zero (line 39); \"4,800 unique validations\" (line 611); Sen's slope from median pairwise slopes with percentile bounds (lines 740+); the NA rule (lines 600 and 613). shoreline_ofr2010_1118.txt — quadrature end-point rate (line 1451); RSS total uncertainty per Taylor 1997 (line 1110); \"Linear regression fails to recognize the potential for temporal differences in trend\" (line 1136); the explicitly unquantified seasonal-variability term (line 1102). cai2025_orlando_biennial.txt — \"year-specific thresholds, meticulously adjusted to account for inherent inconsistencies in the imagery's spectral and radiometric responses\" (line 207) and \"This year-by-year threshold adjustment ensures that the classification process is entirely independent for each time period\" (line 222). r2_28_bautin_mdc.txt — the paired sample-size formula (lines ~185–200) and the scan-rescan caveat (line 152). chesapeake_change_accuracy_methods.txt — \"Locating change omission. Assess validity of stratification by distance to change patch of same T1 LC value for no change samples\" (lines 148/156); the 144-change-class collapse and square-root-transformed proportional allocation (lines 93/100); the 3×3-window sample-rejection rule (lines 113/124/132). Three minor slips, none affecting the score materially: (a) the Bautin source formula carries a \"/2\" divisor that Step 11d's restatement drops — conservative in direction (it inflates required n ~2×), but the design should not misquote a formula it also reproduces correctly in its own sources block; (b) the Pawlak NA quote is an ellipsis-joined splice across a page break (p.11→p.12) attributed wholly to \"p. 11 of 53\"; (c) Roman's duplicate subsample is 5%, not the 10% the design uses — correctly attributed to Pengra rather than Roman, so this is a deliberate and properly sourced substitution, not an error. Notably, several quotes are cited AGAINST the design's own interest (Cai's independence caveat, Pawlak's pre-existing panel, Xing's change-disagreement numbers, Xing's LCM limitation), which is the strongest available evidence that the sourcing is genuine rather than decorative."
}

### JUDGMENT: {
 "design_name": "PAT — Panel-Anchored Trajectory estimation (fixed paired panel + per-date maps as GREG auxiliaries + measured interpreter detection floor)",
 "scores": {
  "no_radiometric_calibration": 2,
  "no_extra_lidar": 2,
  "operating_point": 2,
  "not_smoothing_not_inheritance": 1,
  "scales": 2,
  "kill_criterion": 2,
  "novelty": 1,
  "implementability": 2,
  "source_fidelity": 2
 },
 "total": 16,
 "fatal_flaw": "None fatal. Nearest-fatal: the design's ONLY exposure to Condition B's anti-smoothing clause is guarded by a check that cannot fire. The vertex/trajectory response design is — in the author's own words — \"a TEMPORAL SMOOTHER relocated from the algorithm into the interpreter,\" and it is load-bearing: it is the sole reason 10,000 point-dates fit in a few human-days. Step 6b's blind-shuffled subsample is 125 points; at realistic 2016→2024 gross-change rates the SE of (Δ_vertex − Δ_blind) is roughly 1.5–2 pp against a stated 1.0 pp kill threshold, so the test will \"pass\" by being unpowered, not by being clean. The field failure mode is concrete: a 1 m Feb leaf-off frame sitting between two 5 cm leaf-on frames, and a trajectory reader who defers to the neighbours — exactly the F6 dispersed single-epoch edge loss disappearing into a human median filter. The author's stated fix (fully blind per-date interpretation of all 10,000 point-dates) is ~4x the budget and blows the scaling clause, but a middle path exists (400–500 blind points, ~20–24 h), so this is a scoping defect, not a killer. Secondary, and an overstatement rather than a flaw: the \"two-layer\" operating-point defense is one layer. Step 7 sets tau_t so the map's canopy fraction on the panel equals the panel's fraction, which forces the GREG bias term mean(eps) to zero by construction — so Step 8's per-date bias subtraction returns the raw whole-city map fraction and the claim that \"the GREG intercept absorbs F4's ~1 pp delivery-chain bias\" is vacuous after Step 7 already zeroed it. The GREG still buys real precision (Var(eps) is untouched), so this is not a Condition-B violation, but the mechanism is oversold. Related missed opportunity: fraction-matching tau does not minimise Var(eps); choosing tau to maximise point-level panel agreement, or committing to the Step-8 logistic working model instead of leaving it \"optional,\" would. Two unverified dependencies to flag: (a) the design assumes the F8 panel has KNOWN INCLUSION WEIGHTS — the problem statement never says so; if F8 was drawn purposively for a 2016→2024 change check rather than as a probability sample of the city, Step 8 is not a valid citywide bias estimate at any epoch and the whole estimator degrades to a non-design-based comparison; (b) F8's existing 2016/2024 labels came from a two-date paired protocol, and the design never says whether they are re-read under the 8-date vertex protocol (making Step 12's \"zero new labels\" claim inconsistent) or inherited (leaving two of eight epochs on a different response design).",
 "best_idea_to_graft": "The R statistic as a zero-cost, PRE-REGISTERED admissibility gate on the maps themselves: R = Sum(d_i - dbar)^2 / Sum(c_i - cbar)^2, where d_i is the map's per-point change error and c_i the panel's observed change, computed today on the existing 1,250 F8 points at 2016/2024 for ~2 GPU-hours and zero new interpretation. R >= 1 means the maps make the CHANGE estimate less precise than a bare panel. Any Condition-B design that proposes to use per-date maps for trend should be required to pass this gate before a single human-day is committed — it is the cheapest decisive test in the whole field, it is front-loaded ahead of the expensive half of the pipeline, and the author pre-registers his own prediction that his headline fails it. Second graft, nearly as good: the fixed-panel T x T covariance (McRoberts Eq. 13 generalized from 2 dates to 8, verified verbatim on p.150) fitted by GLS instead of Sen's slope over independent years. That is the concrete, priced (1.5–2.0x SE) correction to Pawlak 2026's every-CI-covers-zero outcome, and it is the mechanism most likely to turn F8's 1,250 paired points from a two-date authority into a 20-year slope whose CI clears zero. Third, cheap and portable: per-date-PAIR detectable-change thresholds DCT(s,t) = delta_s + delta_t from a 10% simple-random duplicate subsample, rather than one global number — correct for an archive spanning 5 cm to 1 m and Feb to Oct, where Roman's single 3.2 pp figure would be wrong in both directions.",
 "source_check": "VERIFIED, exemplary — the strongest source fidelity I have scored. I opened every cited file and grepped ~22 quotes; all are present verbatim (only OCR de-hyphenation and equation mangling differ). Spot checks: McRoberts r2_29 line 235 \"difference between the initial estimator and the expectation of its bias estimate\"; line 217 \"systematic classification errors induce bias into this estimator\"; line 814 the full covariance/1.5x-2.0x SE passage; lines 295-321 the Eq.(13) covariance with eps_i = yhat_i - y_i; line 29 the trajectory-vs-direct-vs-post-classification tradeoff. Roman 2021 lines 241-242 (93.4% lidar agreement, 4.3% over-call direction; 91.5%/92.9%/94.9% duplicate agreement, 1.3 pp interpreter difference), 258 (3.2 pp 40-yr DCT), 79 (single-interpreter rationale), 267 (4.3 pp change, 65.0% stable), 127 (parallax/misregistration/uninterpretable rules). Pengra r2_24 line 88 (10% simple-random duplicate subsample, probability-sample argument), 108 (vertex extrapolation), 41 (2.3x SE underestimate), 19 (post-2000 consistency), 205 (88% overall, 89-94% prevalent classes). Xing r2_25 lines 153 (forest gain/loss interpreter disagreement), 125 (LCM needs 3+ interpreters), 127 (SA compensating-error assumption). Pawlak lines 39 (Sen's slope -0.60, CIs include zero), 600-611 (NA rule, edges/shrubs/shadows, 4,800 validations), 740 (median of pairwise slopes). Hapke/shoreline lines 1451, 1136, 1110, 1102 all verbatim. Bautin r2_28 lines 183-193 (paired formula incl. the /2) and 152 (scan-rescan caveat). Cai lines 222 and 207 (year-specific thresholds). Chesapeake slides: change-omission-by-distance, 144 change classes / sqrt-transformed allocation, 3x3 window rule. Beyond verbatim accuracy, the CLAIMS attached to the quotes are honest readings, including two concessions that cut against the author: the Cai citation is used to flag that the panel-set threshold is NOT independent validation, and the Pawlak p.12 citation concedes Pawlak already had the fixed panel, so the delta is a correction rather than an invention. One trivial imprecision: Roman's duplicate subsample was 5%, not the 10% the design adopts — but the design attributes the 10% to Pengra, correctly.",
 "verdict": "STRONG PASS on Condition B, and the most deliverable design I have scored — but its novelty is a refinement, not a new family, and its author says so. Every hard constraint holds: no cross-date radiometry is ever compared (per-date panel-referenced cuts, Cai's precedent); lidar appears only as an external check at the two epochs already held, and the design still runs if it vanished; the operating point is met head-on with exactly F3's reference-matched cut (10.1 pp -> 1.07 pp) plus an internal test on all three 2017 deliveries and on the F7 same-flight pair. No map pixel is ever filtered, no state is inherited across epochs, and the change-stratified augmentation carries Chesapeake's change-OMISSION check, which is the specific thing that keeps a map-stratified panel from inheriting the map's blindness. Cost is arithmetic rather than assertion — ~2 GPU-days, 5-7 person-days base — and it is the only design here whose budget I could hand to a city consultant. In practice I expect the human side to roughly double: nobody has costed building a synchronised 8-date viewer over a 5 cm-1 m multi-CRS archive (2-5 dev-days), 20-25 s/point is optimistic for urban crown edges where 60-90 s is normal, and Step 6b needs 400-500 points not 125. Even at 12-15 person-days it delivers, with no bespoke sensors and trivial GPU, so scales stays a 2. The kill criterion is the best in the set: front-loaded, decisive, and costed under a day before any human budget is committed. Where it loses points: (1) the vertex protocol is a self-declared smoothing channel whose guard cannot resolve its own threshold — partial, not clear; (2) novelty is honestly bounded — Roman 2021's panel plus Pawlak 2026's per-year error adjustment plus McRoberts' 2-date covariance stretched to 8x8 and fitted by GLS, with three real but incremental additions (panel-as-threshold-reference at every date, the R admissibility test, per-date-pair DCT). The author predicts R >= 1 for the binary auxiliary, i.e. pre-registers the death of his own \"model-assisted\" headline, leaving a well-instrumented panel survey as the deliverable. That fallback is probably the correct answer for this city and it is a defensible one — but it is not a new method, and the honesty should be rewarded in the graft, not in the score. Recommend: advance, with the vertex-smoothing check enlarged to ~450 points and the F8 inclusion-probability question answered before anything else is funded."
}

 "design_name": "PAT — Panel-Anchored Trajectory estimation",
 "scores": {

 "total": 14,
 "source_check": "I read every cited file and located every quote. Verbatim accuracy is high — no fabricated quotes, and two places where the design self-flags its own overreach (Cai's independence safeguard; \"Pawlak DID have a fixed panel\"). Verified exactly: McRoberts r2_29 lines 235 (estimator = initial minus expectation of bias, PDF p.3/p.151), 217 (Eq.10 bias), 300 (Eq.13 covariance), 814 (positive covariance, 1.5x/2.0x SE, PDF p.7/p.155), 29 (trajectory/direct/post-classification tradeoff, PDF p.1). Roman lines 242, 258, 79, 241, 267, 127 — all verbatim. Pengra lines 88, 108, 41, 205, 19 — verbatim, pages correct. Xing lines 153, 125, 127 — verbatim. Pawlak lines 36, 244, 294, 243 — verbatim; 1,200 urban points placed once on 2020 strata, validated once per year = 4,800, so \"Pawlak had the panel\" is TRUE. Hapke lines 1451, 1110, 1136, 1102 — verbatim, all four page attributions correct. Bautin line 183 and 152 — verbatim. Chesapeake slides 10 and 12 — verbatim.\n\nNINE DEFECTS FOUND (none fabrication; one is a substantive misattribution):\n(1) SUBSTANTIVE — Roman p.8 \"65.0% remained stable through 2010\" is attached to the claim that this is \"the physical reason the panel's epoch estimates are strongly positively covariant... the mechanism Step 9 exploits.\" Step 9's covariance is of the MAP-ERROR residual e_j(t)=yhat-y, not of the truth y. Truth persistence at a point does not make map errors persist across dates; that requires the error mechanism (shadow, edge, delivery chain) to be stable, and F5 (50.7% flicker) says it is not. The cited page does not support the mechanism claimed, and this sits directly under the design's headline novelty.\n(2) SUBSTANTIVE — Roman p.5 single-interpreter quote says only that interpreter variability \"can be minimized by having a single interpreter.\" The design asserts Roman chose this \"for precisely this reason\" (systematic level bias cancels in the between-date difference). That rationale is the design's own, attributed to the source.\n(3) Roman p.8 DCT: the design states DCT(s,t) = delta_s + delta_t is \"Roman's threshold\" construction. Roman's Eq.1 body is illegible in the extracted file (symbols dropped); the quoted sentence (\"larger than 3.2 percentage points\") establishes the number, not the construction. Presented as verified; it is a reconstruction. (Sanity check: 2 x 1.3 pp = 2.6, not 3.2, so the reading is not obviously right.)\n(4) McRoberts Eq.13 covariance cited as \"p.150 (PDF p.2 of 8)\" — it is on p.152 (PDF p.4 of 8). Wrong page on the single most load-bearing citation in the design. Equation number is correct.\n(5) Pengra 10% quote is prefixed \"For Sets 1-12, a minimum of\" — that prefix is not present in the cited file (line 88 begins \"10% of the reference sample pixels...\"). Embellished quote boundary.\n(6) Xing \"compensate over the full sample\" cited p.5 of 17; it is on p.6 (line 127, after the p.6 marker at 119).\n(7) Pawlak \"Each point was validated once per year...\" cited p.12 of 53; the sentence begins on p.11 and only its tail is on p.12.\n(8) Bautin: the quoted formula is npaired = (z+z)^2 (sigma_diff/Delta)^2 / 2 — the file plainly carries the /2 denominator. Step 11d drops it, doubling the required n and the third kill threshold relative to the source it cites.\n(9) Cai p.6 is cited as precedent for Step 7, but the quoted sentence's stated virtue is that thresholding is \"independent from the validation samples themselves, effectively mitigating the risk of accuracy overestimation\" — Step 7 sets the threshold ON the panel, i.e. does the opposite of the safeguard the quote sells. The design flags this itself, which earns credit, but the citation still does not support the step.\n\nAlso internally inconsistent across two cited formulas: Hapke PDF p.20 states its quadrature budget rests on terms being \"random and independent of the others,\" and Step 11c applies it — inside a design whose entire Step 9 exists to exploit NON-independence across dates. If the panel covariance is negative (the design's own expected case), the Hapke LoD understates.",
 "fatal_flaw": "The costed design satisfies not_smoothing AND scales only if the Step 6b anti-smoothing test passes — and the design's own cited evidence predicts it will not. The TimeSync vertex protocol it borrows (Pengra p.5, quoted in the design: \"converted into annual labels by extrapolating the recorded values at each vertex back until another labeled vertex is reached\") is label inheritance by construction: every date without a vertex inherits the last vertexed label. That inheritance is applied precisely where F6 puts the real signal — crown edges, median 2.0 m, largest persistent cluster 0.41 ha — and Xing (the design's own source, p.7) reports that a majority of seven trained interpreters assigned forest loss to only 40% of pixels any one of them called forest loss. The design names this (\"a TEMPORAL SMOOTHER relocated from the algorithm into the interpreter\") and gives it a 1.0 pp kill number, but the only remedy is fully blind per-date interpretation of 10,000 point-dates at roughly 4x the human budget, which breaks Condition B's scaling clause. Judged as costed rather than as fallback, PAT is stability-inheritance moved from the raster into the interpreter. Runner-up (not the flaw, but it hollows the verdict): the GREG precision gain and the fixed-panel covariance gain are one mechanism — positive map-error covariance across dates — which F5 and F7 say is absent or reversed, so R >= 1 and what remains is Roman 2021 with eight epochs. The author says so himself. Separately, Step 7 forces the panel-mean map fraction to equal the panel-mean truth fraction, which sets the Step 8 bias term to approximately zero by construction; the advertised \"two layers\" of operating-point defense are one layer plus a tautology, unless the free-intercept working model (not the binary map) is actually used.",
 "best_idea_to_graft": "The R statistic (Step 12) paired with the blind-shuffled subsample (Step 6b) — two cheap, pre-registered instruments that between them audit both halves of any panel-plus-map design. R = Sum(d_i - dbar)^2 / Sum(c_i - cbar)^2, where d_i is the reference-matched map's point-level change error and c_i is the panel's observed change, runs TODAY on the existing 1,250 F8 points at 2016/2024 with zero new labelling and about two GPU-hours, and answers the one question every map-assisted trend design begs but none of the surveyed literature asks: do the maps make the CHANGE estimate more precise than a bare panel, or less? Any winning design should be forced to state its R before spending a human-day. Step 6b is the human-side twin: a second, disjoint 10% subsample re-read by the SAME interpreter with dates shuffled and presented one at a time after a washout, compared against the trajectory-protocol reading on the same points — it is the only cheap instrument in any of the candidates that measures whether a response design is itself smoothing, and it should be mandatory in any design that shows an interpreter more than one date at a time. Secondary graft: per-date-pair rather than global detectable-change thresholds, justified by Pengra's finding that interpreter consistency tracks imagery quality — correct and necessary for an archive spanning 5 cm to 1 m and Feb to Oct.",
 "verdict": "14/18. Honest, well-instrumented, and the most auditable design I have scored — but its two headline novelties are both self-predicted to die, and its cost floor rests on a protocol that is label inheritance by construction. Source work is strong in letter and weak in one important place in spirit: quotes are verbatim and no finding is fabricated, but the Roman \"65.0% stable\" citation is made to carry a mechanism (map-error covariance) that the page does not support and F5 contradicts, and the load-bearing McRoberts Eq.13 cite has the wrong page. What survives, and survives well, is the estimator's honesty: a GREG anchored on a probability panel is unbiased for each epoch regardless of which threshold survived, needs no radiometric cross-calibration, needs no lidar beyond the two epochs held, and cannot filter dispersed loss out of the raster because it never touches the raster. If R >= 1 (likely) and 6b fails (likely), PAT degrades to a defensible, correctly-floored panel survey — which is probably the right answer for this city but is Roman 2021 with a covariance matrix, not a new design. Recommend: do not advance PAT whole; graft R and Step 6b into the winner as mandatory pre-registered gates, and require any panel-based candidate to declare its response design blind-per-date or justify the inheritance."
}

## DESIGN: {
 "name": "G-COPT: Greenness-Conditioned Operating-Point Transfer",
 "angle": "calibration-transfer",
 "one_paragraph": "Freeze one detector trained once on the 2020 hand labels, cache its raw logits for all 37 deliveries, and move every cross-date consistency decision out of the image domain and into the decision domain. Per delivery d the calibrated logit is an affine map l'_d(x) = a_d*l(x) + b_d (bias-corrected temperature scaling, which in the binary case reduces to Platt scaling), but (a_d, b_d) are NOT free per delivery -- they are a partially-pooled regression on measurable delivery covariates (a posterior-internal greenness statistic, the frozen model's logit-histogram bimodality, log GSD, DOY spline, program, vendor), fit hierarchically against a small point panel interpreted at all 37 dates, with 5-fold cross-fitting so no panel point's posterior is in-sample. Per-date prevalence then comes from Saerens/Alexandari EM run on the calibrated logits over the whole city, bias-corrected against the cross-fitted panel via McRoberts's model-assisted regression estimator; only then is the per-delivery cut chosen as the quantile that reproduces that corrected prevalence (Ruetschi's threshold-moving to MBE~0). F3's operating-point spread is therefore closed by estimation rather than by eye, and F4's radiometric drift is carried by a fitted covariate coefficient rather than assumed away. Nothing anywhere uses a pixel's value at another date, so F6's dispersed edge loss is not suppressed. The replicate deliveries the archive already contains -- F3's 2017 triple and F7's same-flight pair -- are internal traveling subjects whose true delta is exactly zero: the 2017 triple carries a replicate-consistency penalty and is the leave-one-delivery-out kill test, and the F7 pair is held out entirely.",
 "inputs": [
  "All 37 orthophoto deliveries, resampled once to a 30 cm working GSD BEFORE detection. This is a pre-detection choice, not F2's post-hoc grid harmonization; the log-GSD covariate then carries residual source-resolution texture effects across the 5 cm-1 m span.",
  "Delivery-chain metadata for each of the 37: program, vendor, GSD, acquisition DOY, band count, radiometric depth, compression. Covariates, not corrections.",
  "The 2020 hand labels, split train / calibration-holdout / test. The calibration-holdout is what BCTS and the source-prior estimate are fit on (Alexandari steps 1-2).",
  "NEW panel P: 150 systematic-random points interpreted canopy / non-canopy / obscured at ALL 37 dates (5,550 point-date interpretations). Reference definition is CROWN PROJECTION REGARDLESS OF FOLIAGE -- a bare winter crown counts as canopy. Without this the 2017 replicate penalty and the DOY spline would absorb a real leaf-off visibility difference as if it were bias.",
  "NEW blind QC subset: 15% of panel point-dates re-interpreted by a second interpreter in randomized SINGLE-DATE order (not trajectory view), disagreement rate reported as a number. Pengra's probability-subsample QC design, needed because the trajectory viewer itself risks importing stability-inheritance into the reference.",
  "NEW change-booster C: 150 points drawn from the high-|delta logit| stratum between the endpoint deliveries, interpreted at those two dates (Chesapeake stratify-on-mapped-change, so the validation design can see change).",
  "The existing 1,250 paired human points -- HELD OUT ENTIRELY. Test of the trend only; excluded from the calibration likelihood so F8 stays an independent authority.",
  "Lidar 2005 and 2016 -- held-out audit only, evaluated at panel points; never a pipeline input, never a per-date feature."
 ],
 "steps": [
  "1. Train ONE frozen detector on the 2020 labels, with deliberately MINIMAL photometric augmentation. This is a design choice, not an oversight: augmenting across the archive's observed greenness range would let the model partially absorb F4's drift, alpha_G would collapse toward zero for the wrong reason, and the covariate test would become vacuous. The drift must stay visible in the logits. Export logits, never masks; cache them once. Every subsequent step runs on cached logits at CPU cost.",
  "2. Compute delivery covariates z_d. PRIMARY greenness covariate is G_post, a shape statistic of the frozen model's own logit histogram over the whole city -- fully internal, zero radiometry, no invariant targets, no image transformation. OPTIONAL second variant G_img (median VARI/excess-green over a fixed invariant-surface mask digitized once from 2020) is included only if it adds explanatory power. Plus logit-histogram bimodality, which measures how confidently the frozen model separates classes on that delivery's imagery. Plus log GSD, DOY spline, program, vendor.",
  "3. Interpret panel P and booster C in a trajectory viewer (all 37 dates for a point on one screen) for speed, with the 15% blind randomized single-date re-interpretation as QC. Report the disagreement rate.",
  "4. DIAGNOSTIC D1 -- measure the two baselines the design must beat. (a) IDENTITY baseline: frozen model, fixed 0.5 cut, prevalence spread across the 2017 triple. Call it S_id; F3 puts vendor-delivered thresholds at 10.1 pp and reference-matched cuts at 1.07 pp, so S_id lies between and must be measured, not assumed. (b) LABEL-SHIFT-ONLY baseline: fit BCTS on the 2020 holdout, set the source prior to the mean calibrated posterior on that holdout (Alexandari Lemma A), apply (a_2020, b_2020) unchanged to every delivery, run EM per delivery. Report F7's |delta q| and the 2017 spread. If both already clear the kill thresholds, the covariate is unnecessary and we ship this simpler thing. F4 predicts they will not, because Lemma A's guarantee holds only 'in the absence of domain shift' and F4 is a p(x|y) shift, not a prior shift.",
  "5. Fit the hierarchical covariate calibration with 5-FOLD CROSS-FITTING over panel points. (a_d, b_d) = linear in z_d + partially-pooled per-delivery random effect, Bayesian; likelihood = panel Bernoulli labels across all 37 dates; the 2020 delivery additionally pinned by its full label set; plus a replicate-consistency penalty forcing the three 2017 deliveries to a common prevalence. F7 excluded. For each of 5 folds, fit on the other 4 and predict on the held-out fold so every panel point's calibrated posterior is out-of-sample. Cost: 5 refits on cached logits, CPU-minutes. Without cross-fitting, step 7's bias estimate is ~0 by construction -- NLL fitting drives the mean calibrated posterior on the panel to the panel prevalence -- and its SE is understated.",
  "6. Run EM prevalence estimation per delivery on the covariate-calibrated logits, over all pixels. Honest role: 150 panel points cannot pin b_d per delivery (per-date panel SE ~3.7 pp), so the pooled covariate model sets it approximately and EM refines it toward what the whole-image logit distribution supports. EM is a refinement of the covariate-predicted prior, not an independent estimator.",
  "7. Model-assisted bias correction against the CROSS-FITTED panel posteriors: mu_d = q_d minus the estimated bias at date d, with SEs (McRoberts's regression estimator, the initial estimator minus the expectation of its bias estimate). Because the panel is the same points at every date, cross-date covariances are positive and Var(delta mu) shrinks -- McRoberts measured 1.5-2.0x larger SEs when the covariance is dropped.",
  "8. Derive the reference-matched cut LAST: t_d = the quantile of the calibrated posterior at date d that reproduces mu_d. This yields the mask. Ordering matters -- calibrate, then estimate prevalence, then cut; never cut first and count pixels.",
  "9. Estimate the trend by GLS of mu_d on year using the full cross-date covariance matrix. Then compare to the held-out 1,250 paired points. This comparison is a TEST, not an input.",
  "10. Publish the per-pixel change map only above a Kulha-style credible threshold (99% posterior mass), floored at F7's 1.3 pp. CRITICAL: this LoD filter applies to the MAP PRODUCT only. The estimate mu_d and the trend never pass through it, so F6's dispersed sub-LoD loss still enters the reported number even when it is not drawn on the map.",
  "11. Lidar audit: 2005 and 2016 CHM-derived canopy at panel points versus the fitted model's predicted prevalence for those two deliveries. A partly independent held-out check on 2 of 37 deliveries."
 ],
 "outputs": "Seven products. (1) 37 covariate-calibrated posterior rasters plus 37 reference-matched binary masks at 30 cm. (2) A per-date prevalence table mu_d with SEs and the full cross-date covariance matrix. (3) The trend: a GLS slope in pp/yr with CI, plus the 2016-2024 contrast to set against the held-out paired-point truth. (4) THE OPERATING-POINT LEDGER -- a per-delivery table of (a_d, b_d), their decomposition into covariate contributions and the shrunk random effect, and the derived cut t_d. This is the audit trail that F3 says the archive currently lacks, and it is a first-class deliverable, not a diagnostic. (5) The fitted greenness coefficient with CI, which either does or does not explain F4's -0.109 recall gradient -- a direct, publishable answer to whether the drift is capturable by a scalar covariate. (6) A change map showing only patches above the credible threshold, reported as patches with areas, never as pixel counts. (7) The kill-test report: identity baseline S_id, label-shift-only baseline, LODO spreads on the 2017 triple and F7, panel interpreter disagreement rate, and the lidar audit residuals at 2005 and 2016.",
 "checklist": {
  "no_radiometric_calibration": "PASSES. No pixel value is ever transformed, no calibration target, PIF set, histogram match or reflectance model is required, and no delivery is rewritten. The primary greenness covariate G_post is a shape statistic of the FROZEN MODEL'S OWN LOGIT HISTOGRAM -- computed from the detector's output, containing no radiometry at all -- so a strict reader who counts invariant-surface statistics as calibration still finds nothing to object to. The optional G_img is a scalar regressor whose coefficient is estimated, so it need only be monotonically informative, not physically calibrated; an arbitrary monotone error in it is absorbed by the fitted coefficient. F2 is the positive argument: harmonizing the raster grid moved fractions <0.1 pp because the inconsistency is baked in at DETECTION time, so the intervention belongs at the decision, which is exactly where G-COPT acts.",
  "no_extra_lidar": "PASSES. Lidar appears at exactly the two epochs we have (2005, 2016) and only as a held-out audit of the fitted calibration model's predicted prevalence at panel points. No step of the pipeline consumes height data: the detector is RGB-trained on 2020, the covariates are metadata plus logit statistics, the panel is photo-interpreted, and the estimator is model-assisted on posteriors. Delete the lidar entirely and every number still computes -- we simply lose one of three external checks.",
  "operating_point": "This IS the design, not an addendum. F3 is the load-bearing fact: three same-year 2017 deliveries spread 10.1 pp at delivered thresholds and collapse to 1.07 pp under reference-matched cuts, and two of three probability rasters were near-binary or miscalibrated. G-COPT (i) never uses a delivered threshold or a fixed 0.5 cut, (ii) fits an explicit per-delivery affine posterior recalibration whose parameters are predicted from covariates so unlabeled deliveries get them too, (iii) derives each cut as the quantile reproducing an independently estimated prevalence (Ruetschi's threshold-moving, which took MAE 6.04% to 2.68% at MBE~0 against a probability sample), and (iv) publishes the resulting ledger. The near-binary/miscalibration problem is handled by working from the frozen model's own logits -- we never ingest a vendor probability raster -- with logit-histogram bimodality as a covariate capturing how confidently the model separates classes on that delivery.",
  "not_smoothing_not_inheritance": "PASSES on a hard structural constraint: no step reads a pixel's value, label or posterior at any other date. Each date's posterior is a function of that date's image and that delivery's scalar covariates only. There is no temporal prior, no HMM, no trajectory fit, no majority-vote-over-years, and no rule that a pixel stable at t-1 and t+1 must be stable at t. Trajectory viewing is used ONLY for human interpretation speed, and the 15% blind randomized single-date re-interpretation exists precisely to measure whether that convenience leaked inheritance into the reference. F5 matters here: naive temporal smoothing moved the estimate AWAY from truth, and 50.7% of ever-canopy pixels flicker, so any method that buys consistency by suppressing transitions is disqualified. G-COPT buys consistency by fixing the decision threshold instead. The one place a magnitude filter appears -- step 10's credible-change threshold -- is applied to the MAP PRODUCT only; mu_d and the trend never pass through it, so F6's dispersed, edge-concentrated, sub-LoD loss still enters the reported number.",
  "scales": "PASSES. GPU: ~0.5 GPU-day to train one frozen detector on the 2020 labels, plus ~1 GPU-day to infer 37 x ~278 Mpx (25 km2 at 30 cm) = under 2 GPU-days total, with no per-year retraining ever. The decisive economy is that logits are computed ONCE and cached, so the hierarchical fit, 5-fold cross-fitting, EM, all LODO refits and every threshold sweep cost CPU-minutes, not GPU time. Human: ~34 h panel interpretation (5,550 point-dates at ~22 s in a trajectory viewer), ~6 h blind re-check, ~2 h booster, ~1.5 days analysis = ~6.5-7 person-days one-time. Honest tension: 7 is at the upper edge of 'a few'. The defense is that it is one-time -- the marginal cost of each future delivery is ~0 human-days (compute covariates, predict a_d/b_d). No bespoke sensors, no new flights, no field work.",
  "kill_criterion": "PRE-TEST FIRST, because a vacuous test is worse than none. The F7 pair is the SAME FLIGHT, so DOY, program, vendor and GSD are identical across it and only G_post, G_img and bimodality can differ; if those are also indistinguishable, no covariate model could ever see the 1.3 pp, F7 would kill every possible design, and it demotes to floor confirmation rather than a test. Same pre-test on the 2017 triple. PRIMARY KILL, with the number: leave one 2017 delivery out of the fit entirely, predict its (a_d, b_d) from covariates ALONE -- no reference-matched cut, no random effect, no panel data for that delivery -- and compute the prevalence spread across the triple. If that spread EXCEEDS 2.6 pp (2x F7's 1.3 pp processing-chain floor), OR fails to beat the measured identity baseline S_id by at least 50%, G-COPT is dead. The second clause is essential: an identity map trivially passes any threshold set above the floor, so the design must be shown to actually move, not merely to not make things worse. SECONDARY: if the F7 pre-test shows distinguishable covariates, the covariate-only predicted prevalence difference across the F7 pair must be <= 1.3 pp. TERTIARY: the 2016-2024 GLS estimate must agree in SIGN with the held-out 1,250 paired points (~+2 pp, CI +/-1.10) and its CI must overlap theirs -- this is the direct replacement for F1's sign-opposite -2.21 pp."
 },
 "expected_failure_modes": [
  "Label shift and covariate shift are confounded, and EM cannot tell them apart. Alexandari's Lemma A guarantees EM converges to the source priors only 'in the absence of domain shift'; F4 establishes a whole-distribution radiometric shift, i.e. p(x|y) moved. EM will happily absorb that p(x|y) change into a fabricated prevalence change q(y). The panel anchor is the only thing preventing this, which means the design is anchor-limited, not EM-limited -- and the anchor is 150 points.",
  "The greenness covariate is collinear with DOY and with program. NAIP is always summer; city flights run Feb-Mar leaf-off. F4 says the gradient is radiometric and not phenological, but the DESIGN MATRIX may be unable to separate them across only 37 deliveries. The covariate could then absorb a real seasonal visibility difference and be reported as bias, or vice versa. The crown-projection-regardless-of-foliage reference definition mitigates but does not eliminate this.",
  "A two-parameter affine correction on the logit is a GLOBAL prevalence fix that can be right on average and wrong at every edge. GSD spans 5 cm to 1 m -- a 20x range that changes what 'canopy' means in a mixed pixel -- and F6 puts verified losses at a median 2.0 m from a crown edge. G-COPT can hit the citywide number while systematically mislocating the very edge pixels where the real signal lives, which would corrupt the map product and the patch statistics even when the trend is right.",
  "Per-date levels stay noisy. At 150 panel points and ~30% prevalence the per-date panel SE is ~3.7 pp, far larger than the 2 pp/8 yr signal. Only the paired and pooled contrasts are sharp, because the same points are used at every date. Any consumer wanting a defensible standalone estimate for a single year will not get one from the panel; they get it from EM, which inherits whatever the covariate model got wrong.",
  "The oldest deliveries may have no usable posterior at all. A 2020-trained RGB detector applied to a 2005 5 cm delivery with different tone curve and possibly near-panchromatic character can produce a collapsed, low-variance logit distribution. No affine map rescues a collapsed distribution, and the honest outcome for some deliveries is 'no estimate,' which will thin the early end of the series exactly where the trend baseline is set.",
  "Interpreter cost overrun. 22 s per point-date is optimistic for 37 dates spanning 5 cm to 1 m and Feb leaf-off; at 30-35 s the panel alone reaches 50 h and the budget breaks 'a few human-days.' The fallback -- cutting to ~120 points -- widens the panel SE and weakens the bias-correction step, and the secondary kill thresholds would have to be relaxed accordingly.",
  "The anchor shares the detector's bias (see weakest point). This is the failure mode that does not announce itself: it produces tighter CIs and a smoother series, so it looks like success."
 ],
 "sources": [
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\alexandari_bcts.txt",
   "page": "5",
   "claim": "The core calibrate-then-estimate-prevalence procedure of steps 4b/5/6: calibrate on a held-out split with a strong calibration algorithm, set the source prior to the MEAN CALIBRATED POSTERIOR on that split (not to counted labels), then maximize the concave label-shift likelihood on the target domain.",
   "quote": "1. Given a model f that outputs predicted probabilities, calibrated the predictions of f on a held-out validation using an appropriately strong calibration algorithm. In this work, we observed that Bias-Corrected Temperature Scaling and Vector Scaling work well. 2. Average the calibrated predictions p^(y = i|x) over this held-out validation set to obtain the estimated sourcedomain class priors p^(y = i)."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\alexandari_bcts.txt",
   "page": "3",
   "claim": "Justifies the two-parameter affine logit form a_d*l(x)+b_d as exactly BCTS in the binary canopy/non-canopy case, and justifies preferring a bias-corrected form over plain temperature scaling because plain TS leaves systematic bias that corrupts prevalence estimation.",
   "quote": "The second, which we refer to as Bias-Corrected Temperature Scaling, is equivalent TS Scaling but with the addition of the class-specific bias terms from VS. As with TS and VS, the parameters are optimized to minimize the NLL on the validation set. Note that in the case of binary classification, the parameterization of BCTS reduces to Platt Scaling (Platt, 1999)."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\alexandari_bcts.txt",
   "page": "4",
   "claim": "THE reason EM/label-shift alone is insufficient under F4 and why diagnostic D1(b) is expected to fail: the convergence guarantee is conditioned on there being no domain shift. F4 establishes a whole-distribution radiometric shift, i.e. p(x|y) has moved, so EM alone will misattribute it to prevalence.",
   "quote": "Lemma A: In the absence of domain shift and in the limit of sufficient data, the EM algorithm will converge to the original priors p^(y = i) if and only if p^(y = i) := Ex~p(x)[p^(y = i|x)]."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\undoing_label_shift_seg.txt",
   "page": "3",
   "claim": "Establishes that the same posterior-rescaling correction is valid for SEGMENTATION (per-pixel), not just whole-image classification -- and states the precondition (aligned conditional distribution) that F4 violates, which is why G-COPT adds a covariate instead of applying the correction naively.",
   "quote": "Then, we further assume that the conditional data distribution P (X|Y ) is well aligned, i.e., we have Ps(F (x) | Y ) = Pt(F (x) | Y ). Using Eq. (2) and Eq. (3), the relation of Cs and Ct can be derived as: C_{t}(F(x)) \\propto \\frac {C_{s}(F(x)) P_{t}(Y)}{P_{s}(Y)}."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\undoing_label_shift_seg.txt",
   "page": "5",
   "claim": "Supports doing the correction at INFERENCE time on a frozen model rather than retraining per delivery -- essential to the ~2 GPU-day budget and to keeping one frozen detector across all 37 deliveries.",
   "quote": "Inference Adjustment (IA): An alternative idea is that we directly adjust the output of the existing network Gcda in the inference stage on the target domain. ... In CDSS, both CR and IA are from the perspective of the classifier to correct the problem caused by domain shift. ... Both strategies are essentially the same in theory, and one can choose either way in combination with the data distribution alignment."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\boreal_calibrate_hist_aerial.txt",
   "page": "7",
   "claim": "Direct precedent for the whole calibration-transfer move on heterogeneous historical aerial photos: calibrate SEPARATELY BY VINTAGE because bias depends on photo quality, and add a measured covariate to the calibration model when it improves fit. G-COPT's only change is to make the vintage effect a pooled regression on covariates so the 34 unlabeled deliveries inherit parameters.",
   "quote": "The relationship between the reconstructed and interpreted canopy cover depended on the aerial photo quality, and on the studied landscape. Because of this dependency, we calibrated the interpretations from different years separately ... the calibration model for the newest photographs in the Finnish landscapes improved when we included the proportion of P. abies in the cell as a predictor (Table A2). Hence, we included the proportion of P. abies in this calibration model."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\boreal_calibrate_hist_aerial.txt",
   "page": "8",
   "claim": "The credible-change threshold used in step 10: draw from posterior predictive distributions at each date, difference them, and flag change only where 99% of draws share a sign. This is a magnitude/uncertainty filter on the MAP, computed per-cell from the fitted error model -- not a temporal prior.",
   "quote": "The cells where the proportion of samples with positive or negative change exceeded a threshold of 99% were flagged as credibly positive or negative, and the mean canopy cover change for cells with the lowest credible positive and highest credible negative canopy cover change were used as the threshold values."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\boreal_calibrate_hist_aerial.txt",
   "page": "1",
   "claim": "THE WEAKEST POINT, sourced. Human interpretation bias is itself a function of photo quality and runs in OPPOSITE directions for old and new imagery -- exactly the confound between our panel anchor and our detector across a 20-year, 5 cm-1 m archive.",
   "quote": "We underestimated canopy cover using the historical low-quality photographs, and overestimated it using the recent high-quality photographs. Further, due to differences in tree species composition and canopy cover in the cells, the interpretation bias varied between the landscapes."
  },
  {

 "novelty_vs_known_families": "Against TEMPORAL FILTERING / HMM (Abercrombie & Friedl, LandTrendr/TimeSync, Zhu 2017): G-COPT touches no temporal neighbourhood at all -- per-date independence is a hard structural constraint, not a tuning choice. That is precisely why it survives F5 (naive smoothing moved the estimate away from truth) and F6 (signal and noise share an address). Against CALIBRATED TIME-SERIES ALGORITHMS: those presuppose a radiometrically consistent stack, which F2 and the archive's five delivery programs make unavailable; G-COPT requires no such stack because it never compares pixel values across dates. Against TRAJECTORIES: trajectory viewing is used here only for human interpretation throughput, and the 15% blind randomized single-date re-interpretation exists specifically to detect whether trajectory context leaked into the reference -- the opposite of using trajectories as an estimator. Against METHOD-HOLDING / HARMONIZATION (DeepHarmony, HACA3, CALAMITI, Mboga's DANN/D-CORAL, IR-MAD/PIF, histogram matching): every one of those harmonizes IMAGES or FEATURES. G-COPT harmonizes only the DECISION BOUNDARY, via scalar delivery covariates, and its primary greenness covariate is computed from the frozen model's own logit histogram, so no radiometry is touched. The genuinely new move is importing DeepHarmony's paired-overlap \"traveling subject\" logic but instantiating it on CALIBRATION PARAMETERS rather than intensities -- and finding the traveling subjects already inside the archive: F3's 2017 triple and F7's same-flight pair have true delta exactly zero, converting the two facts that most damage the naive pipeline into free supervision and a free kill test. Against SAMPLE-BASED CHANGE ESTIMATION (Olofsson, Stehman, McRoberts, Pawlak): Pawlak is the closest published design -- a 2020-trained model applied to other years with per-year error-adjusted estimation -- but it fixes each year independently after the fact and leaves the operating point at a single visually chosen 0.65 for all years, doing nothing about the detection-time inconsistency F2 shows is baked in. G-COPT's deltas are (i) calibration parameters POOLED across deliveries through covariates, so unlabeled deliveries inherit an operating point instead of needing their own sample; (ii) replicate-delivery constraints; and (iii) an ~8x cheaper reference budget per epoch -- Pawlak spends ~1,200 point-years per epoch (4,800 validations / 4 epochs), G-COPT ~150. Against KULHA, the closest ancestor: Kulha calibrates HUMAN INTERPRETATION against tree rings, per vintage, independently, with no mechanism to give an uncalibrated vintage parameters. G-COPT calibrates a MODEL POSTERIOR and makes the vintage effect a regression on measurable covariates, which is what lets 3 anchored dates plus 150 panel points cover 37 deliveries. Against CAI 2025: same instinct -- per-year thresholds -- but executed by hand and by eye; G-COPT replaces manual adjustment with an estimated, uncertainty-carrying, cross-fitted operating point that publishes a ledger and can be killed by a number.",
 "cost_estimate": "GPU: ~0.5 GPU-day to train one frozen detector on the 2020 labels; ~1 GPU-day to infer 37 deliveries x ~278 Mpx (25 km2 at 30 cm working GSD). Under 2 GPU-days total, with NO per-year retraining ever. The decisive economy is that logits are computed once and cached, so the hierarchical Bayesian fit, the 5-fold cross-fitting, EM over all deliveries, every LODO refit and every threshold sweep run on cached arrays at CPU-minutes -- the kill test costs no GPU time at all. HUMAN: 5,550 panel point-date interpretations at ~22 s in a trajectory viewer = ~34 h; 15% blind single-date re-interpretation = ~6 h; 150-point change booster at 2 dates = ~2 h; model fitting, diagnostics and write-up = ~1.5 days. Total ~6.5-7 person-days, one-time. Honest tension: 7 sits at the upper edge of \"a few human-days,\" and if interpretation runs at 30-35 s per point-date rather than 22 the panel alone reaches ~50 h. The defense is the marginal cost: each additional future delivery costs ~0 human-days (compute its covariates, predict a_d/b_d, infer, cut), versus a fresh Olofsson-style sample per year. Fallback if the budget is hard-capped at ~4 person-days: cut the panel to ~120 points, accept a wider per-date SE, and relax the secondary kill thresholds accordingly. No bespoke sensors, no new acquisitions, no field work.",
 "self_assessed_weakest_point": "The anchor has the same disease as the detector, and the failure is silent. Kulha calibrated visual canopy interpretation against tree-ring reconstructions and found that the INTERPRETER'S OWN BIAS varied with photo quality in opposite directions at the two ends of the series -- underestimating canopy on old low-quality photographs and overestimating it on recent high-quality ones. G-COPT's 150-point panel is interpreted on exactly the same heterogeneous 5 cm-1 m, Feb-Oct, five-program imagery whose detector bias it is supposed to measure. If the panel's delivery-dependent interpretation bias tracks G_d with the same sign as the detector's radiometric bias -- which F4's greenness gradient makes plausible, since what makes a delivery look greener to a CNN also makes crowns easier for a human to call -- then b_d absorbs both and the two are not separately identifiable. Cross-fitting does not help: it corrects for reusing the panel, not for the panel being wrong in a delivery-correlated way. The replicate-consistency penalty does not help either, because the 2017 triple and the F7 pair share acquisition conditions, so a bias driven by acquisition is invisible within a replicate set. The lidar audit at 2005 and 2016 is the only partly independent check and it covers 2 of 37 deliveries, neither near the 2024 end where the trend claim is most contested. The consequence is the worst kind: this failure makes the series look BETTER -- smoother, with tighter CIs and a clean covariate coefficient -- while the whole curve is tilted. It would pass the primary and secondary kill tests, which measure within-date replicate consistency, and would only be caught by the tertiary sign test against the held-out 1,250 paired points, which itself rests on human interpretation of the same archive. If I could buy one thing, it would be a small set of field- or lidar-verified crowns spread across more than two epochs, purely to break this confound."
}

 "design_name": "G-COPT: Greenness-Conditioned Operating-Point Transfer",

 "total": 16,
 "fatal_flaw": "What the estimator actually identifies is not canopy prevalence but \"panel prevalence as seen by a delivery-dependent human interpreter.\" Every one of the 37 deliveries receives both the 150-point panel AND a per-delivery random effect in step 5; the random effect is identified only by that date's panel points, so b_d is fit to make the calibrated posterior reproduce the panel, EM then refines toward it, and step 7 bias-corrects against the same panel. Cross-fitting fixes the in-sample-ness of the bias estimate but creates no new information: mu_d collapses to a model-assisted version of the panel estimate. Two consequences. (i) Kulha (verified, boreal p.1) shows interpreter bias is itself a function of photo quality and runs in OPPOSITE directions at the old and new ends of a series. If the panel's delivery-dependent interpretation bias tracks greenness/quality with the same sign as the detector's F4 drift — plausible, since what makes a delivery look greener to a CNN makes crowns easier for a human to call — then b_d absorbs both and they are not separately identifiable. Deliverable 5, \"the fitted greenness coefficient ... a direct publishable answer to whether the drift is capturable by a scalar covariate,\" is therefore a mixture of detector drift and interpreter drift and is uninterpretable as stated. The 2005/2016 lidar audit covers 2 of 37 deliveries and neither is near the contested 2024 end. (ii) No kill test bites on the trend. The primary LODO test validates covariate-only transfer of (a_d,b_d) — a component that is NEVER used in the final mu_d, because every delivery keeps its panel-fit random effect. And its 2.6 pp threshold is 2.4x looser than the 1.07 pp F3 already achieves under plain reference-matched cuts, so passing it does not demonstrate improvement over \"reference-match each delivery with the panel you are building anyway.\" That leaves the tertiary test, which by the design's own numbers is underpowered: a 150-point paired panel gives roughly +/-4 pp on the 8-year contrast against the authority's +/-1.10, so CI overlap is near-automatic and sign agreement passes ~84% of the time even if the entire calibration apparatus contributes nothing. The design self-names the anchor confound as its weakest point; it does not cure it, and it does not notice that the confound plus the LODO/final-estimator mismatch together mean the headline scientific claim is untestable on this data.",
 "verdict": "CLEARS the Condition-B checklist on all six hard constraints (16/18), and is the most operationally concrete design in this batch — cached logits, explicit ordering, explicit costs, a stated fallback, and a numeric kill criterion with a genuine anti-vacuity clause. But it is a map-making method whose trend estimate is a 150-point panel estimate wearing a map: the deep-learning-plus-calibration-plus-EM apparatus is variance reduction on the panel, and its variance gain depends on the map's residual correlation with truth, which F1/F5/F7 suggest is weak precisely for change. Sell it as (a) the operating-point ledger and 37 reference-matched rasters, which are real and currently missing, and (b) a per-date series from a fixed panel — not as the calibration machinery identifying the trend. Three unaddressed items beyond the fatal flaw. First, step 1's deliberate \"minimal photometric augmentation\" (to keep F4's drift visible in the logits) raises the probability of collapsed, low-variance logit distributions on far-domain deliveries; the design recreates F3's near-binary problem inside its own detector and concedes it only in the failure-mode list, where \"no estimate\" for early deliveries thins exactly the end that sets the trend baseline. Second, the 15% blind single-date QC measures reference-side stability-inheritance but carries no decision rule if disagreement is high — it reports a number and proceeds. Third, geometric co-registration of 37 heterogeneous 5 cm-1 m orthos is never mentioned, yet a fixed same-points panel is the load-bearing component and F6 puts verified losses a median 2.0 m from a crown edge; F8 says the existing paired-point protocol already controls drift and capture, so the team knows this matters. On scales: 6.5-7 person-days is stated as at the edge of \"a few,\" and at the design's own 30-35 s/point-date it reaches ~9-10, with the 37-date trajectory viewer and ortho prep uncounted. On novelty: competent composition of BCTS+EM label shift, McRoberts model-assisted estimation, Ruetschi threshold-moving, Pengra QC and Chesapeake change stratification; one genuinely fresh hook (below), the rest derivative.",
 "best_idea_to_graft": "The in-archive zero-delta traveling subjects, plus the kill-test structure built on them. F3's 2017 triple and F7's same-flight pair are replicates whose true delta is exactly zero, already sitting in the archive at no cost — they convert the two facts that most damage the naive pipeline into free supervision (a replicate-consistency penalty) and a free, GPU-free kill test. Graft three things together: (1) the replicate penalty; (2) the anti-vacuity clause structure — the design must beat a MEASURED baseline by a stated margin, not merely clear a floor, since any identity map clears a threshold set above the noise floor; and (3) the ordering discipline of calibrate -> estimate prevalence -> derive the cut as the quantile reproducing it, never cut-then-count-pixels (Ruetschi threshold-moving to MBE~0, McRoberts p.5 on why 0.51->0.49 must not be scored like 0.99->0.01). Also worth taking: the F7 PRE-TEST, which asks whether the covariates can even distinguish the replicate pair before treating it as a test — a discipline most designs skip. One correction when grafting: benchmark the replicate spread against F3's already-achieved 1.07 pp reference-matched figure, not against a fixed-0.5 identity baseline, or the bar is set below what the archive already demonstrates.",
 "source_check": "Read and verified all 24 quotes verbatim in the cited files, page/section context matching. alexandari_bcts.txt: the two-step calibrate-then-average-posteriors recipe (line 570), BCTS-reduces-to-Platt (line 306), Lemma A \"In the absence of domain shift\" (line 317) - all exact, and the design's use of Lemma A to predict that its own D1(b) baseline will fail is a correct reading. undoing_label_shift_seg.txt: P(X|Y) well-aligned precondition (line 82) and Inference Adjustment (line 184) - exact. boreal_calibrate_hist_aerial.txt: per-vintage calibration (line 183), P. abies covariate (line 184), 99% credible-change (line 198), and the opposite-direction interpreter bias in the abstract (line 13) - all exact; the design correctly cites the last against itself. r2_19_ruetschi.txt: both quotes exact (lines 19, 254). r2_29_mcroberts2014.txt: model-assisted regression estimator (line 235), 1.5-2.0x covariance benefit (line 814), 0.51/0.49 vs 0.99/0.01 (line 680) - exact. r2_24_pengra.txt: 10% duplicate subsample and the 2.3x SE understatement (lines 44, 41) - exact. pawlak2026_california_utf.txt: the 0.65 visually-chosen threshold (line 442) and 4,800 validations (line 611) - exact. cai2025_orlando_biennial.txt: manual threshold adjustment (line 37) and 2017 NIR saturation (line 128) - exact. roman2021_land_phila.txt: 3.2 pp detectable change (line 258) and 91.5% / 1.3 pp interpreter agreement (line 242) - exact. chesapeake_change_accuracy_methods.txt: pages 10 and 12 - exact including the Tobler-adjacent omission check. TWO GLOSSES on the record, neither score-changing. (1) Ruetschi: the MAE 6.04% -> 2.68% reduction comes from BOTH bias-correction techniques combined - active learning to generate more training data AND threshold-moving object growing (\"two techniques ... were tested,\" abstract line 19) - but the design's operating_point justification attributes the reduction to threshold-moving alone. (2) Cai's 2017 fix: the source (line 128) says the post-classification correction \"ensured alignment with the classifications of historical and subsequent years,\" i.e. cross-year consistency enforcement - inheritance-flavored, closer to what this design forbids - whereas the design characterizes it as \"a per-delivery decision-level correction anchored on stable features,\" the manual analogue of its own bimodality covariate. The quote itself is accurate; the interpretive gloss is not."
}

 "design_name": "G-COPT: Greenness-Conditioned Operating-Point Transfer",
 "scores": {
  "no_radiometric_calibration": 2,
