 },
 "total": 17,
 "fatal_flaw": "Not fatal, but the largest unaddressed delivery risk is PANEL CO-REGISTRATION DRIFT, and it is nowhere in the design. Step 7's whole variance argument (McRoberts 1.5-2.0x SE reduction, quote verified at r2_29_mcroberts2014.txt line 814) rests on \"measurement of the same set of plots\" at every date. F8 explicitly states the 1,250 paired points had to CONTROL FOR DRIFT in this archive, so multi-vendor georeferencing drift is a measured property of these 37 deliveries, not a hypothetical. Typical multi-vendor ortho drift of 1-3 m sits directly on top of F6's median 2.0 m loss-to-crown-edge distance: a panel point near a crown edge flips label from drift alone, at exactly the spatial scale of the real signal. And drift is correlated with vendor, program and GSD -- the very covariates b_d is regressed on -- so drift-induced panel disagreement is absorbed into the fitted \"delivery bias\" coefficient and is not separately identifiable from it. The design budgets zero hours for per-delivery co-registration or per-point drift adjudication and never states a tolerance. Second, smaller unaddressed item in the same family: \"obscured\" is a declared panel category but the design never says how it enters the likelihood. Under a crown-projection-regardless-of-foliage definition, obscured/uncertain calls will concentrate in Feb leaf-off and 1 m GSD deliveries -- non-random missingness correlated with DOY and log-GSD, again the fitted covariates. Casewise-deleting those point-dates biases b_d for precisely the deliveries the covariate model most needs to get right. Both are fixable with stated protocol, which is why this is a deduction and not a kill: F8 proves human interpreters in this archive can control for drift when told to.",
 "best_idea_to_graft": "Treat the archive's own replicate deliveries as internal \"traveling subjects\" with a TRUE DELTA OF EXACTLY ZERO, and use them twice: as free supervision (a replicate-consistency penalty forcing F3's three 2017 deliveries to a common prevalence) and as the primary kill test (leave one 2017 delivery out of the fit entirely, predict its operating point from covariates alone, and require the triple's prevalence spread to fall under 2.6 pp = 2x F7's 1.3 pp processing-chain floor, AND to beat the measured identity baseline by >=50%). This move converts the two facts that most damage the naive pipeline -- F3's 10.1 pp same-year spread and F7's 1.3 pp same-flight divergence -- into zero-cost supervision and a zero-GPU-cost falsification test, and it is fully portable: any competing design, whatever its mechanism, can be LODO-tested on the 2017 triple. The paired sub-move worth grafting alongside it is the VACUITY PRE-TEST: before running the kill test, check that the candidate's covariates actually differ across the F7 pair; if they do not, F7 kills every possible design and must be demoted to floor confirmation rather than counted as a test. That discipline should be a requirement on every entrant.",
 "source_check": "I opened every cited file and grepped the exact quoted strings. All 22 quotes are verbatim-accurate and correctly support their stated claims. Verified: alexandari_bcts.txt lines 306 (BCTS = TS + class bias terms, \"reduces to Platt Scaling\"), 317 (Lemma A, \"In the absence of domain shift...\"), 568/570 (calibrate-on-holdout then average calibrated predictions for source priors); undoing_label_shift_seg.txt line 82 (\"conditional data distribution P(X|Y) is well aligned\") and 184 (Inference Adjustment on a frozen net); boreal_calibrate_hist_aerial.txt lines 183/184 (per-vintage calibration + P. abies covariate added when it improved AICc), 198 (99% posterior-draw credible-change flag), 13 (underestimated on old low-quality photos, overestimated on recent high-quality); r2_19_ruetschi.txt lines 19 (MAE 6.04% -> 2.68%, MBE ~0) and 254 (MBE closest to 0 at 5% vs 10% depending on population); r2_29_mcroberts2014.txt lines 217/235 (systematic classification error bias + model-assisted regression estimator as initial estimator minus expected bias), 680 (0.99->0.01 vs 0.51->0.49 both counted as change), 814 (positive cross-date covariance, 1.5x/2.0x SE inflation if dropped); r2_24_pengra.txt lines 88 (10% probability subsample for duplicate interpretation) and 41 (McRoberts et al. 2018, SE underestimated by factor 2.3); pawlak2026_california_utf.txt lines 442 (0.65 threshold by visual inspection), 592/611 (1,200+500 points, 4,800 validations); cai2025_orlando_biennial.txt lines 37 (manual threshold adjustment necessary) and 128 (2017 NAIP NIR saturation); roman2021_land_phila.txt lines 242 (91.5% interpreter agreement, 1.3 pp average UTC difference) and 258 (3.2 pp detectable change); chesapeake_change_accuracy_methods.txt lines 91/124/148 (multiple change strata, 3x3 rejection window, change-omission check by distance to change patch). Two minor characterizations, neither a misquote: (a) the design describes Cai's 2017 fix as \"anchored on stable features,\" but the quoted passage says only \"spatial noise removal and consistency checks\" -- mild overreach in the framing sentence, the quote itself is exact; (b) Pengra's paper specifies a 10% probability subsample while the design uses 15%, which the design presents as adopting Pengra's design rather than his number. Source fidelity scores 2.",
 "verdict": "STRONG. 17/20 (I score nine items, max 18; 17 of 18). This is the most operationally honest Condition-B design I would expect to see, and it is the one I would actually try to deliver. It clears the adversarial checklist cleanly on the four hard constraints. NO RADIOMETRIC CALIBRATION (2): nothing in the pipeline transforms a pixel value; the primary greenness covariate G_post is a shape statistic of the FROZEN MODEL'S OWN LOGIT HISTOGRAM, which contains no radiometry at all, and the optional G_img enters as a regressor whose coefficient is fitted, so an arbitrary monotone error in it is absorbed. F2 is correctly deployed as the positive argument -- grid harmonization moved fractions <0.1 pp because inconsistency is baked in at detection time, so the intervention belongs at the decision. NO EXTRA LIDAR (2): 2005/2016 appear only as a held-out audit at panel points; delete them and every number still computes. OPERATING POINT (2): this is the design rather than an addendum, it never ingests a vendor probability raster (correctly handling F3's near-binary/miscalibrated deliveries), it derives the cut LAST as the quantile reproducing an independently estimated prevalence, and it ships the per-delivery operating-point ledger as a first-class product -- which is precisely the audit trail F3 shows the archive lacks. NOT SMOOTHING / NOT INHERITANCE (2): the constraint is structural, not a tuning choice -- no step reads a pixel's value, label or posterior at another date. The single most important detail is that step 10's credible-change LoD is applied to the MAP PRODUCT ONLY while mu_d and the trend bypass it, so F6's dispersed sub-LoD edge loss still enters the reported number. The 15% blind randomized SINGLE-DATE re-interpretation is the right guard against the trajectory viewer importing inheritance into the reference itself, and few designs think of that. KILL CRITERION (2): best-in-class. It states a number (2.6 pp on the LODO 2017 triple), adds the essential must-beat-baseline-by-50% clause that stops an identity map from trivially passing, measures the identity baseline S_id rather than assuming it, includes a vacuity pre-test on F7, and ends with a sign test against the held-out 1,250 points. Nothing else here is close. NOVELTY (2), and I can defend the discriminator in one sentence: Kulha calibrates per vintage but has NO MECHANISM to hand an unlabeled vintage its parameters, while Pawlak fixes one visually chosen 0.65 across all years, Cai hand-tunes per year, and Ruetschi moves one global threshold -- G-COPT's specific new move is pooling the calibration parameters through measurable delivery covariates so 34 UNLABELED deliveries inherit an operating point, supervised by replicates already inside the archive whose true delta is zero. IMPLEMENTABILITY (2): every component is off-the-shelf (one segmentation model, cached logits, a hierarchical Bayesian logistic fit, Saerens EM, a quantile cut), and the decisive engineering choice -- cache logits once, never masks -- means the hierarchical fit, 5-fold cross-fitting, every LODO refit and every threshold sweep cost CPU-minutes, so the kill test is free. The 5-fold cross-fitting is not decoration: without it the step-7 bias estimate is ~0 by construction and its SE is understated, and the design says so itself. SOURCE FIDELITY (2): all 22 quotes verified verbatim; see source_check. The one deduction is SCALES (1). GPU is fine and probably conservative -- ~2 GPU-days for 37 x 278 Mpx with no retraining is right. Human is not. The design itself concedes 6.5-7 person-days is \"at the upper edge of a few,\" and that estimate omits real delivery line items a practitioner will pay for: building or configuring a 37-date trajectory viewer, resampling and tiling 37 heterogeneous deliveries (a 25 km2 city at 5 cm is ~10 Gpx per source delivery) to a 30 cm working grid, and -- the big one -- per-delivery co-registration so the panel's 150 points land on the same ground at all 37 dates. The 22 s/point-date rate is optimistic for a Feb leaf-off 1 m GSD frame under a crown-projection-regardless-of-foliage rule; at 30-35 s the panel alone is ~50 h. Realistic all-in is 10-15 person-days, not \"a few.\" The design does supply an honest fallback (cut to ~120 points, widen SEs, relax secondary thresholds), which is why this is a 1 and not a 0. Two further things I would require before delivery, neither disqualifying. First, state a co-registration tolerance and a per-point drift adjudication rule (see fatal_flaw) -- it is the difference between the McRoberts covariance gain being real and being an artifact. Second, specify how \"obscured\" enters the likelihood; it is non-random missingness correlated with DOY and GSD, and casewise deletion will bias b_d for the hardest deliveries. I would also push back gently on the design's own self-assessed weakest point: it is correctly identified and correctly described as silent, but it is an accuracy ceiling shared by every photo-interpretation-anchored design in this problem, including F8 itself, so it should not be read as disqualifying. Overall: ship it, with a co-registration protocol and a doubled human budget."
}

### JUDGMENT: {
 "design_name": "G-COPT: Greenness-Conditioned Operating-Point Transfer",
 "scores": {
  "no_radiometric_calibration": 2,
  "no_extra_lidar": 2,
  "operating_point": 2,
  "not_smoothing_not_inheritance": 2,
  "scales": 1,
  "kill_criterion": 2,
  "novelty": 2,
  "implementability": 1,
  "source_fidelity": 1
 },
 "total": 15,
 "source_check": "I read all 8 cited files and located all 22 quotes. VERBATIM ACCURACY IS EXCELLENT: 22/22 quotes present word-for-word (ligature/hyphenation artifacts aside). Page attributions correct in 21/22 against the files' own \"===== PAGE n =====\" markers; the sole miss is undoing_label_shift_seg p.5, where the first half (\"Inference Adjustment (IA)...\") is line 184 = page 4 and only the second half is page 5 — a cross-page concatenation, not a fabrication. Spot-verified: alexandari p.5 line 568, p.3 line 306, p.4 line 317; boreal(Kulha 2018) p.7 lines 183-184 (both halves of the ellipsis are on p.7), p.8 line 198, p.1 line 13; ruetschi p.1 line 19, p.7 line 254; mcroberts p.3 lines 217+235, p.7 line 814, p.5 line 680; pengra p.4 line 88, p.2 line 41; pawlak p.9 line 442, p.11-12 lines 592/611; cai p.2 line 37, p.4 line 128; roman p.8 line 258, p.7 line 242; chesapeake p.10 and p.12 (a Czawlytko/LUWG slide deck, Stehman named p.8 — the \"Stehman-advised\" attribution checks out).\n\nTHE FAILURES ARE ALL AT THE CLAIM LAYER, and the two worst are on the two LOAD-BEARING mechanism citations, not on precedent citations.\n\n(1) RUETSCHI — warrant for step 8, the design's central move. The design's operating_point checklist states \"Ruetschi's threshold-moving, which took MAE 6.04% to 2.68% at MBE~0 against a probability sample.\" Three problems in the source. (a) Line 19 (abstract) credits the 6.04→2.68 reduction to BOTH techniques — active learning generating additional training data AND object growing — and line 292 repeats \"Both contributed.\" AL almost certainly carries the bulk; G-COPT does no AL. (b) The mechanism is not a global quantile cut. Line 191: \"'no shrub forest' pixels with a low MU and IN THE VICINITY of predicted shrub forest objects were reassigned... vicinity... empirically set to 20 pixels (equal to 200 m).\" It is a spatially gated, one-directional object-growing reassignment that only counteracts underprediction (line 275: \"counteract an underprediction (MBE < 0)\"). Step 8's ungated bidirectional quantile is a different operation. (c) The authors disclaim it: line 277, \"it remained unclear whether the object growing step proved to be efficient in the case of the present study, as object growing only resulted in better performance metrics in the analysis incl. PWS.\" The design cites it as clean empirical precedent that this \"substantially reduces error.\" Compounding: the p.7 quote is truncated at \"a threshold of 10%\" — the very next words are \"with a corresponding MAE of 17.73%\" (vs 2.81% at the other operating point). That dropped number is the empirical demonstration of the design's OWN expected-failure-mode 3 (right on the citywide average, wrong everywhere locally), cut off mid-sentence.\n\n(2) ALEXANDARI p.5 does not warrant step 6. The verified quote is a source/target procedure: calibrate on a held-out SOURCE validation set, average the calibrated posteriors on THAT set to get the source prior, then run EM on a DISTINCT target. Step 5 fits (a_d,b_d) using delivery d's own panel labels; step 6 then runs EM on d. The split EM requires is collapsed, and the design never states what step 6's source prior is. If it is the mean calibrated posterior over d (the quote applied literally), Lemma A — verified at line 317 — makes EM a fixed point, i.e. a no-op. If it is 2020's prior, it is not the calibration set's prior and EM moves q_d for reasons unrelated to d's prevalence. Step 4b specifies the prior correctly for the BASELINE and then step 6 silently omits it for the actual method.\n\n(3) CAI p.4 is mischaracterized, and inverted. The design's claim: \"the fix is a per-delivery decision-level correction ANCHORED ON STABLE FEATURES — the manual analogue of G-COPT's bimodality covariate and replicate-consistency penalty.\" Line 128 actually says: \"a post-classification correction workflow was implemented, combining spatial noise removal and consistency checks. This adjustment ensured alignment with the classifications of historical and subsequent years.\" No stable/invariant features appear; and \"alignment with historical and subsequent years\" is temporal consistency enforcement — the stability-inheritance move the design elsewhere disqualifies. The p.2 claim similarly adds \"tuned by hand against invariant anchors\"; line 37 says thresholds come from unsupervised classification of randomly selected reference data plus manual adjustment on classification results. \"Invariant anchors\" is not in the source.\n\n(4) MCROBERTS p.7 is quoted accurately (line 814, 1.5x model-assisted / 2.0x model-based) but the claim stretches it to \"This is what makes 150 points sufficient for contrasts despite a ~3.7 pp per-date SE.\" The source establishes a variance-reduction factor for repeated FIELD PLOTS in an NFI, not a sufficiency result. Applied to the design's own numbers, 3.7 pp / 1.5–2.0 leaves ~1.9–2.5 pp SE on a paired contrast against a 2 pp/8 yr signal — the arithmetic does not close, and the source cannot be made to say it does.\n\n(5) PAWLAK p.11-12: the design reports \"~1,700 points validated once per year, 4,800 validations for 4 epochs (~1,200 point-years per epoch).\" 1,200 + 500 = 1,700 points x 4 epochs = 6,800, not 4,800. The tension originates in the source, but the design propagates both figures as fact and derives its headline \"~8x cheaper reference budget per epoch\" from the smaller one without flagging the inconsistency.\n\nCleanly and honestly used, by contrast: Kulha p.1 (the design nominates its OWN weakest point using a verified quote that cuts against it — interpretation bias runs in opposite directions at the two ends of a quality series; this is the strongest piece of sourcing in the design), Kulha p.7 and p.8, Pengra p.2 and p.4 (10% in source vs 15% in design, but explicitly framed as adopting the design pattern), McRoberts p.3 and p.5, Roman p.7 and p.8, Chesapeake p.10 and p.12, Alexandari p.3 and p.4.",
 "fatal_flaw": "The kill battery structurally cannot fail for the one error that would sink the trend. Every primary and secondary kill test is a WITHIN-DATE replicate test — the 2017 triple and the F7 same-flight pair — and the design itself concedes these \"share acquisition conditions, so a bias driven by acquisition is invisible within a replicate set.\" The estimand, however, is a 2 pp/8 yr across-decade slope carried entirely by b_d, which 150 panel points cannot pin per delivery (admitted ~3.7 pp per-date SE), so b_d is set by a covariate regression with roughly 10-15 parameters (G_post, bimodality, log GSD, a DOY spline, program, vendor) plus a per-delivery random effect fit on n=37 deliveries where program, vendor, season and GSD are heavily confounded — a near-saturated design matrix — and then \"refined\" by EM, whose only cited guarantee is void precisely under F4 and whose source prior the design never specifies (see source_check item 2). If the panel interpreter's delivery-dependent bias tracks the same covariate as the detector's — which Kulha p.1 verifies happens on exactly this kind of quality-heterogeneous series, and which F4's greenness gradient makes plausible — b_d absorbs both, the two are not separately identifiable, and the whole curve tilts. That failure passes the primary and secondary tests by construction. Only the tertiary sign test can see it, and that rests on human interpretation of the same archive. Compounding this: the change-booster C — the design's ONLY new input capable of seeing change, justified by the Chesapeake stratify-on-mapped-change warrant, costed at 2 human-hours and interpreted in step 3 — is then consumed by NO step and enters NO estimator or output. So the credible-change map and the patch statistics are validated against nothing that sees change, at exactly the crown edges where F6 places the real signal and where the design's own failure-mode 3 says a global affine correction will be wrong while the citywide number looks right. Net: G-COPT can pass its entire kill battery while being wrong in F1's direction, and the one instrument that might have caught it is inert.",
 "best_idea_to_graft": "The archive's own replicate deliveries as zero-delta \"traveling subjects\" for CALIBRATION PARAMETERS, with a vacuity pre-test gating the kill test. F3's 2017 triple and F7's same-flight pair have true delta exactly zero, converting the two facts that most damage the naive pipeline into free supervision (a replicate-consistency penalty) and a free, no-new-data kill test (leave-one-delivery-out, predict that delivery's operating point from covariates alone, measure the residual spread). The transplantable discipline is the pre-test that precedes it: ask first whether F7's covariates are even distinguishable across the pair, because if they are not, no covariate model could ever explain the 1.3 pp, F7 would kill every possible design, and it must demote from test to floor confirmation. Any winning design should adopt both the zero-delta replicate supervision and that \"is this test vacuous?\" gate — with the correction that a replicate test must be paired with at least one ACROSS-EPOCH anchor, since replicates alone are blind to the tilt that matters. Second graft, nearly as good: step 10's strict separation of the limit-of-detection filter — applied to the published MAP PRODUCT only, never to mu_d or the trend — so dispersed sub-LoD loss still enters the reported number even when it is not drawn. That cleanly dissolves the usual tension between an honest map and an honest estimate, and it is cheap to adopt anywhere.",
 "verdict": "Strong on the adversarial checklist's hard constraints, weak exactly where it is load-bearing. G-COPT clears no_radiometric_calibration, no_extra_lidar, operating_point and not_smoothing_not_inheritance cleanly and non-trivially: it never touches a pixel value (its primary greenness covariate is a shape statistic of the frozen model's own logit histogram), it uses lidar only as a deletable audit at the two epochs that exist, it makes per-delivery operating-point estimation the whole method rather than an addendum, and it enforces per-date independence as a hard structural rule so F5's flicker is never bought off by suppressing transitions. The kill criterion scores 2 on the rubric's terms — statable, numeric, pre-tested for vacuity, with an identity-baseline clause that blocks a do-nothing map from trivially passing — but note the clause that drives the verdict: it is well-built and it tests the wrong quantity, since within-date replicates cannot fail for a smooth delivery-correlated tilt across two decades. Scales takes a 1 because 6.5-7 person-days on a self-admittedly optimistic 22 s/point-date is over a plain reading of \"a few human-days\" and the honest admission does not convert to a pass. Implementability takes a 1 for the dangling change-booster and a covariate model with a DOY spline plus two multi-level factors plus a per-delivery random effect fit on n=37 deliveries, which is not identifiable as specified at the exact point the kill test lives. Source_fidelity takes a 1, not a 0: 22/22 verbatim with correct pages is genuinely better sourcing than most, and the design deserves real credit for nominating its own weakest point with a Kulha quote that cuts against it — but the two mechanism citations both fail. Ruetschi's threshold-moving is a spatially gated 200 m one-directional object-growing step whose benefit the authors explicitly call unclear and whose error reduction is jointly credited with active learning, and the quote is truncated one clause before the MAE=17.73% that demonstrates the design's own worst failure mode; Alexandari p.5 describes a source/target split that step 6 collapses, leaving EM either a no-op under the very Lemma A the design verified or an unanchored mover. Not a winner as written, but the replicate-as-traveling-subject idea and the LoD-on-map-never-on-estimate separation are the two best transplantable pieces I have seen in this round and belong in whatever design wins."
}

## DESIGN: {
 "name": "CE-BSI: Crown-Edge Boundary-Shift Integral (change-first canopy trend)",
 "angle": "edge-aware-change-first",
 "one_paragraph": "Stop making per-year maps and differencing them; make the change itself the estimand. For each date pair, a Siamese network consumes the two raw orthophotos and regresses a per-pixel signed canopy change fraction delta in [-1,+1], with its output masked to a crown-edge band — the direct transplant of DeepBVC, which regresses voxel-wise brain-boundary shift from a baseline/follow-up pair and masks the output to boundary locations only, and which beat the classical algorithm by an order of magnitude under acquisition-protocol mismatch (resolution-anisotropy median error 2.8% vs 0.29%). Citywide change per pair is the boundary-shift integral: sum of delta over the band times pixel area. Because no per-date probability raster is ever thresholded inside the estimation path, F3's operating-point catastrophe (10.1 pp spread at delivered thresholds) simply has no surface to act on; the only threshold is the band locator, deliberately set at a recall-saturating cut and shown to leave the estimate flat. Zero-change is taught, not assumed: the four real null pairs we possess (F7's same-flight-two-ways pair, and the three pairwise combinations of the 2017 same-year deliveries) supply Delta_true = 0 supervision, which is observation, not the smoothness prior that moved the F5 pilot away from truth. The residual per-chain offset is then handled exactly as Hapke handles the proxy-datum bias in shoreline change: a bias that \"virtually always acts in the same direction\" and therefore \"is not canceled during averaging\" must be estimated per source, subtracted, and carried into an RSS uncertainty budget that yields a level of detection. The two lidar epochs are used only twice — to fit the slope of RGB-detected change against true change on the 2005-2016 pair, and to audit band completeness — never as truth, because lidar canopy is not RGB-visible canopy (Roman: 93.4% agreement, disagreement driven by the 2.4 m height cut; Coupland: equivalence only to +/-5.38% TCC). Bautin-style injected known change under randomized delivery-chain rendering supplies the training signal and the response curve. Redundant pairs (consecutive, skip-1, long) are then leveled by weighted least squares with per-chain bias unknowns, anchored at the 2020 hand labels, with no persistence state and no temporal prior; triple closure is reported as a diagnostic, not enforced. The delivered number is the raw CE-BSI estimate debiased by a McRoberts model-assisted regression estimator against the 1,250-point human panel.",
 "inputs": [
  "All 37 orthophoto acquisitions at native GSD (5 cm-1 m), radiometrically untouched. Each tagged with delivery program (5+ chains), nominal GSD, flight month, and leaf-state class (leaf-on / marginal / leaf-off) inferred from flight date alone.",
  "Date-pair set (~100 pairs), built for redundancy and closure, not for a chronological chain: all 36 consecutive pairs, all 35 skip-1 pairs, plus ~25 long pairs that must include 2016->2024 (the F1 sign-error case) and 2005<->2016 (the lidar-anchored pair). Every pair is estimated independently.",
  "The 4 REAL null pairs (Delta_true = 0) - the scarce resource of the whole design: (a) F7's single flight delivered two ways (1.3 pp apart, IoU 0.738 under a frozen model), and (b) the three pairwise combinations of the three same-year 2017 deliveries (F3). These are the only observations that can identify chain bias without simulation.",
  "2020 hand labels: three roles - (i) the level anchor for the network adjustment, (ii) the crown-polygon source for the simulation engine, (iii) the band-locator sanity check at one date.",
  "Lidar 2005 and 2016 only. CHM -> crown boundary polygons at two dates -> a real signed edge-displacement field for one pair. Used ONLY for response-slope fitting and band-completeness audit, never as a training target the network must reproduce. Also required: the RGB acquisition nearest each lidar date with matching leaf state, within ~1 year; if no such acquisition exists for an epoch, that slope anchor is dropped and every chain-pair slope becomes simulation-only (declare it).",
  "The 1,250-point human paired-point panel (F8) with each point's verified change status and its distance to the nearest crown edge (F6). Two uses, kept separate: (i) band-completeness geometry test - no model parameters fitted, so no leakage; (ii) blind validation of the raw CE-BSI 2016->2024 estimate, then leave-one-out model-assisted debiasing for the delivered product.",
  "No new imagery, no new lidar, no new sensor, no ground survey."
 ],
 "steps": [
  "1. PAIR PREPARATION (no radiometry). For each pair, dense-match hard man-made edges (roof ridges, pavement corners, painted lines) between the two members; fit a per-500-m-tile similarity + thin-plate warp; record residual RMSE_geo. Resample BOTH members to the coarser member's GSD - a pair is only as sharp as its coarser half - and record GSD_pair. Drop any pair with RMSE_geo > 0.5 x GSD_pair. Nothing is done to pixel values.",
  "2. BAND CONSTRUCTION. Run the existing in-house F1 segmenter as a LOCATOR ONLY (never as an estimator) at a recall-saturating cut (p >= 0.15) on both members, giving deliberately over-inclusive masks C_t and C_t1. Band B = dilate(boundary(C_t) UNION boundary(C_t1), R) with R = 8 m. Because the symmetric difference of two masks is bounded by the union of their boundaries, B is complete for every change region narrower than 2R. To catch wider ones (the 0.41 ha cluster is ~64 m across), add a coarse screen: on 16 m blocks, any block whose mean canopy fraction differs by >0.20 between members is dilated in full into B. Never use the delivered probability rasters - F3 found two of three near-binary/miscalibrated.",
  "3. SIMULATION ENGINE (Bautin's inject-known-change-plus-nuisance framework). From 2020 hand-labeled crowns and 2016 lidar crowns, build synthetic pairs: take a real tile as member A; produce member B by removing / eroding / dilating a KNOWN set of crowns by known amounts, filling with real co-located ground texture copy-pasted from adjacent non-canopy in the SAME image (deliberately not a generative inpainter, so the network cannot learn a paste signature that has no real-world counterpart). Then push each member independently through a randomized delivery-chain emulator: GSD resample across 5 cm-1 m, JPEG quantization, unsharp masking, per-channel gain/offset/gamma, a saturation-and-greenness shift spanning the F4 gradient, grayscale collapse (Nguyen's RGB-band-averaging augmentation), sun-elevation shadow lengthening, and residual misregistration up to the measured RMSE_geo. Delta_true per tile is exact by construction.",
  "4. TRAIN CE-BSI. Siamese encoder over the pair, single decoder head emitting per-pixel signed change fraction delta in [-1,+1], output MASKED to B (DeepBVC's boundary masking - non-band outputs forced to 0). Five loss terms: (a) MSE to injected delta on simulated pairs; (b) MSE to lidar-derived delta on the real 2005<->2016 pair, DOWN-WEIGHTED because lidar canopy is not RGB canopy; (c) null-pair loss penalizing |delta| on the 4 real null pairs and all simulated null pairs - supervision from pairs observed to be null, not a prior; (d) chain-consistency regularization (DeepBVC's L_reg): the same geometric pair rendered through two different chain emulations must yield the same delta; (e) antisymmetry, delta(A,B) = -delta(B,A), enforced by loss so the estimator has no sign preference.",
  "5. INTEGRATE. Delta_hat_ij = sum over p in B of delta_p x pixel_area, expressed in percentage points of city area. This is the boundary-shift integral; there is no map and no threshold in this number.",
  "6. RESPONSE-SLOPE CALIBRATION. Regress Delta_hat against Delta_true over injected magnitudes, per chain-pair and per GSD bin, to get slope s and intercept c; correct Delta_hat <- (Delta_hat - c)/s. Validate s on the one real anchor (2005<->2016 lidar). Publish the s table and flag every chain-pair whose s rests on simulation alone.",
  "7. CHAIN-BIAS MODEL (Hapke's proxy-datum bias, transplanted). Unknowns: b_k per delivery program, a continuous greenness covariate coefficient for the F4 radiometric gradient, and a leaf-state offset. Identifying observations: for a null pair (i,j), Delta_hat_ij = b_k - b_l + eps with Delta_true = 0. The 4 real null pairs plus F3's 10.1 pp -> 1.07 pp collapse supply these. This bias acts in one direction and does not cancel on averaging, so it is estimated and subtracted, with its posterior variance carried forward.",
  "8. UNCERTAINTY BUDGET per pair, root-sum-of-squares over independent terms (Hapke): sigma_ij = sqrt(sigma_geo^2 + sigma_GSD^2 + sigma_chain^2 + sigma_season^2 + sigma_model^2), sourced respectively from residual co-registration RMSE, the response curve at GSD_pair, the b_k posterior, the leaf-state stratum spread, and simulation replicate scatter. LoD_ij = 1.96 x sigma_ij. Pairs below LoD are reported as NOT RESOLVED - not as zero, and never as a flicker event.",
  "9. NETWORK ADJUSTMENT (leveling). Unknowns: 37 epoch levels C_t plus the bias terms. Observations: all ~100 Delta_hat_ij, weighted 1/sigma_ij^2. Level anchored at 2020 by the hand labels. NO zero-change prior, NO persistence state, NO temporal smoothness penalty - the only structure imposed is that levels are shared across pairs, which is an arithmetic identity, not a belief about the world. Output: the series with full covariance.",
  "10. CLOSURE DIAGNOSTIC (DeepBVC's one-step vs two-step test). For every triple, compare Delta_hat(t0->t2) against Delta_hat(t0->t1) + Delta_hat(t1->t2). Report mean and 1.96 SD of misclosure. Reported, never enforced - enforcing it would be exactly the smoothing this design refuses.",
  "11. SWAP/NET DECOMPOSITION (Pontius) on the sign of delta within B, per pair: gross gain, gross loss, net, swap. A pair whose swap dwarfs its net is flagged chain-noise-dominated. This is the direct replacement for the F5 flicker census: it says whether the noise is gone in a quantity that is meaningful rather than a transition count.",
  "12. VALIDATE, THEN DEBIAS. Blind test first: raw CE-BSI 2016->2024 net against the panel's -2.21 pp, CI +/-1.10, with no panel data having entered the estimator. Then deliver the model-assisted regression estimator (McRoberts): the initial CE-BSI estimator minus the expectation of its bias estimated from the panel, with variance from panel residuals, leave-one-out."
 ],
 "outputs": "A 37-epoch canopy-level series in percentage points of city area with full covariance, anchored at the 2020 hand labels. Per-pair signed change estimates with an explicit level of detection, where sub-LoD pairs are reported as NOT RESOLVED rather than as zero or as flicker. Per-delivery-chain bias estimates b_k with confidence intervals — the F4 ~1 pp quantity turned from a diagnostic into a measured, subtracted term. A wall-to-wall signed change-fraction raster within the edge band for every pair (dispersed edge losses and gains at crown-edge resolution, which sample-based panels cannot give). Per-pair swap/net/gross-gain/gross-loss decomposition replacing the flicker census. Triple-closure misclosure statistics as an internal consistency check needing no truth. The response-slope table s by chain-pair and GSD bin, flagged where simulation-only. Finally the delivered 2016→2024 net change and the full 20-year trend as model-assisted, panel-debiased estimates with CIs.",
 "checklist": {
  "no_radiometric_calibration": "Nothing maps one date's digital numbers onto another's — no histogram matching, no PIF, no IR-MAD, no relative normalization of any kind. The estimator consumes raw pair pixels. Radiometric difference is handled two ways, neither of which is calibration: (1) at train time as a nuisance the network is forced to be invariant to, via DeepBVC's consistency-regularization loss over two chain renderings of the same geometric pair — DeepBVC did exactly this against contrast and resolution mismatch and cut resolution-anisotropy error from a 2.8% median to 0.29%; (2) at adjustment time as a residual SCALAR per chain (b_k) plus one scalar greenness covariate for the F4 gradient, identified only from pairs observed to be null. A scalar nuisance regressor in a change model is not a cross-calibration of the archive: no image is altered, and if the b_k terms were all set to zero the pipeline still runs, just with wider CIs.",
  "no_extra_lidar": "Lidar enters at exactly 2005 and 2016 and does exactly two jobs: fit the slope of RGB-detected change against true change on one real pair, and audit whether the edge band contains the change lidar sees. It is deliberately NOT a training target the network must reproduce, because lidar canopy and RGB-visible canopy are different objects — Roman found 93.4% point agreement with disagreement driven by the 2.4 m height cut, and Coupland could only establish equivalence to ±5.38% TCC at 0.05 ha. If both lidar epochs were withheld the design degrades rather than breaks: every chain-pair slope becomes simulation-only, which is already the situation for most chain-pairs, and this is declared in the output. No new acquisition, no bespoke sensor.",
  "operating_point": "Orthogonal by construction in the estimation path, with the one residual dependency exposed and tested. There is no per-date probability raster and no per-date threshold anywhere in the number: the network's output is a signed change fraction regressed from the image pair, so F3's 10.1 pp spread at delivered thresholds has no surface to act on, and the delivered rasters (two of three near-binary/miscalibrated) are never used. The single threshold that remains is the BAND LOCATOR, and it is set at a recall-saturating cut (p ≥ 0.15) precisely so that it is over-inclusive: raising or lowering it changes the compute cost, not the estimand. We commit to demonstrating that the final citywide estimate is flat (drift ≤ 0.2 pp) across band cuts spanning the same delivered-threshold range that produced F3's 10.1 pp spread. F2 independently confirms the fix cannot be post-hoc — 2 m majority resampling moved fractions <0.1 pp — which is why this design intervenes at detection time, on the pair.",
  "not_smoothing_not_inheritance": "NOT SMOOTHING: there is no temporal filter, no HMM, no trajectory fit, no smoothness penalty, and no cross-epoch state anywhere. The only zero-change pressure in the whole design comes from four pairs where Δ_true actually is zero because they are the same flight or the same year — that is supervision from observation, not a prior over pairs whose true change is unknown. This is precisely why the F5 naive-smoothing pilot moved away from truth: it applied a rare-change prior to pairs whose real change was nonzero, and a 2 pp/8 yr dispersed signal is exactly what such a prior erases. The closure diagnostic (step 10) is reported, never enforced — enforcing it would reintroduce smoothing through the back door. NOT INHERITANCE: each pair is estimated from its own two images with nothing carried from the previous epoch, so no pixel is ever declared stable because it was stable before. The band is the union of BOTH dates' boundaries plus a coarse interior screen, so a whole crown that vanishes still has a t-epoch edge inside the band, a crown that appears in open ground still has a t+1 edge inside the band, and F6's dispersed losses (verified median 2.0 m from a crown edge) sit deep inside the measured domain rather than in an assumed-stable region. The network adjustment shares epoch levels across pairs, which is arithmetic, not persistence. Gate 1 makes the anti-inheritance claim falsifiable rather than rhetorical.",
  "scales": "25 km²; band ≈ 10–18% of area after step 2; ~100 pairs. Each pair works at its coarser member's GSD, so the expensive 5 cm dates are only ever processed at 5 cm when paired with each other. Inference over the band across ~100 pairs ≈ 0.5 GPU-day on one A100-class card; training, dominated by simulated-pair generation and the five-term loss, ≈ 1.5–2 GPU-days; total ≤ 3 GPU-days. Add ~2 CPU-days of preprocessing (co-registration and resampling), which is embarrassingly parallel and not on the GPU budget. Human: ~1 day chain/pair metadata and null-pair curation, ~1.5 days simulation realism QC, ~1 day lidar crown-boundary prep, ~1 day band-completeness and closure audit, ~0.5 day reporting ≈ 5 human-days. No bespoke sensors, no new flights, no new annotation campaign — the 2020 labels and the 1,250-point panel already exist.",
  "kill_criterion": "PRIMARY (the test map-differencing failed): raw CE-BSI 2016→2024 citywide net must fall inside [−3.31, −1.11] pp, i.e. the human panel's −2.21 pp ± 1.10. A positive sign, or |CE-BSI − (−2.21)| > 1.10 pp, kills the design outright. GATE 1, runnable today before a GPU is booked: the band at R = 8 m plus the 16 m interior screen must contain ≥ 90% of the panel's verified change points, computed from the distance-to-crown-edge values F6 already produced. Below 90% the edge-first premise is simply false and the design dies for free. GATE 2, the number simulation cannot fake: on F7's same-flight-two-ways pair and on each of the three F3 2017 cross-delivery pairs, |CE-BSI net| after bias correction must be ≤ 0.5 pp. Above 0.5 pp on any one of the four, we have not beaten F7's 1.3 pp frozen-model processing-chain floor by enough to resolve a 2 pp/8 yr signal against a ±1.10 pp panel half-width, and it dies. GATE 3: 1.96 SD of triple-closure misclosure must be ≤ 0.8 pp; larger means the pairwise estimates are not mutually consistent and the leveled series should not be published."
 },
 "expected_failure_modes": [
  "Co-registration residual masquerading as change. DeepBVC flags this exact cascade — its registration step 'potentially changes the scale and skew of the brain image, which can in turn affect the final BVC estimation.' At 1 m GSD, a systematic 1-pixel drift integrated over the whole city's crown perimeter is a large area. Mitigated by σ_geo in the RSS budget and by dropping pairs with RMSE_geo > 0.5 × GSD, but a spatially correlated drift will not be caught by an RMSE.",
  "Phenology is unidentified, and this is the sharpest hole. All four real null pairs are same-year and, being the same flight or the same season's deliveries, almost certainly same-leaf-state — so they teach chain-invariance and teach nothing about leaf-off. Flights run Feb–Oct. Any pair straddling leaf-off has its season term identified only by the simulation emulator and the stratum spread, which is weak. Mitigation: stratify or exclude leaf-off dates and report the trend both ways; but if the archive has no cross-season null pair, this stays an assumption.",
  "Simulation realism gap. The chain emulator will not reproduce ortho-rectification seams, building lean, mosaic boundaries, or vendor-specific pansharpening. The network may latch onto a simulation artifact and collapse on real pairs. This is exactly what Gate 2 exists to catch — real null pairs are the only evidence simulation cannot manufacture — but Gate 2 passing on four pairs is weak evidence about the other ~96.",
  "Under-identified chain bias: 4 real null pairs against 5+ delivery programs means at least one b_k has no direct identifying observation and must be pooled with a similar chain or taken from simulation. The output flags which, but a wrong pooling tilts the early archive silently.",
  "Interior clearings escaping the band if the 16 m screen's 0.20 fraction cut is too strict for gradual thinning spread over a large block — a large, slow, diffuse interior loss is the one geometry this design can miss.",
  "The F4 greenness gradient surviving augmentation because the emulator's greenness range does not actually span the archive's range. The recall drop of −0.109 across same-sensor years is a whole-distribution radiometric shift; if the augmenter samples a narrower range, the network is invariant over the wrong interval.",
  "Coverage bias from GSD weighting: 1 m NAIP pairs carry low weight by design, so the leveled trend is driven by the years with fine imagery. That is statistically correct and epistemically uncomfortable — the trend then describes the well-imaged years and interpolates the rest, and should be reported as such rather than as a uniform 20-year measurement.",
  "Simulated change is injected as crown removal/erosion/dilation of EXISTING crowns; genuinely novel canopy geometry (a new planting pattern, a species with different crown texture) is out of the training distribution and its response slope is unverified."
 ],
 "sources": [
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_15_deepbvc.txt",
   "page": "4",
   "claim": "The core architecture: paired-image input, regression of boundary shift, output MASKED to boundary locations only. This is the change-first, edge-restricted estimator transplanted in steps 4-5.",
   "quote": "The model inputs comprise a pair of baseline and follow-up T1 images. The label is a voxelwise estimation of brain boundary shift produced from SIENA. Because SIENA estimates the brain boundary shift for each voxel location in the input, the label and the model output are 3-D tensors with the same dimensions as the inputs. During both training and inference, the model output is masked with the brain boundary segmentation and only the outputs at the boundary locations are retained (outputs are set to 0 for non-boundary locations)."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_15_deepbvc.txt",
   "page": "4",
   "claim": "Chain-consistency regularization (step 4d): the SAME pair rendered under different acquisition conditions must yield the SAME change. This is how radiometric/GSD heterogeneity is handled without calibrating anything.",
   "quote": "Furthermore, to render the model insensitive to differences in imaging quality during training, we adopted a consistency regularisation loss. Specifically, the loss minimised the difference between the predictions derived from the original images and the augmented images, such that brain volume differences were maintained in the context of an isolated change in imaging acquisition conditions."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_15_deepbvc.txt",
   "page": "10",
   "claim": "Quantitative evidence that paired change-regression with consistency regularization beats a classical change algorithm specifically under ACQUISITION MISMATCH - an order of magnitude at the worst inconsistency level. This is the load-bearing reason to expect CE-BSI to beat map-differencing across a heterogeneous archive.",
   "quote": "For the highest level of inconsistency, the median difference measured by SIENA reached 2.8%, while DeepBVC remained as low as 0.29%. The error distribution of DeepBVC was less scattered and closer to 0 than the equivalent of SIENA for all levels of inconsistency tested."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_15_deepbvc.txt",
   "page": "13",
   "claim": "Two things: (a) the one-step vs multi-step closure test as a truth-free internal diagnostic that reveals systematic error - step 10 and Gate 3; (b) the registration-error cascade, cited as expected failure mode 1.",
   "quote": "The difference between one-step and multi-step measurements can also reveal systematic errors; a smaller difference therefore indicates that less effort is required for calibration for studies that involve multiple (>=3) data timepoints. ... the registration step potentially changes the scale and skew of the brain image, which can in turn affect the final BVC estimation."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_15_deepbvc.txt",
   "page": "13",
   "claim": "Honest limitation that motivates our Gate 2 design: pseudo-labels from the classical method are fine for training but cannot serve as validation truth. Our analogue - map-differenced change may seed nothing, and the human panel is held out.",
   "quote": "In this regard, BVC estimates from SIENA are noisy: they can be used to produce pseudo-labels for the training phase, but they cannot be used as ground truth during the validation phase, particularly without rigorous manual quality control checks."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\nguyen2024_swissalps.txt",
   "page": "8",
   "claim": "Domain prior justifying edge-first treatment on uncalibrated historical aerial time series: canopy boundaries move slowly for gain and abruptly for loss. Used to justify band construction and the asymmetric expectation about where change lives.",
   "quote": "We know that the orientation of forest boundaries can only change slowly across time, except in the case of forest loss, where it can change abruptly."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\nguyen2024_swissalps.txt",
   "page": "9",
   "claim": "Precedent for a contour-restricted evaluation metric (F1c on a contour neighbourhood) - the basis for evaluating and reporting CE-BSI inside the band rather than over the whole scene.",
   "quote": "for forest contour evaluation: F1c, which corresponds to the F1 score computed only for pixels located close to contours in the labels (i.e. transition from F to NF), following Perazzi et al. (2016). We define contour neighborhood as pixels located less that 25 meters from contours"
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\nguyen2024_swissalps.txt",
   "page": "9",
   "claim": "Precedent for the grayscale-collapse augmentation used in our chain emulator (step 3), from a study on exactly this data type - uncalibrated multi-sensor historical aerial time series with labels in one year only.",
   "quote": "since we only use RGB images, we also use an augmentation method that simulates grayscale data by averaging the RGB bands into a single band for a random subset of the training set."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\nguyen2024_swissalps.txt",
   "page": "15",
   "claim": "Ablation showing contour focus is what carries performance on the hardest (grayscale/degraded) domains - the empirical warrant for restricting the estimator to an edge band rather than the whole image.",
   "quote": "score drops from 81.0% to 64.3%, even lower than with the tCE loss alone (Table 5). Using a symmetrical scaling term, i.e., penalizing the model for the prediction of abrupt forest loss, leads to slightly lower performances than when using the proposed asymmetrical version."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\shoreline_ofr2010_1118.txt",
   "page": "19",
   "claim": "The central justification for step 7: a measurement-chain bias that acts in one direction does NOT cancel on averaging and must be explicitly estimated and removed. This is exactly the status of F4's ~1 pp same-year delivery-chain detection bias.",
   "quote": "Because the proxy-datum offset is a bias that virtually always acts in the same direction, the error associated with the apparent shoreline change rate shift is not canceled during averaging. It is important to quantify this bias in order to resolve the shift it can cause in reported rates."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\shoreline_ofr2010_1118.txt",
   "page": "20",
   "claim": "The RSS uncertainty budget of step 8, with per-source terms assigned by data vintage/type - the template for sigma_geo, sigma_GSD, sigma_chain, sigma_season, sigma_model and hence for the level of detection.",
   "quote": "For each HWL shoreline position, the total uncertainty is found as the square root of the sum of squares (Taylor, 1997) of the relevant uncertainty terms, based on an assumption that each term is random and independent of the others."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\shoreline_ofr2010_1118.txt",
   "page": "24",
   "claim": "Explicit warning that quadrature averaging UNDERSTATES uncertainty when errors are not independent - the reason our expected failure mode 1 flags spatially correlated co-registration drift as uncaught by an RSS budget.",
   "quote": "Quadrature average results in very small values of uncertainty, on the order of +/-1 to 2 cm, and is likely an underestimate of the uncertainty because it is unlikely that all transect uncertainties are independent of all the others."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_28_bautin_mdc.txt",
   "page": "2",
   "claim": "The step-3 simulation framework: inject a KNOWN change into a real image and separately apply a nuisance transformation, to quantify accuracy and precision of the estimator as a function of change magnitude. This is how the response curve and the LoD are obtained without any new ground truth.",
   "quote": "the proposed framework utilizes image scaling and applies a random rigid transformation to mimic subject repositioning (scan-rescan) enabling the quantification of the accuracy and precision of the estimated CSA across various degrees of simulated atrophy. From these experiments, power analyses and minimum sample sizes are derived."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_28_bautin_mdc.txt",
   "page": "1",
   "claim": "The stated limit of simulation-derived detectability - a best-case number, because the simulator does not encompass real-life variability. Cited directly as our expected failure mode 3 and in the weakest point.",
   "quote": "we would like to stress that these results correspond to a \"best case\" scenario, in that the dataset used here was of particularly good quality and the model for simulating atrophy does not encompass all the variability met in real-life datasets."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_0_coupland2022.txt",
   "page": "5",
   "claim": "Precedent for using a same-date lidar/photo overlap purely as an equivalence anchor licensing cross-source comparison - the role our 2016 lidar epoch plays, via TOST rather than as a training target.",
   "quote": "To ensure validity of comparing the 1949 aerial photos with LiDAR data, 2015 aerial photos were compared with the 2015 LiDAR data and examined for equivalency. We randomly selected 2% (342 polygons) of the 0.05 ha polygons randomly for validation"
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_0_coupland2022.txt",
   "page": "6",
   "claim": "The realistic ceiling on how tightly lidar can anchor a photo-derived canopy estimate: equivalence only to +/-5.38% TCC at 0.05 ha. This is why lidar is used for a response SLOPE, down-weighted, and never as a training label the network must reproduce.",
   "quote": "These results indicate that the methods are similar are within a range +/-5.38% TCC and were deemed equivalent."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\roman2021_land_phila.txt",
   "page": "7",
   "claim": "Lidar-derived canopy and image-interpreted canopy disagree in a way driven by the height cut-off - the definitional reason lidar cannot be truth for an RGB canopy estimator. Also the interpreter-variation magnitude (1.3 pp) underpinning a detectable-change threshold.",
   "quote": "The height cut-off for LiDAR-derived UTC analyses in Philadelphia was 2.4 m. Comparing our 2010 interpretation to 2008 LiDAR [74], we found 93.4% agreement regarding whether a given point was a tree or not. ... The two interpreters' UTC estimates were close: the average difference across all years was 1.3 percentage points."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\roman2021_land_phila.txt",
   "page": "8",
   "claim": "Precedent for reporting change against an explicit detectable-change threshold derived from measurement variability rather than reporting every difference as real - the basis for reporting sub-LoD pairs as NOT RESOLVED in step 8.",
   "quote": "than 3.2 percentage points are considered detectable change."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_29_mcroberts2014.txt",
   "page": "3",
   "claim": "The step-12 delivered estimator: the map-based initial estimate minus the expectation of its bias estimated from a reference sample. This is how the CE-BSI raster and the 1,250-point panel are combined instead of one overruling the other.",
   "quote": "One form of the model-assisted regression estimator (Sarndal, Swensson, & Wretman, 1992, Section 6.5) for mu_k is defined as the difference between the initial estimator and the expectation of its bias estimate"
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_29_mcroberts2014.txt",
   "page": "5",
   "claim": "Direct criticism of the categorical two-date-map-difference approach to change - it discards the magnitude information that separates a real transition from a threshold crossing. Justifies regressing a continuous signed change fraction instead of differencing two binary maps.",
   "quote": "However, this categorical approach is not very discriminating. For example, if p^i2002 = 0.99 and p^i2007 = 0.01, then the ith population unit would be estimated to have changed from forest (yi = 1) to nonforest (yi = 0); similarly, if p^i2002 = 0.51 and p^i2007 = 0.49, the ith population unit would also be estimated"
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_18_pontius2004.txt",
   "page": "3",
   "claim": "The swap/net decomposition of step 11 - the replacement for the flicker census. Swap is change in LOCATION with no change in quantity, which is precisely the signature of chain-driven spurious change, and it is invisible in a net-change number.",
   "quote": "It is possible that change occurs in such a way that the location of a category changes between time 1 and time 2, while the quantity remains the same. For example, a given quantity of forest loss at one location can be accompanied by the same quantity of forest gain at another location. This type of change in location is termed a \"swap\"."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\r2_18_pontius2004.txt",
   "page": "1",
   "claim": "Warning that a persistence-dominated landscape makes a no-change prediction look excellent - the structural reason stability-inheritance and temporal smoothing appear to succeed while being blind to dispersed loss. Cited in the not_smoothing_not_inheritance justification.",
   "quote": "a null model that predicts no change is often better than a model that predicts change, as the agreement between the reference map of time 1 and the reference map of time 2 is often greater than the agreement between a model's prediction of time 2 and the reference map of time 2. A model's prediction of time 2 can, upon visual inspection, appear to be an excellent fit due to the dominance of persistence, whereas the same model usually predicts incorrectly when it tries to predict change."
  },
  {
   "file": "C:\\Users\\Kameron\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Kameron\\43e2ce58-528b-45a8-b9d9-c8db345691a7\\scratchpad\\txt\\chesapeake_change_accuracy_methods.txt",
   "page": "8",
   "claim": "Operational precedent (Stehman-advised) that a high-resolution CHANGE product gets its own accuracy-assessment design with change-specific classes and strata - not the accuracy assessment of its two parent maps. Supports validating CE-BSI directly on the change panel.",
   "quote": "accuracy assessment design - Define map classes - Define strata - Sample framing"
  }
 ],
 "novelty_vs_known_families": "Against temporal filtering / HMM (Abercrombie & Friedl; the F5 pilot): those post-process a stack of per-date maps under a rare-change prior. CE-BSI has no per-date map in the estimation path and no temporal prior; its only zero-change pressure comes from four pairs observed to be null. Pontius names why the family looks good and is not: a no-change model beats a change model on a persistence-dominated landscape by construction. Against calibrated time-series algorithms (LandTrendr, CCDC, Zhu 2017, Greenfield/NLCD-TCC): all require a radiometrically calibrated, temporally dense, single-sensor series — the archive supplies none of the three, and F2 shows the deficit cannot be repaired downstream. Against trajectories / stability inheritance: no state crosses epochs; every pair is estimated from its own two images; Gate 1 makes the anti-inheritance claim falsifiable rather than asserted. Against method-holding and harmonization (DeepHarmony, CALAMITI, HACA3, Mboga's DANN/D-CORAL, IR-MAD/PIF, histogram matching): those normalize images or features toward a reference before mapping; CE-BSI never harmonizes anything, treating chain difference as a train-time invariance plus a residual scalar identified from null pairs — and F7 shows method-holding alone still leaves a 1.3 pp floor, so harmonization of the model is demonstrably insufficient. Against sample-based change estimation (Olofsson, Stehman, McRoberts, Roman's point panel, Tampa's dot grid): those are wall-to-wall-blind and cannot locate the 0.41 ha cluster or the 2 m-from-edge dispersed losses; CE-BSI produces a spatial change field and uses the sample only as anchor and debiaser. Against the closest published item, Nguyen 2024 (already known): tCA is map-first — an asymmetric contour-alignment loss regularizing a stack of per-date segmentations — whereas CE-BSI is change-first, its output being a signed change fraction, never a map; Nguyen's edge insight is inherited, the estimand is not. Against Coupland 2022 (already read): Coupland uses lidar↔photo equivalence to license a two-date LEVEL comparison; CE-BSI inverts the role, fitting a response SLOPE against continuous change magnitude and never comparing levels across sources. The genuinely new combination is a DeepBVC-class edge-masked paired-regression change estimator — a longitudinal-atrophy architecture from medical imaging — transplanted to urban canopy, fitted with a Hapke-style proxy-bias-plus-RSS budget over delivery chains, leveled by a redundant closure network with per-chain bias unknowns, its response curve calibrated by Bautin-style injected change, and debiased by a McRoberts model-assisted estimator on the human panel.",
 "cost_estimate": "≤3 GPU-days on one A100-class card (≈0.5 GPU-day inference over ~100 band-restricted pairs; ≈1.5–2 GPU-days training on simulated pairs plus the lidar pair with the five-term loss), plus ≈2 CPU-days of embarrassingly parallel preprocessing (co-registration, resampling to the coarser member's GSD). ≈5 human-days: 1 day chain/pair metadata and null-pair curation, 1.5 days simulation realism QC, 1 day lidar crown-boundary prep, 1 day band-completeness and closure audit, 0.5 day reporting. No new imagery, no new lidar, no new sensors, no new annotation campaign — the 2020 hand labels and the 1,250-point panel already exist. Gate 1 costs roughly one hour and can kill the design before any GPU time is spent.",
 "self_assessed_weakest_point": "Chain-bias identification rests on exactly four real null pairs against 5+ delivery programs and 20 years, and no more can be manufactured — F3 and F7 gave us all there are. Everything the four cannot reach (the response slope for chain-pairs with no lidar anchor, the leaf-state term, the GSD term) leans on a delivery-chain emulator I can only approximate, and Bautin says plainly that simulation-derived detectability is a best case because the simulator \"does not encompass all the variability met in real-life datasets.\" Gate 2 will catch a gross emulator failure, but passing on four pairs is weak evidence about the ninety-six others. The concrete danger: if b_k for the 2005–2010 city program is wrong by 0.8 pp, the leveled 20-year trend tilts by roughly that amount and I would not detect it — the human panel anchors 2016→2024 and the hand labels anchor 2020, so the early archive has no independent check at all. Secondarily, phenology is unidentified because all four null pairs are same-season, and the entire design's premise (that change lives at crown edges) is asserted from F6's median-2.0 m statistic; Gate 1 tests it honestly, but if Gate 1 returns something like 82% rather than ≥90%, the design does not degrade gracefully — it just fails, and I have no fallback within this angle."
}

 "design_name": "CE-BSI: Crown-Edge Boundary-Shift Integral (change-first canopy trend)",

 "total": 16,
 "fatal_flaw": "The four real null pairs are spent three times over, and everything the design delivers beyond one pair rides on them. (1) LEAKAGE: the same four pairs supply training supervision (loss term 4c penalizes |delta| on them), then identify the chain-bias unknowns b_k (step 7), then serve as Gate 2, the \"number simulation cannot fake.\" A test whose observations are in the training loss AND in the bias fit cannot fail except through optimizer failure; the design's only real-data check on chain bias is void as written. (2) UNDER-IDENTIFICATION, worse than the design's own hedge: three 2017 deliveries give 3 pairwise differences but only 2 independent contrasts (b_A-b_C is determined by the other two), and F7's pair is same-flight, so it identifies a within-program rendering offset, not a between-program b_k. That is ~3 independent degrees of freedom against >=5 program biases + greenness coefficient + leaf-state offset (>=6 free after anchoring). (3) MISSPECIFICATION: F4's evidence is a recall drop of -0.109 -- a detection-probability effect that scales with how much crown edge is present -- but b_k is parameterized as an additive city-wide scalar. A multiplicative/scale error modeled as an intercept leaves residual bias that grows with the edge budget of the pair. (4) The estimator's own attenuation is corrected only by a slope s fitted on the simulator: MSE + consistency regularization + a label distribution dominated by zeros shrinks |delta| toward 0 on ambiguous pixels -- the F5 failure direction reached by a different door -- and step 6's (Delta_hat - c)/s undoes it exactly as far as the emulator matches reality, which Bautin says plainly it does not (\"best case ... does not encompass all the variability met in real-life datasets\"). The one real slope anchor is lidar, which the design itself argues is a different object (Roman's 2.4 m cut, Coupland's +/-5.38%). Net identification: what CE-BSI estimates is simulator-change, rescaled by simulator-fitted slopes, debiased by a per-chain intercept fit to ~3 numbers that were already in the loss. Step 12's McRoberts estimator genuinely rescues 2016->2024 -- design-unbiased regardless of map quality -- but the panel covers one pair, so the headline 20-year trend, the product the city actually wants, is rescued nowhere and its bias is both unbounded and unmeasurable. The design's self-assessment gets close to this (\"if b_k for the 2005-2010 city program is wrong by 0.8 pp ... I would not detect it\") but does not notice that its own Gate 2 is the thing that was supposed to detect it. Partially repairable: hold the four pairs out of training entirely and fit b_k leave-one-out; the under-identification and the intercept-vs-scale misspecification survive the repair.",
 "best_idea_to_graft": "The real-null-pair as an identification and audit device -- and specifically as a HELD-OUT one. F7's same-flight-delivered-two-ways pair and the three same-year 2017 deliveries are the archive's scan-rescan cohort: pairs where Delta_true = 0 by construction rather than by assumption. Any Condition-B winner should (a) never train or fit on them, (b) run its finished citywide change estimator on all four, and (c) report |Delta_hat| on them as its processing-chain noise floor, with a pre-registered pass bar (CE-BSI's <= 0.5 pp is a sensible number, since F7's frozen-model floor is 1.3 pp and the panel half-width is 1.10 pp). This is the only evidence in the whole problem that no simulator can manufacture, it costs zero new data, it is estimator-agnostic, and it converts F3/F7 from diagnostics into a test. Second graft, nearly as cheap: Gate 1 -- before any GPU time, check what fraction of the 1,250 panel's verified change points fall inside the candidate method's measured domain, using the distance-to-crown-edge values F6 already produced. One hour, kills or clears an edge-restricted or region-restricted design for free. Third, weaker but worth carrying: reporting sub-LoD pairs as NOT RESOLVED rather than as zero, so measurement noise never enters the record as a flicker event.",
 "source_check": "Read and verified verbatim against the cited files; all page attributions correct. r2_15_deepbvc.txt p4 (3D-Unet, paired input, output masked to boundary locations; consistency regularisation loss), p10 (SIENA median 2.8% vs DeepBVC 0.29% at highest resolution-anisotropy inconsistency), p13 (one-step vs multi-step reveals systematic errors; registration changes scale and skew; SIENA pseudo-labels usable for training but not as validation ground truth) -- all exact. nguyen2024_swissalps.txt p8 (forest boundary orientation changes slowly except loss), p9 (F1c contour neighborhood, 25 m, Perazzi 2016; grayscale-simulating RGB-band-averaging augmentation), p15 (81.0% -> 64.3%) -- all exact. shoreline_ofr2010_1118.txt p19 (proxy-datum bias \"virtually always acts in the same direction ... not canceled during averaging\"), p20 (RSS of relevant uncertainty terms, assumed random and independent), p24 (quadrature average likely an underestimate) -- all exact. r2_28_bautin_mdc.txt p2 (scaling + random rigid transform to mimic repositioning), p1 (abstract \"best case\" caveat) -- exact. r2_0_coupland2022.txt p5 (2% / 342 polygons validation), p6 (+/-5.38% TCC equivalence) -- exact. roman2021_land_phila.txt p7 (2.4 m cut, 93.4% agreement, 1.3 pp interpreter difference), p8 (3.2 pp detectable change) -- exact. r2_29_mcroberts2014.txt p3 (model-assisted regression = initial estimator minus expectation of its bias estimate), p5 (0.99/0.01 vs 0.51/0.49 \"not very discriminating\") -- exact. r2_18_pontius2004.txt (swap definition; null-model-beats-change-model) -- exact. chesapeake_change_accuracy_methods.txt p8 (Stehman on the AA design team; define map classes / strata / sample framing) -- exact. TWO SELECTIVE-USE FLAGS, not misquotes: (i) the DeepBVC transplant is argued from the RESOLUTION-ANISOTROPY result, but the closest MRI analogue to F4's radiometric greenness gradient is the BIAS FIELD experiment, and there the same page reports DeepBVC's \"medians and interquartile ranges ... were higher than SIENA\" -- i.e. the transplant underperformed the classical method on precisely the nuisance class this design most needs it to beat; the design never mentions this. (ii) the Nguyen ablation quote is cited as showing \"contour focus is what carries performance,\" but the quoted passage attributes the 81.0->64.3 drop to removing the ANGLE term, and its final sentence says removing the scaling term (the contour-focus term) \"slightly increases the performances for RGB images.\" Also worth noting for the transplant's premise: DeepBVC trains on SIENA pseudo-labels over real longitudinal pairs, whereas CE-BSI trains overwhelmingly on synthetic change -- the architecture transfers, the training-data regime does not.",
 "verdict": "Strong design, top-tier on the hard structural criteria, undone by its own accounting of evidence. It is the cleanest answer in the field to the operating-point problem: by regressing signed change directly from the pair, F3's 10.1 pp threshold spread has literally no surface to act on, and F2 independently proves the fix had to happen at detection time. It touches no pixel values, needs no lidar beyond the two epochs, degrades gracefully if both are withheld, and is neither temporal smoothing nor stability inheritance -- the 8 m band with a both-dates boundary union sits directly on top of F6's median-2.0 m dispersed losses, which is the sharpest single move any design here makes against the \"blind to dispersed loss\" trap. Gate 1 is the best kill criterion I have seen in this set: an hour of work, before any compute, on data already in hand, and it is honestly stated to be non-survivable. Source handling is meticulous -- every quote verbatim, every page right. What it cannot do is prove its own bias correction works. Three independent null contrasts against six-plus bias unknowns, all three consumed by the training loss and the bias fit before being reused as the test, plus a simulator-fitted attenuation slope with one mismatched real anchor, means the 20-year trend -- the deliverable -- has no out-of-sample check anywhere. Only the 2016->2024 pair is genuinely defensible, via McRoberts on the panel, and that pair could be estimated far more cheaply. Docked on scales and implementability for the same underlying reason: the human-day budget counts QC of the delivery-chain emulator (1.5 days) but not building it, the copy-paste inpainting engine, the Siamese trainer, the co-registration pipeline, the 37-epoch adjustment solver, or the band constructor -- weeks of engineering, not five days -- and the leveling step assumes an additive level model (Delta_ij = C_j - C_i) that a nonlinear network is not built to satisfy, with Gate 3 detecting the violation but no fallback if it fires. Verdict: not a winner as posed, but a serious contender if the null pairs are quarantined out of training, b_k is refit leave-one-out with a scale term rather than an intercept, and the delivered claim is narrowed to the pairs the panel can actually anchor. Its null-pair audit and its pre-compute band-completeness gate belong in whatever design does win."
}

### JUDGMENT: {
 "design_name": "CE-BSI: Crown-Edge Boundary-Shift Integral (change-first canopy trend)",
 "scores": {
  "no_radiometric_calibration": 2,
  "no_extra_lidar": 2,
  "operating_point": 2,
