 "total": 15,
 "fatal_flaw": "Not disqualifying, but the sharpest single flaw: ALL nonzero-change supervision is synthetic. The only real supervision the design has is four zero-change pairs and one deliberately down-weighted lidar pair; every response slope s that converts detected boundary shift into pp of city canopy therefore comes from the chain emulator. That emulator must span \"the F4 gradient\" in greenness — but F4 is known only as a recall drop of -0.109, not as a measured distribution, so the emulator's range has to be set from the very radiometric shift the design (correctly) refuses to calibrate. The design's own cited precedent makes this worse rather than better: DeepBVC's 2.8%-to-0.29% win is on resolution anisotropy, and on bias-field (the radiometric analogue) the same paper reports DeepBVC's medians and IQRs were HIGHER than the classical method. So the load-bearing evidence is for the GSD nuisance, not the radiometric one, and DeepBVC trained against two nuisances where CE-BSI asks the same trick to absorb nine.\n\nTwo concrete practitioner consequences the design does not cover. (a) FAILURE IS UNATTRIBUTABLE. If the primary gate misses the panel's [-3.31, -1.11] pp, nothing in the pipeline separates a bad emulator from an incomplete band from a wrong slope from a wrong b_k; the author concedes the design \"does not degrade gracefully\" and there is no fallback inside the angle. (b) GATE 1 IS BLIND TO THE ONE REAL HOLE in the anti-inheritance claim. A small crown that the recall-limited in-house locator misses at BOTH dates produces no boundary, hence no band, and the 16 m screen compares two masks that are both zero there, so it never triggers — that loss is structurally invisible to CE-BSI. Gate 1 as specified measures distance from panel points to REFERENCE crown edges (F6 geometry), not membership in the constructed band, so it passes at >=90% while this hole is open. Gate 1 must be respecified as band membership under the actual p>=0.15 masks of each pair, or it certifies a premise it never tested.\n\nSupporting cost/spec defects: the step-1 drop rule (RMSE_geo > 0.5 x GSD_pair) demands ~2.5 cm cross-vendor co-registration residual on 5 cm pairs, which is unachievable on historical urban orthos (building lean and DTM differences alone give decimeter-to-meter local displacement) while permitting 50 cm residual on 1 m pairs where a half-pixel citywide edge displacement is enormous relative to a perimeter integral — the tolerance tightens exactly where geometry is hardest and loosens where the integral is most sensitive. The copy-paste inpainter avoids a generative signature but substitutes a stronger one: pasted sunlit ground under a removed crown teaches \"removal = bright patch,\" when real crown loss leaves formerly-shaded or neighbor-shadowed surface. And the five loss weights have no clean tuning set: the panel is held out for the kill test, so tuning happens on simulation alone, which Bautin explicitly calls a best case. First thing to verify before anything else: whether the 2024 delivery chain matches any of the four null pairs (all 2017 or same-flight). If it does not, b_k for the single pair the design stakes its life on is simulation-only.",
 "verdict": "STRONG CONTENDER - among the best-specified Condition-B designs, and it clears the hard checklist. Applying the operational reading of \"<= a few human-days\" (per-run analyst labor, which the design's ~5 days plausibly covers), all six Condition-B constraints hold; the omitted build cost is an honesty problem, not a constraint violation, and I scored it under `scales` rather than treating it as disqualifying.\n\nWhat it gets genuinely right, and better than its peers: the estimand is changed rather than the recipe. Because no per-date probability raster is thresholded inside the estimation path, F3's 10.1 pp catastrophe has no surface to act on, and F2 independently proves the fix had to move upstream to detection time — this design is the one that actually acts on that finding instead of quoting it. It is not smoothing (no filter, no state, no temporal prior; the only zero-change pressure comes from four pairs where zero is an observation) and not inheritance (each pair estimated from its own two images; the band is the union of BOTH dates' boundaries, so a vanished crown still has a t-epoch edge inside the measured domain). Sub-LoD pairs reported as NOT RESOLVED rather than zero or flicker is the single most professionally mature output decision in the set. The kill criteria are the best I have scored: ordered by cost, numeric, and Gate 1 costs about an hour and can kill the design before a GPU is booked - though it must be respecified first (see fatal_flaw).\n\nWhere it will hurt on delivery: the ~5 human-days enumerate only analyst QC and silently exclude building six novel subsystems (cross-vendor TPS co-registration over ~3,700 tile fits, a nine-component chain emulator, a crown-injection simulator, a five-term masked Siamese regressor, a 40+-unknown weighted network adjustment with covariance propagation, and a McRoberts LOO estimator). That is a person-quarter for a strong team, not a week, and a city client told \"five days\" will be told the wrong thing. Compute is fine and the GPU estimate is right to within a small factor.\n\nNovelty is real - a longitudinal-atrophy architecture from medical imaging transplanted to canopy, with per-chain bias identified from observed nulls, is a combination I have not seen. In passing: the leveling half is essentially SBAS (small-baseline-subset InSAR) - redundant pairwise estimates, network adjustment, closure as diagnostic - a large mature family the design neither names nor differentiates against. That is a compliment to the arithmetic and a gap in the novelty argument.\n\nRecommendation: do not run this as specified. Run Gate 1 first, respecified as segmenter-band membership rather than reference-edge distance, and check the 2024 chain against the null-pair set. Both are hours of work and both can kill it for free. If it survives, graft its bias model into whatever wins.",
 "best_idea_to_graft": "THE FOUR REAL NULL PAIRS AS THE IDENTIFYING OBSERVATIONS FOR A PER-CHAIN BIAS TERM, carried into an RSS uncertainty budget that yields a level of detection. This is the single most valuable idea in the design and it is completely architecture-independent - it grafts onto a map-first pipeline, a sample-based estimator, or anything else.\n\nThe move: recognize that F7's same-flight-delivered-two-ways pair and the three pairwise combinations of the 2017 same-year deliveries are the ONLY observations in the entire archive where the truth is known exactly (Delta_true = 0), and that they are therefore not curiosities but the scarce identifying resource of the whole problem. For a null pair (i,j) drawn from chains k and l, any nonzero estimate is b_k - b_l + eps, which identifies chain-bias differences with no simulation and no new data. Hapke's logic supplies the reason this matters: a measurement-chain bias that \"virtually always acts in the same direction\" is \"not canceled during averaging\" - which is exactly the status of F4's ~1 pp same-year detection bias, and exactly why it has been quietly tilting every trend estimate so far. Estimate it, subtract it, and carry its posterior variance into sigma so it widens the CI instead of hiding in the point estimate.\n\nThe output convention that comes with it is equally graftable and arguably worth more to the city than the estimate itself: pairs falling below LoD = 1.96 x sigma are reported as NOT RESOLVED - not as zero, and never as a flicker event. That one rule retires the F5 flicker census as an artifact-counting exercise and replaces it with a defensible statement about what the archive can and cannot see. Roman's paper is the precedent for publishing an explicit detectable-change threshold rather than reporting every difference as real.\n\nSECOND, but only after respecification: Gate 1 - kill the design for free before a GPU is booked, by testing whether the measured domain actually contains the panel's verified change points. As written it tests distance to reference crown edges and would certify a premise it never checked; rewritten to test membership in the ACTUAL constructed band (the p>=0.15 masks plus 8 m dilation plus the 16 m interior screen, per pair), it becomes the cheapest honest test in the whole tournament and every edge-restricted or band-restricted design should be made to pass it first.\n\nTHIRD, cheap and portable: report triple closure (Delta(t0->t2) vs Delta(t0->t1) + Delta(t1->t2)) as a truth-free internal consistency diagnostic, NEVER enforced. It costs nothing, needs no reference data, and enforcing it would smuggle smoothing back in through the door this design correctly locked.",
 "source_check": "Read all cited files and grepped every quote. VERBATIM ACCURACY: 20/20 quotes match the source text exactly, including the long ones (DeepBVC p4 masking passage and consistency-regularisation passage; Hapke's \"not canceled during averaging\" and \"square root of the sum of squares\"; Hapke's quadrature-underestimate warning; Bautin's \"mimic subject repositioning\" and \"best case\" caveat; Coupland's 342-polygon TOST and +/-5.38% TCC; Roman's 2.4 m cut / 93.4% agreement / 1.3 pp interpreter difference / 3.2 pp detectable-change threshold; McRoberts' model-assisted definition and the 0.99->0.01 vs 0.51->0.49 criticism; Pontius' swap definition and null-model warning; Nguyen's forest-boundary prior, F1c contour metric, and RGB-band-averaging augmentation; Chesapeake's AA design steps). No fabrication, no paraphrase-as-quote, no invented numbers. Page attributions are unverifiable against flat text dumps but content placement is consistent.\n\nWHY THE SCORE IS 1 AND NOT 2 - one claim column materially overgeneralizes a source the design leans on hardest. The design cites DeepBVC's \"median difference measured by SIENA reached 2.8%, while DeepBVC remained as low as 0.29%\" and labels it \"the load-bearing reason to expect CE-BSI to beat map-differencing across a heterogeneous archive,\" and separately cites the consistency-regularisation loss as the mechanism that handles F4's radiometric gradient without calibration. But that result is specifically for SPATIAL RESOLUTION ANISOTROPY - the GSD dimension. Twelve lines earlier in the same file (r2_15_deepbvc.txt line 417) the paper reports: \"For bias field inconsistencies, the medians and interquartile ranges for DeepBVC were higher than SIENA.\" Bias field is the MRI analogue of a radiometric/illumination gradient - i.e. the analogue of F4, the archive's dominant nuisance and the one the design most needs the transplant to cover. On that dimension the cited method showed NO advantage over the classical baseline. The design quotes the win and does not surface the loss.\n\nLine 467 of the same file compounds it: \"Though we only included random contrast and spacing anisotropy in training, the model demonstrated improved reliability on test-retest data...\" - DeepBVC's invariance was trained against exactly two nuisances. CE-BSI's step-3 emulator asks the same consistency-regularisation trick to absorb nine (GSD resample, JPEG, unsharp, per-channel gain/offset/gamma, saturation/greenness, grayscale collapse, sun-elevation shadow, misregistration). The precedent does not extend that far, and the design presents it as though it does.\n\nSECOND, MINOR: the Nguyen p15 ablation is quoted accurately but its claim column (\"contour focus is what carries performance on the hardest domains\") is looser than the text supports. The passage attributes the 81.0%->64.3% F1c drop to removing the ANGLE TERM of the tCA loss, and its own next sentence cuts the other way: \"Removing the scaling term completely, which makes the model focus on all pixels instead of predicted contours only, slightly decreases the performances at contours, but slightly increases the overall performances for RGB images.\" That is mild evidence against a blanket contour-restriction claim, and it went unmentioned.\n\nEverything else checks out, and the design deserves credit for citing sources AGAINST itself where it does - Bautin's \"best case\" caveat and Hapke's quadrature-underestimate warning are both quoted in the expected_failure_modes rather than buried. The DeepBVC bias-field omission is the one place that discipline lapses, and it lapses on the load-bearing claim."
}

### JUDGMENT: {
 "design_name": "CE-BSI: Crown-Edge Boundary-Shift Integral (change-first canopy trend)",
 "source_check": "Read all 9 cited files and checked all 24 quotes against the cited page. QUOTE ACCURACY: 24/24 verbatim and correctly page-attributed — including faithful reproduction of source typos (Nguyen \"less that 25 meters\" p.9; Coupland \"the methods are similar are within\" p.6) and an honest mid-sentence truncation across a page break (Roman p.8 \"than 3.2 percentage points...\"). This is the cleanest quoting I have audited. CLAIM ACCURACY: four failures, two of them load-bearing. (1) REFUTED — Nguyen p.15. The design claims the 81.0→64.3 ablation shows \"contour focus is what carries performance on the hardest (grayscale) domains.\" The cited passage says the ANGLE TERM does: \"the angle term is what makes the tCA loss complementary... The F1c score drops from 81.0% to 64.3%.\" The very next sentence, on the same page, ablates contour focus itself: \"Removing the scaling term completely (no scaling), which makes the model focus on all pixels instead of predicted contours only, slightly decreases the performances at contours, but slightly increases the overall performances for RGB images\" (81.0→79.5 F1c overall; RGB IoU 83.0→83.3). The cited page therefore near-refutes the claim it is cited for. (2) OVERREACH — DeepBVC p.10. The 2.8% vs 0.29% figure comes from the Maclaren test-retest experiment where \"The expected PBVC for both methods was 0 for pairs of identical scans\" (p.8) — a spurious-change/reliability result, not accuracy on real change. The paper says so explicitly on p.13, one sentence before the pseudo-label quote the design does cite: \"Although DeepBVC did not inappropriately detect BVC in the test-retest cohort, this does not necessarily demonstrate capacity to accurately determine atrophy estimates in cases with true brain tissue volume differences.\" The design calls this number \"the load-bearing reason to expect CE-BSI to beat map-differencing.\" It licenses noise suppression only. (3) OMISSION — DeepBVC p.10/13. Two disclosures bearing directly on the transplant are absent: \"For bias field inconsistencies, the medians and interquartile ranges for DeepBVC were higher than SIENA\" (p.10) — bias field is a spatially varying intensity gradient, the closest MRI analogue to F4's whole-distribution radiometric greenness gradient, and it is the one nuisance class where the donor method LOST; and \"the lack of a 'true' multi-centre dataset... all images were obtained from a single MS clinic\" (p.13) — the multi-vendor generalization the whole transplant assumes was never tested in the source. (4) INVERTED — Pontius p.3. Swap is defined there as REAL landscape change that net change conceals (\"a given quantity of forest loss at one location can be accompanied by the same quantity of forest gain at another location\"). The design recasts it as \"precisely the signature of chain-driven spurious change.\" Given F6 (dispersed, edge-concentrated real gain and loss citywide), real swap is expected to be large, so \"swap dwarfs net ⇒ chain-noise-dominated\" is confounded on the design's own facts and unsupported by the page cited. CLEAN: all three Hapke quotes (pp.19/20/24) are verbatim and the proxy-datum-bias, RSS-budget and correlated-error transplants are faithful; both Bautin quotes (pp.1/2) verbatim with the \"best case\" caveat honestly carried forward; both Coupland (pp.5/6) and both Roman (pp.7/8) verbatim and correctly characterized (±5.38% TCC at 0.05 ha; 93.4% agreement, 2.4 m height cut; 3.2 pp 40-yr detectable-change threshold); both McRoberts (pp.3/5) verbatim and fairly used; Chesapeake p.8 verbatim, thin (a slide deck) but honestly labeled. Minor unflagged tensions: Nguyen's pipeline DOES radiometrically normalize per year (Eq. 12, p.9) and credits it for a measured gain, which sits awkwardly under a no-calibration design citing Nguyen's augmentation as precedent; and Hapke identifies the proxy-datum bias from an independent physical model (wave runup, Eq. 1), not from observed null cases, so the transplant's identification strategy is weaker than the source's.",
 "scores": {
  "no_radiometric_calibration": 2,
  "no_extra_lidar": 2,
  "operating_point": 2,
  "not_smoothing_not_inheritance": 1,
  "scales": 1,
  "kill_criterion": 1,
  "novelty": 2,
  "implementability": 1,

 },
 "total": 13,
 "fatal_flaw": "THE ONLY NON-SIMULATED EVIDENCE IS SPENT AS TRAINING DATA, SO THE REAL-CHANGE RESPONSE IS SET ENTIRELY BY AN UNVALIDATED EMULATOR AND NO SURVIVING CHECK CAN DETECT IT. The four real null pairs (F7's same-flight-two-ways pair plus the three 2017 cross-delivery pairs) are the design's declared \"scarce resource of the whole design\" — and it uses the identical four, with no held-out split anywhere, three times: step 4(c) trains a loss penalizing |delta| on them; step 7 identifies the per-chain biases b_k from Delta_hat on them; Gate 2 then tests |CE-BSI| ≤ 0.5 pp on them and calls this \"the number simulation cannot fake.\" Gate 2 is a test on training data — the network was explicitly fit to emit ~0 on exactly those pairs, so it passes by construction. Step 7 is circular for the same reason: Delta_hat ≈ 0 there by training, so b_k − b_l ≈ 0 and the \"measured, subtracted\" chain bias is an artifact of the loss rather than an estimate. (Step 7 also claims F3's 10.1→1.07 pp collapse as identifying observations, but those came from the F1 segmenter under map-differencing, not from CE-BSI, and cannot identify parameters of a different estimator.) What remains is that three of five loss terms — null-pair, chain-consistency, antisymmetry — plus the boundary masking carry zero information about real change magnitude; all the real-change signal comes from (a) the simulated crown removal/erosion/dilation and (b) one down-weighted, definitionally mismatched lidar pair (Coupland ±5.38%; Roman's 2.4 m cut) that is itself conditional on an RGB acquisition existing within ~1 year at matching leaf state. This is precisely where the transplant breaks from its donor: DeepBVC had SIENA pseudo-labels on real longitudinal pairs throughout training — real-change supervision — and the design deliberately declines the analogue (\"map-differenced change may seed nothing\"). It keeps DeepBVC's architecture while discarding the only thing that gave it a real-change response. The predictable failure is shrinkage toward zero on real pairs at a different rate than on simulated ones, corrected in step 6 by dividing by a simulation-fitted slope s that is then wrong by an unknown factor (and dividing by a small s amplifies both bias and variance). Nothing catches it: Gate 1 is model-free geometry, Gate 2 is leaked, Gate 3 (closure ≤ 0.8 pp) is trivially satisfied by an all-zeros estimator. Exactly one of four criteria — the primary 2016→2024 test against −2.21 ± 1.10 pp — can discriminate, on a single pair, with no power analysis. And the source audit shows why this is not a hypothetical: the design's headline warrant (DeepBVC 2.8%→0.29%) is itself a zero-change reliability result the source explicitly says \"does not necessarily demonstrate capacity to accurately determine atrophy estimates in cases with true... volume differences.\" An estimator optimized to output zero, validated almost entirely on producing zero, has been imported into a problem whose answer is a small nonzero number.",
 "best_idea_to_graft": "THE HELD-OUT NULL-PAIR ACCEPTANCE GATE. F7's same-flight-two-ways pair and the three pairwise combinations of the 2017 same-year deliveries are four observations whose true change is zero by construction — the only zero-change evidence in the whole problem that no simulator can manufacture. Any candidate design should be required to return |citywide net change| ≤ 0.5 pp on all four, against F7's measured 1.3 pp frozen-model processing-chain floor, before its 2016→2024 number is believed. Graft it with the fix this design missed: hold the four out of training and out of parameter identification ENTIRELY, using them only as a post-hoc acceptance gate — otherwise the graft imports the leak. Two smaller companions are worth taking: (a) Gate 1 — the model-free, roughly one-hour, runnable-today band-completeness test (does an 8 m edge band plus a 16 m interior screen contain ≥90% of the panel's verified change points, computed from the distance-to-crown-edge values F6 already produced?), a kill criterion that costs nothing and can falsify an edge-first premise before any GPU is booked; and (b) the discipline of reporting triple-closure misclosure as a diagnostic and never enforcing it, which is the cleanest articulation in the field of how to get consistency information without smuggling in the smoothing that moved the F5 pilot away from truth.",
 "verdict": "STRONG CONTENDER, NOT A WINNER AS WRITTEN — 13/20, the best-argued design I have audited on citation discipline and the clearest answer anyone has given to F3. Its operating-point elimination is genuine rather than rhetorical: making signed change the regressed estimand means no per-date probability raster is ever thresholded inside the number, so F3's 10.1 pp spread has no surface to act on, and the residual band-locator threshold is set over-inclusively with a committed flatness test (≤0.2 pp drift). It touches no pixel values, uses no lidar beyond 2005/2016 with a declared graceful degradation, and is neither temporal smoothing nor stability inheritance in the families' proper sense. Quoting is impeccable — 24/24 verbatim, correct pages, typos preserved. But it fails the source auditor's test twice where it matters most: the Nguyen p.15 ablation is contradicted by the sentence adjacent to the one quoted, and the DeepBVC headline number is a zero-change reliability result that the source itself disclaims for exactly the inference drawn — while the source's bias-field loss (the analogue of F4's radiometric gradient) and its single-clinic limitation go unmentioned. Those two overreaches are not incidental; they are the same defect that produces the fatal flaw, which the design's own honest self-assessment circles but does not name: its only non-simulated evidence is consumed as training data and as parameter identification before being reused as its headline gate, leaving one discriminating test standing and the entire real-change response resting on an emulator no check can audit. Fixable in principle — hold the four null pairs out, add a real-data response-slope gate that a zero estimator cannot pass, and correct the two citations — but not fixable within the scoring window. Mine it heavily for the winner rather than running it."
}

## F.3 Round-5 critic
**1. Engines.** ~409 queries -> ~140 candidates -> 36 reads (26 full, 10 unreadable) -> 0 adjudicated >=4/5.
LIVE (fed reads): Crossref (S5/S8/S9/S10: Fensham, Carmel 2001, Olofsson, Walton, Nix) and WebSearch (S4/S11: Berlin, Flanders, Atlanta 2023, GLA) - never blocked. Treesearch answers only via `?text=` (S8/S10, 6 PDFs); S5 hit the SPA shell and logged it dead, so the Australian lineage went untested there. J-STAGE/CiNii clean, new coverage, no twin. SHARE/OSF `_search` plus Copernicus (Marty 2025). Wayback/mdpi-res/AUF `/custom-print/` unblock, not discover.
DEAD: OpenAlex ($0, fifth round running); Google Scholar (429 on the first request, 0 rows); S2 `/paper/search` (6 of 8 lost to 429 - exactly the criterion-phrase ones); RISS/ScienceON/CNKI (JS-only; S12 fell back to WebSearch, nothing); Europe PMC answered everything, zero relevant hits in five sweeps - retire it; proceedings.esri.com retired; the publisher wall (Elsevier TDM, IEEE, CSIRO, Cloudflare hosts). Structural negatives: S6 (no peer-reviewed cross-vendor repeat-corridor consistency study exists), S7 (ASPRS/URISA/ESRI archives empty, outside Crossref).

**2. Verdict.** No read moves A or B; two enumeration edits. Nix et al. 2021/2023 (Philadelphia parks) is a fifth near-shape 3/5 and, after Healy 2022, the second study deriving detectable-change thresholds (6.1/2.1/4.7 pp; 87.4-96.2% agreement) by re-delineating the same plots at every date - a precedent for the F8 arbiter, so it belongs in the B synthesis, not only the T count. Blackman 2019 (131 pp) closes its open question: no C4 analysis the 2020 paper omitted -> Blackman & Yuan confirmed 3/5. Rider: Flanders 2015 alone sets a CI-based detectable-change threshold between epochs and blames a raw shift (6.0->4.4%) on a classifier rule change.

**3. Three remaining.** (1) OpenAlex full text, never run in five rounds; the cron fires once at 17:07, so spend it on `https://api.openalex.org/works?filter=fulltext.search:"spurious change",title_and_abstract.search:canopy&per-page=200`, swapping "minimum detectable change" / "inter-date" / "interpreter agreement" / "not directly comparable", and re-run the six dead S1 queries in that pass. (2) Xing & Stehman 2024 RSE 306:114289 and Stehman 2022 RSE 269:112806 fill the flaw all three designs share; untried: `https://pubs.usgs.gov/publication/70226588` (record matched, document link never fetched) and `experts.esf.edu/esploro` search "Xing interpreter variability" (same host served the Mousoupetros 2017 precursor in full). (3) Data repositories, unswept in every round: `https://zenodo.org/api/records?q="tree canopy" AND ("change detection" OR "time series")&type=dataset&size=100` and `https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/items?q="tree canopy" change aerial&format=json&max=100` - per-epoch accuracy tables live in dataset docs, not papers. Rider: Fensham 2007 via UQ eSpace UQ:162053.

**4. Item 7.** Yes, first time: PACC (17/16/17) and Change Census (15/17/17) pass 3/3; E2-MRF fails (one judge N; edge-free calibration where the years differ). Both passes are nominal: Census's Kill Test 3 is circular as written. PACC passes only because l_t sits outside the gate: `PACC_measured_numbers.txt` already measures the arbiter's own cross-date bias on human-certified stable points at -2.32 pp +/-3.62 (n=1,045) - UNDETERMINED, a CI spanning both zero and the whole effect (-2.21 to -3.48 pp), so the gate cannot fail on cross-date bias. Single experiment: pre-registered, date-blinded, order-randomized re-interpretation of the Neyman-sized stable stratum - N~2,070 for +/-1.5 pp (1.1 h at the measured 1.94 s/label) or 4,657 for +/-1.0 pp (2.5 h) - with same-window replicate deliveries (0.15 pp floor) seeded as true nulls and the fail threshold fixed in advance. It supplies PACC's missing term, de-circularizes Census KT3, and tests E2-MRF's edge claim if anchors include 0-2 m pixels.

**5.** Stronger: 409 queries, 12 modalities, 36 reads, zero >=4/5, two structural negatives - but a hostile reviewer will say no full-text index over paywalled literature ever ran (OpenAlex $0 all five rounds; Scopus/WoS/Dimensions/Lens never touched; Scholar 13 queries in one day; 6 of 8 phrase queries dead) while the founding accuracy papers (Foody, Olofsson, Burnicki, Stehman, Xing) stay UNREADABLE.

# APPENDIX G — Adjudication panel for the two round-5 splits (wf_dcdf2d63-f11, 2026-09-06 ~12:00; six Opus adjudicators, three lenses × two candidates, full text read)

## G.NIX: majority total 3/5; individual totals 3/3/3; per-criterion yes-votes C1 3/3, C2 0/3, C3 3/3, C4 3/3, C5 0/3

### ARCHIVE-AND-EPOCHS (total 3) — pages read: all 26 pages via 2 Read calls (lines 1-506 of r5_s2_nix2021.txt, through the final "===== PAGE 26 of 26 =====" marker), plus targeted greps for radiometr/histogram/calibrat/normaliz/LiDAR/satellite/NAIP/Landsat and for additional year-dates
- C1 = Y (p.6 (PDF marker "===== PAGE 6 of 26 =====")): "The 2018 and 2000 aerial photographs were orthorecti ed to the Pennsylvania State Plane coordinate system." — Table 1 on the same page lists four all-aerial, all sub-metre epochs from two different providers (City of Philadelphia 2018 colour 0.076 m; DVRPC 1959/1980/2000 grayscale 0.61/0.61/0.46 m), and the only preprocessing described anywhere is geometric — orthorectification, cropping and georeferencing (p.6) — with a grep for radiometr/histogram/calibrat/normaliz returning zero hits in the methods, satisfying R3(v).
- C2 = N (p.6 (PDF marker "===== PAGE 6 of 26 =====")): "We assessed change over 59 years in approximately 20-year increments using aerial photography captured in 1959, 1980, 2000, and 2018 (Table 1)." — Only four mapped epochs exist (Table 1 rows and Table 3 rows on p.9 are 1959/1980/2000/2018, and no fifth imagery date appears anywhere in the text, no lidar or satellite year either), so despite the 59-year span R3(iv) makes this an automatic N.
- C3 = Y (p.8 (PDF marker "===== PAGE 8 of 26 =====")): "One interpreter delineated all UTC patches to maintain consistency (Berland 2012)" — This is wall-to-wall manual polygon segmentation of canopy rather than a plot inventory or random-point estimate, confirmed by Table 3 (p.9) reporting tree cover ha, %, patch count and mean patch size for every park in all four years.
- C4 = Y (p.8 (PDF marker "===== PAGE 8 of 26 =====")): "The detectable change thresholds for the entire 59-year period were 6.1, 2.1 and 4.7 percentage points" — These are interpreter-derived numeric minimum changes below which change cannot be distinguished from interpretation noise, and they are applied between dates (p.9: West Fairmount's 17.5 pp is "well above" it, East Fairmount's change "not considered detectable"), which R3(i) states counts as C4.
- C5 = N (p.8 (PDF marker "===== PAGE 8 of 26 =====")): "Percent agreement (Supplementary Information S3) ranged from 87.4–96.2%, depending on the speci c park and year." — The 3-ha second-interpreter plots are a single state-agreement design (interpreter A versus interpreter B on each year's map separately) that R3(i) already assigns to C4 as the source of the detectable-change threshold, and R3(iii) forbids counting the same design twice; no independent C5 evidence remains, because the text never states the same plots were re-observed at every date and never tallies change per unit as R3(ii) requires (S3 is not in the file, and R4 forbids assuming its contents), while the archival records and interviews are qualitative corroboration, not a validation design that measures change.
- Disagreement with reader/skeptics: I flip the reader's C5 from Y to N: the reader spends one design element twice, using the second-interpreter 3-ha plot agreement to fund both the C4 detectable-change threshold and the C5 change-validation, which R3(iii) explicitly forbids; I agree with the VALIDATION skeptic's refutation on this point. The ARCHIVE/EPOCHS and METHOD skeptics both carried C5=Y forward untested because it lay outside their lenses, so their total of 4 rests on an unexamined score, not on evidence. On C3 I side with the METHOD skeptic against the reader: Table 2 rule 5 is only a drafting rule, and the load-bearing proof is p.8 "delineated all UTC patches" plus the complete per-park, per-year metrics in Table 3. Within my own lens I confirm both skeptic and reader on C1 (two providers, all sub-metre aerial, purely geometric preprocessing) and on C2=N (four mapped epochs, no hidden fifth date), so Nix lands at 3/5 and fails the Condition A threshold.

### METHOD-AND-METRIC (total 3) — pages read: all 26 pages via 2 Read calls (lines 1-506; file header says PAGES: 26, not ~20)
- C1 = Y (6): "Images were retrieved from the Pennsylvania Spatial Data Access (PASDA) and the Delaware Valley Regional Planning Commission (DVRPC)" — Table 1 on PAGE 6 lists two distinct acquisition programmes and camera generations - City of Philadelphia 2018 digital colour at 0.076 m versus DVRPC 1959/1980/2000 grayscale film at 0.61/0.61/0.46 m, all sub-metre - and the only stated preprocessing on the same page is purely geometric (2018/2000 orthorectified; 1959/1980 cropped then georeferenced to the 2018 image), with zero hits anywhere in the text for radiometric, histogram, normalisation, calibration, contrast or tone matching.
- C2 = N (6): "We assessed change over 59 years in approximately 20-year increments using aerial photography captured in 1959, 1980, 2000, and 2018 (Table 1)" — Only four mapped epochs exist (Table 1 on PAGE 6 and Table 3 on PAGE 9 both carry exactly the rows 1959/1980/2000/2018), and R3(iv) makes four mapped epochs an automatic N regardless of the 59-year span.
- C3 = Y (8): "One interpreter delineated all UTC patches to maintain consistency (Berland 2012), and a second interpreter delineated a subsection of the study area" — Under my lens the decisive evidence is not this sentence alone but its combination with Table 3 (PAGE 9), which reports tree cover ha, tree cover %, patch count and mean patch size for all three parks in every one of the four years, and the PAGE 7 statement that 'subsequent years were overlaid to determine areas where canopy has been lost, gained, or persisted' - arithmetically impossible without complete wall-to-wall polygon coverage of each park at each date, produced by one stated manual method (Table 2 rules, fixed 100 m2 minimum mapping unit); the PAGE 7 practice of copying obscured canopy edges 'from one image's polygons to the other' is stability inheritance, a comparability caveat, not a coverage or method-change gap.
- C4 = Y (8): "The detectable change thresholds for the entire 59-year period were 6.1, 2.1 and 4.7 percentage points" — This is an interpreter-derived detectable-change threshold expressed as a number and applied between dates (PAGE 9: West Fairmount's 17.5 pp gain is 'well above the detectable change threshold' while East Fairmount's change 'was not considered detectable'), which R3(i) makes count as C4; the 87.4-96.2% per-park-per-year agreement figures would not have qualified on my lens as they are per-epoch accuracy, so the Y rests solely on the numeric threshold.
- C5 = N (8): "The second interpreter re-delineated canopy cover for roughly 10% of the total area of each park within randomly placed 3 ha circular plots" — Two independent legs. First, R3(iii): this second-interpreter plot design is the whole of Section 2.3 and is the arithmetic input to the detectable-change threshold I have just credited for C4, so it cannot be spent twice, and R3(i) says the threshold fits C4. Second, even setting that aside it does not meet R3(ii): it tallies inter-interpreter agreement per date rather than change per unit across dates, the plots are described as 'randomly placed' with no statement that the same plots were revisited at every epoch, and R4 forbids inferring that. Nothing else in the paper validates the change estimate - no field plots, no lidar, no McNemar or paired-change test, no reference change series (greps for field/lidar/reference/validat return only citations and the archival-newspaper search); the archival records and three interviews are qualitative corroboration of drivers. Screening a reported change against a noise threshold is not a validation design that can see change.
- Disagreement with reader/skeptics: I agree with the VALIDATION skeptic's revised total of 3 and disagree with the reader and with both the ARCHIVE/EPOCHS and METHOD/METRIC skeptics on C5 alone. All three of them spend the single Section 2.3 second-interpreter design twice - once as the detectable-change threshold for C4 and again as a change-capable validation for C5 - which R3(iii) forbids; the reader's premise that the same 3-ha reference plots were re-delineated at each date is nowhere stated in the text (PAGE 8 says only 'randomly placed 3 ha circular plots'), so under R4 it cannot be assumed. On C3 I reach the same Y as the reader but reject the reader's quote (Table 2 rule 5 is a drafting instruction about clipping, not evidence of coverage) and rest the score on 'delineated all UTC patches' plus the complete per-park-per-year metrics in Table 3 and the year-on-year overlay procedure. C1, C2 and C4 I score exactly as all four prior records do.

### VALIDATION lens (hardest on C5) (total 3) — pages read: all 26 pages via 2 Read calls (lines 1-400 and 400-506; last marker "===== PAGE 26 of 26 =====" reached), plus one confirmatory Grep for radiometric/validation terms
- C1 = Y (6): "Images were retrieved from the Pennsylvania Spatial Data Access (PASDA) and the Delaware Valley Regional Planning Commission (DVRPC)" — Table 1 on the same page lists two distinct providers and camera generations, all sub-metre (City of Philadelphia 2018 digital colour 0.076 m; DVRPC grayscale film 1959/1980 0.61 m and 2000 0.46 m), and the only preprocessing described is geometric (orthorectification/georeferencing) — a Grep for radiometr/calibrat/histogram/normali/contrast/tone returns no cross-date radiometric step anywhere in the text.
- C2 = N (6): "aerial photography captured in 1959, 1980, 2000, and 2018 (Table 1)" — Only four mapped epochs exist (confirmed by Table 3 on page 9, whose rows are 1959/1980/2000/2018, and by Fig. 2's four-year size-class panels); under binding rule (iv) four epochs is N regardless of the 59-year span.
- C3 = Y (8): "One interpreter delineated all UTC patches to maintain consistency" — This is wall-to-wall manual polygon segmentation of canopy, not a plot inventory or point sample, and Table 3 (page 9) reports tree-cover area, percent, patch count and mean patch size for every park in all four years, which requires complete polygon coverage at each epoch.
- C4 = Y (8): "The detectable change thresholds for the entire 59-year period were 6.1, 2.1 and 4.7 percentage points" — A stated numeric minimum change below which the method cannot distinguish change from interpretation noise, derived from the 87.4-96.2% second-interpreter agreement and actually applied between dates (page 9: West Fairmount's 17.5 pp is 'well above', East Fairmount's change 'not considered detectable') — exactly the construction rule (i) says counts as C4.
- C5 = N (8): "The second interpreter re-delineated canopy cover for roughly 10% of the total area of each park within randomly placed 3 ha circular plots" — Three independent grounds: (a) this is the very design that produces the detectable-change thresholds scored for C4, and rule (iii) forbids spending one design twice — it fits C4 best; (b) even standing alone it tallies inter-interpreter agreement per park per date, never change per unit, so it fails rule (ii)'s 'tallying change per unit' requirement; (c) the text never states the same plots were re-used at every date (S3 is not in the file) and R4 forbids assuming it. Section 2.3 is the complete error assessment (shadows, parallax, misregistration, per-date percent agreement, threshold); there is no change-stratified sample, no paired change comparison or McNemar, and no independent change reference — the archival records and interviews are qualitative driver attribution in which the change maps were shown to staff as prompts (page 9), not a reference change series.
- Disagreement with reader/skeptics: I agree with the VALIDATION skeptic and split from the reader and the ARCHIVE and METHOD skeptics, who all carried C5=Y: they score the same second-interpreter 3-ha-plot design for both C4 and C5, which binding rule (iii) prohibits, and the threshold construction is precisely what rule (i) reserves for C4. The reader's own VALIDATION_DESIGN paragraph in fact describes the threshold machinery (per-date percent agreement feeding a noise screen), not a design that observes change — screening a change estimate against a noise floor is not validating it against a reference change. I also note the reader's claim that the same plots were re-delineated at each date is not stated anywhere in the text, so under R4 it cannot support a Y. On C1-C4 I concur with all three skeptics, though for C3 I rest on page 8's 'delineated all UTC patches' plus Table 3's complete per-year metrics rather than the reader's Table 2 drafting rule.
## G.BLACKMAN: majority total 3/5; individual totals 3/3/3; per-criterion yes-votes C1 3/3, C2 3/3, C3 3/3, C4 0/3, C5 0/3

### ARCHIVE-AND-EPOCHS (total 3) — pages read: all 131 pages (whole txt file, lines 1-2502) via 7 consecutive Read calls, plus two targeted greps (threshold/consistency terms; radiometric/calibration terms)
- C1 = Y (58 (marker; thesis p.48)): "Due to the differences between the three types of aerial photographic images, different histogram stretches, learning options, and input representations were utilized" — Table 4 (PAGE 48) lists three independent aerial suppliers/generations across the mapped epochs - MHAPO panchromatic film (1938/1951/1964), City of St Peter Engineers Office hardcopy scanned at 600 DPI (1995), and USGS NAIP 4-band digital (2008/2017) - all at 3.06 ft (0.93 m, sub-meter), with per-image (not cross-date) histogram stretches and per-image training and no radiometric normalisation or histogram matching between dates anywhere in the text (grep for radiometric/calibrat/normali/histogram returns only per-image statements); the 1928 engineering map, the 2010 lidar and the Google/FSA i-Tree imagery are not mapped epochs, so nothing non-aerial or coarser than 0.93 m enters the six-map series.
- C2 = Y (47 (marker; thesis p.37)): "multi-temporal photographic images were selected for 1938, 1951, 1964, 1995, 2008, and 2017 (Table 4)" — Six epochs each carry a wall-to-wall OBIA canopy map (Figures 8-13, PAGES 75-80; per-year accuracy in Table 13, PAGE 69; every pair summing ~2325 acres in Table 23, PAGE 85) spanning 1938-2017 = 79 years, comfortably clearing the five-epoch/ten-year bar even after I refuse to count 1928 (engineering map only, boundary source), 2010 (lidar) and 2019 (i-Tree points only) as mapped epochs.
- C3 = Y (58 (marker; thesis p.48)): "the results were repeatedly refined using the Removing Clutter tool (Yuan 2008; Overwatch Systems Ltd 2013) until the final class polygon shapefile was created" — Feature Analyst OBIA segmentation produces a full class-polygon shapefile per date over imagery clipped to the whole 1928/2017 city boundary (PAGE 53: analysis "took place within the City of St Peter boundary of 1928 and 2017"), i.e. canopy mapping, not a plot inventory.
- C4 = N (74 (marker; thesis p.64)): "It is likely, based on the very small percentages of cover change, that the change is within error margins associated with accuracy assessment" — Inter-date noise is only acknowledged qualitatively - no flicker, spurious-change or repeat-interpretation agreement statistic exists, and no numeric detectable-change threshold is ever stated (grep for threshold/detectable/error margin/noise/consistency finds nothing else); the only numbers are per-date overall accuracy/kappa (Table 13) computed against the very image being classified, and the i-Tree +/-1.45% SE, which is a binomial confidence interval on a single-date state estimate rather than a stated minimum change distinguishable from noise, so R3(i) is not triggered.
- C5 = N (59 (marker; thesis p.49)): "the reference images used within ERDAS IMAGINE were the same aerial photographic images used to extract the classifications" — A change/trend estimate is produced (Table 23 post-classification change matrices), but its validation cannot see change: per-date error matrices with samples redrawn each year against a circular reference (the classified image itself), plus field truth at 2017 only over ~1/4 of the area (PAGES 60, 72); R3(ii) does not rescue the i-Tree design because the same 1000 KML points are re-observed at only 2 of the 7 dates and no change is tallied per unit - only two aggregate proportions, whose implied 2008-2019 change (30.3% to 36.8%, non-overlapping CIs) contradicts the OBIA 2008-2017 change (-0.23 pp) in sign and magnitude yet is read as canopy "remains stable" (PAGE 83), so it functions as a level cross-check, never as a change validation.
- Disagreement with reader/skeptics: I agree with the reader and all three skeptics on C1-C4 (my archive/epochs lens confirms three distinct aerial suppliers, uniform 0.93 m sub-meter resolution, no cross-date radiometric step, and six genuine mapped epochs over 79 years; C4 fails for want of any stated number). I disagree with the reader and with the archive and method skeptics on C5, and side with the validation skeptic: the i-Tree 1000-point re-interpretation meets neither prong of R3(ii) - same units at only 2 of 7 dates, and aggregate proportions rather than a per-unit change tally - so the interpretive rule does not apply and the plain criterion must be met on its own terms. It is not met: the only designs left are per-date accuracy against the same image used for classification and 2017-only ground truth, both state-level and change-blind. Decisively, the i-Tree series and the OBIA series disagree on the sign of 2008-2019 change and the thesis never reconciles them, which is the opposite of a validation design that can see change.

### METHOD-AND-METRIC (total 3) — pages read: all 131 pages, via 7 consecutive Read calls covering txt lines 1-2502 (pages 109-131 are the reference list); pages 47-53, 58-59, 62-63, 68-75, 84-85, 98 re-read for imagery sources, epochs, mapping method, accuracy assessment and change assessment
- C1 = Y (58): "Due to the differences between the three types of aerial photographic images, different histogram stretches, learning options, and input representations were utilized" — Table 4 (p.48) lists four independent suppliers/generations - City of St Peter Engineers Office (1995 hardcopy scanned), MHAPO panchromatic film (1938/1951/1964), USGS NAIP color-IR (2008/2017), USDA FSA for i-Tree - all at 3.06 ft (0.93 m, sub-meter), and the only radiometric handling described is per-image-type stretching for classification, never a cross-date normalisation or histogram-matching step.
- C2 = Y (47): "multi-temporal photographic images were selected for 1938, 1951, 1964, 1995, 2008, and 2017" — Six mapped aerial epochs spanning 79 years clears the five-epoch, ten-year minimum; the 1928 item is an engineering map used only for the city boundary and 2019 is i-Tree points, so neither is needed to reach five.
- C3 = Y (53): "the aerial photographic images were clipped to the 1928 and 2017 City of St Peter polygon feature classes" — Every one of the six epochs is mosaicked, clipped to the same full city boundary and classified wall-to-wall by one stated method (Feature Analyst OBIA, supervised multi-class, clutter removal, 3x3 majority filter, pp.56-58), with per-epoch canopy maps at Figures 8-13 (pp.75-80) and Table 23 (p.85) summing every pair to ~2325.4 acres - impossible without a complete raster at all six dates.
- C4 = N (68): "The overall Kappa statistics show strong agreement (>0.8) (Congalton and Green 2019) between the years, with the exception of 1995 (0.78)." — This 'between the years' phrasing is the study's most inter-date-sounding sentence but it is only Table 13's column of per-year overall accuracy/kappa, each computed against the very image it classified (p.59: 'the reference images used within ERDAS IMAGINE were the same aerial photographic images used to extract the classifications'), i.e. a per-epoch accuracy percentage, which my lens explicitly excludes; the thesis states no detectable-change threshold, no same-date disagreement, no repeat-classification variance and no interpreter-repeatability figure, and where it comes closest (p.74, change 'is within error margins associated with accuracy assessment') it gives no number, while the LiDAR claim that 'the 2010 LiDAR data corroborates the 2008 OBIA data' (p.98) is unnumbered, single-date and cross-method.
- C5 = N (59): "the reference images used within ERDAS IMAGINE were the same aerial photographic images used to extract the classifications" — A change/trend estimate certainly exists (Table 23, p.85), but nothing validates it against observed change: the per-date error matrices are circular and drawn fresh at each date, field truth exists at 2017 only over about a quarter of the area (p.72), and the i-Tree design - 1000 points made on 2019 imagery then re-interpreted on 2008 via KML (p.62) - fails rule (ii) on both limbs, since it covers only two dates rather than every date (2019 has no OBIA map at all) and reports only two marginal proportions with binomial SEs (Table 22, p.72), never a per-unit change tally or paired comparison; it is a second estimator that disagrees with OBIA at 2008 (30.3% vs 35.53%) and is left unreconciled, not a validation that can see change.
- Disagreement with reader/skeptics: I split from the reader and the archive/epochs and method/metric skeptics on C5: they credit the i-Tree KML design, but rule (ii) requires the same units re-observed at EVERY date AND a change tally per unit, and Blackman re-observes 1000 points at only two dates - one of which (2019) has no canopy map - and reports only per-date proportions and standard errors, never a per-point change comparison, so the six-epoch OBIA change product goes wholly unvalidated. I therefore land with the validation skeptic at 3/5, below the 4/5 Condition A bar. On C4 I agree with all four inputs that it fails, but I would put the weight differently: the p.68 'between the years' kappa sentence, not the LiDAR corroboration, is the real trap, and it collapses to Table 13's per-year self-referential accuracies. On C1-C3 I concur, though I note C1 rests on 3.06 ft (0.93 m) imagery, which is sub-meter only marginally.

### VALIDATION (total 3) — pages read: all 131 pages (txt lines 1-2502, through the final "===== PAGE 131 of 131 =====" marker) via 7 consecutive Read calls at offsets 1, 400, 800, 1199, 1598, 1997, 2396
- C1 = Y (PAGE 58 of 131 (thesis p.48)): "Due to the differences between the three types of aerial photographic images, different histogram stretches, learning options, and input representations were utilized" — Table 4 (PAGE 48 marker) lists three separate suppliers/generations of sub-meter aerial imagery (MHAPO panchromatic film 1938/1951/1964, City of St Peter scanned hardcopy 1995, USGS NAIP color-IR 2008/2017, all 3.06 ft = 0.93 m), and the only radiometric handling is a per-image-type stretch chosen for classification, with no cross-date normalisation or histogram matching described anywhere.
- C2 = Y (PAGE 47 of 131 (thesis p.37)): "multi-temporal photographic images were selected for 1938, 1951, 1964, 1995, 2008, and 2017 (Table 4)." — Six epochs each carry an OBIA canopy map (Figures 8-13, PAGE 75-80 markers) and span 79 years, clearing the five-epoch/ten-year bar; 1928 is only a georeferenced engineering map supplying a boundary, and 2019 has i-Tree points only, so neither is needed.
- C3 = Y (PAGE 53 of 131 (thesis p.43)): "The analysis to determine the urban forest canopy cover took place within the City of St Peter boundary of 1928 and 2017." — This is wall-to-wall object-based segmentation of tree/shrub canopy into class polygons for each date (Feature Analyst, PAGE 58 marker: "the final class polygon shapefile was created"), with Table 23 summing every date-pair to ~2325 acres of full-extent raster, not a plot inventory.
- C4 = N (PAGE 74 of 131 (thesis p.64)): "It is likely, based on the very small percentages of cover change, that the change is within error margins associated with accuracy assessment" — Temporal inconsistency is only acknowledged, never quantified: this sentence attaches no number, and the only figures produced are per-date overall accuracy/Kappa (Table 13) computed against the very image that was classified, plus i-Tree's per-date binomial SE (+/-1.45%), neither of which is stated or applied as a minimum detectable change, so no interpreter- or method-derived detectable-change threshold exists under R3(i).
- C5 = N (PAGE 59 of 131 (thesis p.49)): "the reference images used within ERDAS IMAGINE were the same aerial photographic images used to extract the classifications." — The change estimate (Table 23 post-classification change matrix, 1938-2017) is validated only by state-agreement designs that cannot see change: a freshly drawn 150-point stratified sample per date whose reference is circular (the classified image itself), one-date-only field truthing over about a quarter of the city (PAGE 60 marker), and unnumbered LiDAR "corroboration" of 2008 alone; the i-Tree KML re-import does re-observe the same 1000 points, but at only two dates (2008 and 2019, one of which is not an OBIA epoch) and it reports only aggregate percentages and SEs with no per-unit change tally, change matrix or paired test, so R3(ii) is not satisfied and it functions as a parallel estimate rather than a change reference.
- Disagreement with reader/skeptics: I disagree with the first reader on C5 and therefore on the total (3, not 4). The reader treats i-Tree's 1000 KML points as a re-observed-unit change validation, but the thesis re-interprets them at only two dates (2008 Google Earth, 2019 Google Maps), neither pair matching the OBIA change intervals, and reports only aggregate canopy percentages with binomial SEs (Table 22) — no per-point change tally, no paired comparison, so R3(ii)'s "same sample units at every date and tally change per unit" is not met and it is a second estimator rather than a reference for change. I agree with the VALIDATION skeptic's N on C5 and with all three inputs that C4 fails for want of any number, and I agree on C1-C3 while resting them on different passages (the per-image-type histogram stretch on PAGE 58 for C1, the six-epoch selection sentence on PAGE 47 for C2, the study-extent sentence on PAGE 53 plus the per-epoch full-boundary figures for C3).
